\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|x-y|^{-2}\,\mathbf{1}_{N^{-1}\lesssim|x-y|\lesssim 1}\,\mathbf{1}_{|y|\gtrsim|x|}dy\lesssim N^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|y|\sim|x|}dy\lesssim N^{2}|x|^{2}

Thus, by (
3.25
) and (
3.26
), we deduce that

(3.27)

\displaystyle|\partial^{\alpha_{2}}_{x}\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{2}^{\alpha_{1}}(x)|\lesssim N^{4-A}|x|^{2-A}\lesssim\big(|x|+N^{-1}\big)^{-2}\mathbf{1}_{N^{-1}\lesssim|x|\lesssim 1}+\langle x\rangle^{2-A}\mathbf{1}_{|x|\gg 1}

in that case, since
|x|\gg N^{-1}
. If
|y|\gg|x|
for
y
in the support of the integrand of
\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{2}(x)
then
|x-y|\sim|y|\gg|x|
and hence, by (
3.25
), we have

(3.28)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\partial^{\alpha_{2}}_{x}\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{2}^{\alpha_{1}}(x)|&\lesssim N^{2-A}|x|^{-A}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|y|^{-2}\,\mathbf{1}_{|x|\lesssim|y|\lesssim 1}dy\\
&\lesssim N^{2-A}|x|^{-A}\big(\langle\,\log x\rangle\,\mathbf{1}_{N^{-1}\lesssim|x|\lesssim 1}+\mathbf{1}_{|x|\gtrsim 1}\big)\\
&\lesssim\big(|x|+N^{-1}\big)^{-2}\bigl\langle\,\log\big(|x|+N^{-1}\big)\bigr\rangle\,\mathbf{1}_{N^{-1}\lesssim|x|\lesssim 1}+\langle x\rangle^{-A}\mathbf{1}_{|x|\gg 1}\end{split}

in that case, where we used
|x|\gg N^{-1}
. The case
|y|\ll|x|
for
y
in the support of the integrand of
\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{2}(x)
is treated via similar arguments and we have

(3.29)

\displaystyle|\partial^{\alpha_{2}}_{x}\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{2}^{\alpha_{1}}(x)|\lesssim\big(|x|+N^{-1}\big)^{-2}\mathbf{1}_{N^{-1}\lesssim|x|\lesssim 1}+\langle x\rangle^{-A}\mathbf{1}_{|x|\gg 1}

in that case as well.

Therefore, combining (
3.27
), (
3.28
), (
3.29
) gives

(3.30)

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt^{\alpha_{1}}_{2}(x)|\lesssim\begin{cases}\big(|x|+N^{-1}\big)^{-2}\bigl\langle\,\log\big(|x|+N^{-1}\big)\bigr\rangle\quad&\text{for $|x|<2$},\\
\langle x\rangle^{-A}\quad&\text{for $|x|\geq 2$}.\\
\end{cases}

Lastly, from (
2.15
) and the definition of the smooth function
I I
, it is easy to see that

(3.31)

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}(x)|\lesssim\langle x\rangle^{-A}.

Thus, (
3.18
) follows from (
3.22
), (
3.30
) and (
3.31
). This concludes the proof of (
3.9
) with
|\alpha|=2
.

The bound (
3.11
) in (ii) is a consequence of arguments similar to those in the proof of (
3.9
) and we omit details.

∎

3.2.
Hyperbolic singularities

We now consider functions which are singular along circles. Recall that for
t>0
,
\mathbb{S}^{1}(t)
denotes the circle of centre
0
and radius
t
.

In the first result of this subsection, we prove estimates on smoothed hyperbolic singularities. This is essentially the hyperbolic counterpart of Lemma
3.1
.

Lemma 3.3
.

Fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
0<\gamma\leq\frac{1}{2}
. Let
\mathcal{M}=\mathbb{T}^{2}
or
\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
\nu_{N}:\mathcal{M}\to\mathbb{R}
be a function satisfying the bound

(3.32)

\displaystyle|\nu_{N}(x)|\lesssim_{A}N^{2}\langle Nx\rangle^{-A}

for all
x\in\mathcal{M}
and any finite
A\geq 1
. Let
0<t\leq 1
,
H_{t}
and
\widetilde{H}_{t}
be the functions given by

\displaystyle H_{t,\gamma}(x)

\displaystyle=|t-|x||^{-\gamma},

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}_{t,\gamma}(x)

\displaystyle=\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|^{-\gamma}

for any
x\in\mathcal{M}\setminus\mathbb{S}^{1}(t)
. Set
H_{N,t,\gamma}:=H_{t,\gamma}*\nu_{N}
and
\widetilde{H}_{N,t,\gamma}:=\widetilde{H}_{t,\gamma}*\nu_{N}
. Then, the following bounds hold:

(3.33)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|H_{N,t,\gamma}(x)|&\lesssim\min\!\big\{N^{\gamma},|t-|x||^{-\gamma}\big\}\,\bigl\langle\,\log\!\big(\!\min\!\big\{N,|t-|x||^{-1}\big\}\big)\bigr\rangle,\\
|\widetilde{H}_{N,t,\gamma}(x)|&\lesssim\min\!\big\{N^{2\gamma},\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|^{-\gamma}\big\}\,\bigl\langle\,\log\big(\!\min\!\big\{N,|t-|x||^{-1}\big\}\big)\bigr\rangle\end{split}

for any
x\in\mathcal{M}\cap B(0,10)
. Here, the implicit constants are independent of
N
.
10
10

10

Here, we chose the ball
B(0,10)
because it contains a copy of the torus
\mathbb{T}^{2}\cong[-\pi,\pi)^{2}
and is bounded, but this choice is otherwise arbitrary.

The proof of Lemma
3.3
is significantly more challenging than that of Lemma
3.1
. This is due to the fact the set of singular points of the functions
H_{t}
(and
\widetilde{H}_{t}
) is not the singleton
\{0\}
, as in the case of functions of the form
x\in\mathcal{M}\mapsto|x|^{-\theta}
,
0<\theta<2
in Lemma
3.2
, but consists of points lying on the circle
\mathbb{S}^{1}(t)
.

Before proceeding with the proof of Lemma
3.3
, we introduce a convenient spatial localization procedure. By (
3.32
), we have
11
11

11

Note that the sum over
k\geq 1
in is finite for
\mathcal{M}=\mathbb{T}^{2}
. Namely, we have
k\lesssim\langle\,\log N\rangle
since
|y|_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\leq 2\sqrt{2}\pi
for any
y\in\mathbb{T}^{2}
.

(3.34)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|H_{N,t,\gamma}(x)|&=\int_{\mathcal{M}}\mathbf{1}_{0\leq|y|_{\mathcal{M}}<N^{-1}}\,|\nu_{N}(y)|H_{t}(x-y)dy\\
&\qquad+\sum_{k\geq 1}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{2^{k-1}N^{-1}\leq|y|_{\mathcal{M}}<2^{k}N^{-1}}\,|\nu_{N}(y)|H_{t}(x-y)dy\\
&\lesssim\sum_{k\geq 0}2^{-200k}\,H^{k}_{N,t,\gamma}(x),\end{split}

where

(3.35)

\displaystyle H^{k}_{N,t,\gamma}(x):=N^{2}\int_{\mathcal{M}}\mathbf{1}_{|y|_{\mathcal{M}}<2^{k}N^{-1}}\,H_{t}(x-y)dy

for all
x\in\mathcal{M}
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. Lastly, set

(3.36)

\displaystyle\widetilde{H}_{N,t,\gamma}(x):=N^{2}\int_{\mathcal{M}}\mathbf{1}_{|y|_{\mathcal{M}}<2^{k}N^{-1}}\,\widetilde{H}_{t}(x-y)dy

for all
x\in\mathcal{M}
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

Lemma
3.3
is an immediate consequence of the following result.

Lemma 3.4
.

Fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
0\leq\gamma\leq\frac{1}{2}
. Let
0<t\leq 1
,
H_{N,t,\gamma}^{k}
and
\widetilde{H}^{k}_{N,t,\gamma}
be as in (
3.35
) and (
3.51
), respecively. Then, the following estimates holds

(3.37)

\displaystyle|H^{k}_{N,t,\gamma}(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{100k}\min\!\big\{N^{\gamma},|t-|x||^{-\gamma}\big\}\,\bigl\langle\,\log\!\big(\!\min\!\big\{N,|t-|x||^{-1}\big\}\big)\bigr\rangle,

(3.38)

\displaystyle|\widetilde{H}^{k}_{N,t,\gamma}(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{100k}\min\!\big\{N^{2\gamma},\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|^{-\gamma}\big\}\,\bigl\langle\,\log\!\big(\!\min\!\big\{N,|t-|x||^{-1}\big\}\big)\bigr\rangle

for any
x\in\mathcal{M}\cap B(0,10)
. Here, the implicit constants are independent of
N
.

Proof.

We first consider (
3.37
) and (
3.38
) in the case
\mathcal{M}=\mathbb{T}^{2}
and prove (
3.37
). Fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
0<t\leq 1
.

\bullet
Step
I
: preliminary reductions and estimates.
Fix
x\in\mathbb{T}^{2}
and let
y\in\mathbb{T}^{2}
be in the support of the integrand of
H^{k}_{N,t,\gamma}
. If
|x-y|\gg t
or
|x-y|\ll t
, it is easy to see that (
3.37
) holds. For instance, let us assume that
|x-y|\ll t
. Then, we have

(3.39)

\displaystyle|H^{k}_{N,t,\gamma}(x)|\lesssim N^{2}t^{-\gamma}\min(N^{-2},t^{2})\lesssim\min(N^{\gamma},t^{-\gamma}).

If
t\gtrsim|x|
, then (
3.39
) clearly implies (
3.37
). Otherwise,
t\ll|x|
and this implies that
|x|\lesssim|y|\lesssim 2^{k}N^{-1}
in view of the condition
|x-y|\ll t
. Thus, the bound

N^{\gamma}\lesssim 2^{\gamma k}|x|^{-\gamma}\lesssim 2^{\gamma k}|t-|x||^{-\gamma}

holds and (
3.39
) implies (
3.37
). The case
|x-y|\gg t
follows from Lemma
3.1
and similar arguments. Therefore, we henceforth assume the extra condition
|x-y|\sim t
in the integrand of
H^{k}_{N,t,\gamma}
in what follows.

Now, we assume that the condition
\big|t^{2}-|x-y|^{2}\big|<N^{-10}
holds in the integrand of
H^{k}_{N,t,\gamma}
, which implies that

|t-|x-y||\lesssim t^{-1}N^{-10},

under the condition
t\sim|x-y|
. Hence, by a polar change of coordinate, we have

\displaystyle|H^{k}_{N,t,\gamma}(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{2}\int_{0}^{10t}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{0<|t-r|\lesssim t^{-1}N^{-10}}}{|t-r|^{\gamma}}rdr\lesssim t^{\gamma}N^{-2},

which is a stronger estimate than (
3.37
).

Therefore, we may assume that the conditions

(3.40)

\displaystyle\begin{split}t\sim|x-y|\qquad\text{and}\qquad\big|t^{2}-|x-y|^{2}\big|\geq N^{-10},\end{split}

hold in the integrand of
H^{k}_{N,t,\gamma}
for the rest of the proof. To sum up, we have

(3.41)

\displaystyle|H^{k}_{N,t,\gamma}(x)|\sim N^{2}t^{\gamma}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|x-y|\sim t}\,\mathbf{1}_{|t^{2}-|x-y|^{2}|\geq N^{-10}|}\,\mathbf{1}_{|y|<2^{k}N^{-1}}\big|t^{2}-|x-y|^{2}\big|^{-\gamma}dy.

By Hölder’s inequality and a polar change of coordinates, we obtain the following basic estimate on
H^{k}_{N,t,\gamma}
:

(3.42)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|H^{k}_{N,t,\gamma}(x)|&\lesssim N^{2}t^{\gamma}|B(0,2^{k}N^{-1})|^{1-\gamma}\Big(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{|t^{2}-|x-y|^{2}|\geq N^{-10}}}{\big|t^{2}-|x-y|^{2}\big|}\mathbf{1}_{|x-y|\sim t}dy\Big)^{\gamma}\\
&\lesssim 2^{2k}t^{\gamma}N^{2\gamma}\langle\,\log N\rangle^{\gamma}.\end{split}

The bound (
3.42
) will be useful in several instances later in the proof.

\bullet
Step
I I
: analysis close to the radial singularity.
Let
y\in\mathbb{T}^{2}
be in the support of the integrand of
H^{k}_{N,t,\gamma}
.
12
12

12

If
\operatorname{Supp}(H^{k}_{N,t,\gamma})=\emptyset
, there is nothing to show. We will discard such cases without further mention in this proof.
We expand the expression
t^{2}-|x-y|^{2}
depending on the sign of
t^{2}-|x|^{2}
as follows:

(3.43)

\displaystyle t^{2}-|x-y|^{2}=\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|\bigg(\text{sgn}(t^{2}-|x^{2}|)+\frac{Q_{x}(y)}{\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|}\bigg),

where

(3.44)

\displaystyle Q_{x}(y)=2\langle x,y\rangle-|y|^{2}

for all
y\in\mathbb{T}^{2}
and
sgn
is the sign function in (
2.28
). We now divide our analysis into several cases.

\bullet
Case 1:
|Q_{x}(y)|\ll|t^{2}-|x|^{2}|
.
In this case, we have that
\big|t^{2}-|x-y|^{2}\big|\sim\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|
by (
3.43
). Hence, by (
3.40
), this leads to the bound

\displaystyle|H^{k}_{N,t,\gamma}(x)|\lesssim 2^{2k}|t-|x||^{-\gamma},

which is acceptable in view of (
3.37
) if
N\gtrsim|t-|x||^{-1}
. Otherwise,
N\ll|t-|x||^{-1}
and we proceed as follows.

If
t\gg|x|
then we have
t\sim|y|
as
t\sim|x-y|
by (
3.40
) and hence
t\lesssim 2^{k}N^{-1}
. This directly gives (
3.37
) from the bound (
3.42
). Similarly, if
t\lesssim N^{-1}
, we get (
3.37
) from the bound (
3.42
). We thus assume that
N^{-1}\lesssim t\lesssim|x|
in the rest of the proof of this case.

By (
3.43
), we have that

\displaystyle\big|t^{2}-|x-y|^{2}\big|\sim\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|\gg|Q_{x}(y)|.

Hence, by (
3.41
) and polar changes of variables, we have

(3.45)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|H^{k}_{N,t,\gamma}(x)|&\lesssim N^{2}t^{\gamma}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|y|<2^{k}N^{-1}}\,|Q_{x}(y)|^{-\gamma}\mathbf{1}_{|Q_{x}(y)|\geq N^{-100}}dy\\
&\quad+N^{2}t^{\gamma}\big|t^{2}-|x^{2}|\big|^{-\gamma}\cdot N^{-25}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|y|<2^{k}N^{-1}}\,|Q_{x}(y)|^{-\frac{1}{4}}\mathbf{1}_{|Q_{x}(y)|<N^{-100}}dy\\
&\lesssim N^{2}t^{\gamma}\int_{0}^{2^{k}N^{-1}}rdr\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{d\theta}{|2\cos(\theta)|x|r-r^{2}|^{\gamma}}\mathbf{1}_{|2\cos(\theta)|x|r-r^{2}|\geq N^{-100}}\\
&\quad+N^{-23+10\gamma}t^{\gamma}\int_{0}^{2^{k}N^{-1}}rdr\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{d\theta}{|2\cos(\theta)|x|r-r^{2}|^{\frac{1}{4}}}\\
&\lesssim N^{2}t^{\gamma}\int_{0}^{2^{k}N^{-1}}r^{1-\gamma}dr\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{d\theta}{|2\cos(\theta)|x|-r|^{\gamma}}\mathbf{1}_{|2\cos(\theta)|x|-r|\geq N^{-100}}\\
&\quad+N^{-15}t^{\gamma}\int_{0}^{2^{k}N^{-1}}r^{\frac{3}{4}}dr\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{d\theta}{|2\cos(\theta)|x|-r|^{\frac{1}{4}}}.\end{split}

Note that if
\theta\in[\frac{\pi}{2},\frac{3\pi}{2}]
then
\theta-\pi\in[-\frac{\pi}{2},\frac{\pi}{2}]
and
\cos(\theta-\pi)>0
. Therefore, we have

|2\cos(\theta)|x|-r|^{-\gamma}=(2\cos(\theta-\pi)|x|+r)^{-\gamma}

for any
r>0
. The last fact together with the symmetry
\cos(-\theta)=\cos(\theta)
for any
\theta\in\mathbb{R}
, shows that the contribution of the angular integral over
\theta\in[0,2\pi]
can be bounded by that over the range
\theta\in[0,\frac{\pi}{2}]
. Thus, from a change of variables and recalling
0<\gamma\leq\frac{1}{2}
, we then get

(3.46)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{d\theta}{|2\cos(\theta)|x|-r|^{\gamma}}\mathbf{1}_{|2\cos(\theta)|x|-r|\geq N^{-100}}\\
&\qquad\quad\lesssim\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\frac{d\theta}{|2\cos(\theta)|x|-r|^{\gamma}}\mathbf{1}_{|2\cos(\theta)|x|-r|\geq N^{-100}}\\
&\qquad\quad\lesssim|x|^{-\gamma}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{du}{\sqrt{1-u^{2}}\big|2u-r|x|^{-1}\big|^{\frac{1}{2}}}\mathbf{1}_{|2u-r|x|^{-1}|\geq N^{-100}}\\
&\qquad\quad\lesssim|x|^{-\gamma}\langle\,\log N\rangle,\end{split}

uniformly in
r>0
. Similarly, we have

(3.47)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}}\frac{d\theta}{|\cos(\theta)|x|-r|^{\frac{1}{4}}}\lesssim|x|^{-\frac{1}{4}},

uniformly in
r>0
. Thus, combining (
3.45
), (
3.46
) and (
3.47
) together with the condition
N^{-1}\lesssim t\lesssim|x|
gives

\displaystyle|H^{k}_{N,t,\gamma}(x)|\lesssim 2^{2k}N^{\gamma}\langle\,\log N\rangle+2^{2k}N^{-10}\lesssim 2^{2k}N^{\gamma}\langle\,\log N\rangle,

as required in (
3.37
).

\bullet
Case 2:
|Q_{x}(y)|\gtrsim\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|
.
By the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the fact that
|y|\lesssim 2^{k}N^{-1}
in the support of the integrand of
H^{k}_{N,t,\gamma}
, we deduce that

(3.48)

\displaystyle\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|\lesssim\max\big(2^{k}N^{-1}|x|,2^{2k}N^{-2}\big).

If
2^{2k}N^{-2}\gg 2^{k}N^{-1}|x|
then we have
|x|\ll 2^{k}N^{-1}
. Hence, we have
t\sim|x-y|\lesssim 2^{k}N^{-1}
as
|y|\leq 2^{k}N^{-1}
. By (
3.42
), (
3.48
) and the simple estimate
|t+|x||\gtrsim|t-|x||
, this shows that

\displaystyle|H^{k}_{N,t,\gamma}(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{2k}N^{\gamma}\langle\,\log N\rangle^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{4k}\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\,\bigl\langle\,\log\!\big(\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|\big)\bigr\rangle^{\gamma}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{4k}|t-|x||^{\gamma}\,\bigl\langle\,\log\!\big(|t-|x||\big)\bigr\rangle^{\gamma},

which is acceptable in view of (
3.37
). Otherwise, we have
2^{2k}N^{-2}\lesssim 2^{k}N^{-1}|x|
and the condition (
3.48
) reduces to

(3.49)

\displaystyle N\lesssim 2^{k}|t-|x||^{-1}.

Note that by (
3.49
), it suffices to show the bound

(3.50)

\displaystyle|H^{k}_{N,t,\gamma}(x)|\lesssim 2^{10k}N^{\gamma}\langle\,\log N\rangle

in order to get (
3.37
). By using (
3.42
) as in Case 1, we may assume that
t\lesssim|x|
for the remainder of the proof.

\bullet
Subcase 2.1:
|Q_{x}(y)|\gg\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|
.
By (
3.43
), we infer that

\displaystyle\big|t^{2}-|x-y|^{2}\big|\sim|Q_{x}(y)|.

Hence, by (
3.40
) with (
3.41
) and by arguing as in (
3.45
) - (
3.47
) in Case 1, we have

\displaystyle|H^{k}_{N,t,\gamma}(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{2}t^{\gamma}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|y|<2^{k}N^{-1}}\,|Q_{x}(y)|^{-\gamma}\mathbf{1}_{|Q_{x}(y)|\gtrsim N^{-10}}dy

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{2k}N^{\gamma}\langle\,\log N\rangle,

as required in (
3.50
).

\bullet
Subcase 2.2:
|Q_{x}(y)|\sim\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|
.
In this case, instead of doing an explicit computation as in Subcase 2.1, we rely on a simple geometric observation combined with a dyadic localization argument.

Let
\mu\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}}
and
\varepsilon=\text{sgn}(t^{2}-|x^{2}|)\in\{+1,-1\}
. Define the sets

\displaystyle E^{\varepsilon}_{k,\mu}=\Big\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:\frac{\mu}{2}<\Big|\varepsilon+\frac{Q_{x}(y)}{\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|}\Big|\leq\mu\Big\}.

Let
\mu
be such that
E^{\varepsilon}_{k,\mu}
is non-empty. Then by (
3.43
) and (
3.40
), we necessarily have that

(3.51)

\displaystyle\mu\gtrsim\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|^{-1}N^{-10}\gtrsim N^{-10}.

With these notations and (
3.41
), we have

(3.52)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|H^{k}_{N,t,\gamma}(x)|&\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}}\\
N^{-10}\lesssim\mu\lesssim 1\end{subarray}}N^{2}t^{\gamma}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|y|<2^{k}N^{-1}}\,\mathbf{1}_{E^{\varepsilon}_{k,\mu}}(y)\,\mathbf{1}_{|x-y|\sim t}\,\big|t^{2}-|x-y|^{2}\big|^{-\gamma}dy\\
&=:\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}}\\
N^{-10}\lesssim\mu\lesssim 1\end{subarray}}\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt^{\varepsilon}_{k,\mu}\end{split}

for all
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
. In order to obtain (
3.50
), it thus suffices to prove the following estimate:

(3.53)

\displaystyle\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}}\\
N^{-10}\lesssim\mu\lesssim 1\end{subarray}}|\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt^{\varepsilon}_{k,\mu}|\lesssim 2^{10k}N^{\gamma}.

in view of the condition (
3.51
).

Fix
\mu\lesssim 1
. Then by definition of the set
E^{\varepsilon}_{k,\mu}
and (
3.52
), we have

(3.54)

\displaystyle|\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt^{\varepsilon}_{k,\mu}|\lesssim N^{2}\mu^{-\gamma}|t-|x||^{-\gamma}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{B(0,2^{k}N^{-1})\cap E^{\varepsilon}_{k,\mu}}(y)\,\mathbf{1}_{|x-y|\sim t}\,dy.

It is easy to see that
B(0,2^{k}N^{-1})\cap E^{\varepsilon}_{k,\mu}
is included in a dilate of
B(0,2^{k}N^{-1})\cap(R_{+}\cup R_{-})
, where
R_{\sigma}
is the rectangle given by

\displaystyle R_{\sigma}:=

\displaystyle\bigg\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:\big(-\sigma\varepsilon+\frac{\mu}{2}\big)\frac{\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|}{2|x|}+O\Big(\frac{2^{2k}N^{-2}}{|x|}\Big)<\langle\sigma e(x),y\rangle

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\leq\big(-\sigma\varepsilon+\mu\big)\frac{\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|}{2|x|}+O\Big(\frac{2^{2k}N^{-2}}{|x|}\Big)\bigg\},

with
e(x)=\frac{x}{|x|}\in\mathbb{S}^{1}
and
\sigma\in\{+,-\}
. The rectangle
R_{\sigma}
has dimensions about

\bigg(\mu\frac{|t^{2}-|x|^{2}|}{|x|}+O\Big(\frac{2^{2k}N^{-2}}{|x|}\Big)\bigg)\times 1

in the directions
\mathbb{R}e(x)
and
\mathbb{R}e(x)^{\perp}
, respectively. Noting that the area of the intersection of any any ball of radius
r>0
and any rectangle of dimensions
r_{1},r_{2}>0
is bounded by
\min(r,r_{1})\min(r,r_{2})
, we have that

\displaystyle|B(0,2^{k}N^{-1})\cap(R_{+}\cup R_{-})|\lesssim 2^{k}N^{-1}\bigg(\mu\frac{\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|}{|x|}+O\Big(\frac{2^{2k}N^{-2}}{|x|}\Big)\bigg).

Thus, plugging the above bound in (
3.54
) together with (
3.49
) and the condition
t\lesssim|x|
yields

(3.55)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt^{\varepsilon}_{k,\mu}|&\lesssim 2^{k}N\mu^{1-\gamma}|t-|x||^{1-\gamma}+2^{3k}|t-|x||^{-\gamma}\mu^{-\gamma}\frac{N^{-1}}{|x|}\\
&\lesssim 2^{2k}\mu^{1-\gamma}N^{\gamma}+2^{3k}|t-|x||^{-\gamma}\mu^{-\gamma}\frac{N^{-1}}{|x|}.\end{split}

Hence, the bound (
3.55
) is enough to get (
3.53
) if
|t-|x||^{-\gamma}\mu^{-\gamma}\frac{N^{-1}}{|x|}\lesssim N^{\gamma}
. Otherwise, we have

(3.56)

\displaystyle N^{1+\gamma}\ll\frac{\mu^{-\gamma}}{|x|}|t-|x||^{-\gamma}.

Let us assume that (
3.42
) holds. We go back to (
3.54
) and bound the integral on the right-hand-side so as to get an estimate with appropriate decay in the parameter
\mu
. If
y\in E^{\varepsilon}_{k,\mu}
then we have
\big|t^{2}-|x-y|^{2}\big|\sim\mu\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|
by definition. Hence, by a polar change of variables, we have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{B(0,2^{k}N^{-1})\cap E^{\varepsilon}_{k,\mu}}(y)\,\mathbf{1}_{|x-y|\sim t}\,dy

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|x-y|\sim t}\,\mathbf{1}_{|t^{2}-|x-y|^{2}|\sim\mu|t^{2}-|x|^{2}|}\,dy

\displaystyle\lesssim\mu\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|,

which, from the condition
t\lesssim|x|
, gives the estimate

(3.57)

\displaystyle|\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt^{\varepsilon}_{k,\mu}|\lesssim N^{2}\mu^{1-\gamma}|x|\cdot|t-|x||^{1-\gamma}.

Now, by (
3.56
) and (
3.49
) successively, we bound the factor
N^{2}
in (
3.57
), leading to

\displaystyle|\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt^{\varepsilon}_{k,\mu}|

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{1-\gamma}\mu^{1-2\gamma}|t-|x||^{1-2\gamma}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{(1-2\gamma)k}N^{\gamma}\mu^{1-2\gamma},

where we used the condition
0<\gamma\leq\frac{1}{2}
. This shows (
3.53
) and proves (
3.37
).

The proof of (
3.38
) follows from similar arguments as that of (
3.37
) and we omit details.

Let us briefly explain how to obtain (
3.37
) in the case
\mathcal{M}=\mathbb{R}^{2}
. The bound (
3.38
) for
\mathcal{M}=\mathbb{R}^{2}
then also follows from similar arguments. We decompose
H^{k}_{N,t,\gamma}
as follows

(3.58)

\displaystyle\begin{split}H^{k}_{N,t,\gamma}(x)&=N^{2}\int_{B(0,20)}\mathbf{1}_{|y|<2^{k}N^{-1}}\,H_{t}(x-y)dy\\
&\qquad\qquad+N^{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus B(0,20)}\mathbf{1}_{|y|<2^{k}N^{-1}}\,H_{t}(x-y)dy\\
&=:H^{k,1}_{N,t,\gamma}(x)+H^{k,2}_{N,t,\gamma}(x)\end{split}

for any
x\in B(0,10)
. Fix
x\in B(0,10)
and let
y
be in the integrand of
H^{k,2}_{N,t,\gamma}(x)
, which is contained in
\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus B(0,20)
. Then we have

H_{t,\gamma}(x-y)\sim|y|^{-\alpha}\gtrsim 1,

since
0<t\leq 1
and hence

(3.59)

\displaystyle|H^{k,2}_{N,t,\gamma}(x)|\lesssim 2^{2k}.

On the other hand, since
H^{k,1}_{N,t,\gamma}(x)
is an integral over a compact domain, by arguing as in the proof of (
3.37
) in the periodic case, we have

|H^{k,1}_{N,t,\gamma}(x)|\lesssim 2^{100k}\min\!\big\{N^{\gamma},|t-|x||^{-\gamma}\big\}\,\bigl\langle\,\log\!\big(\!\min\!\big\{N,|t-|x||^{-1}\big\}\big)\bigr\rangle

for any
x\in B(0,10
), which together with the bounds (
3.58
) and (
3.59
) proves (
3.37
) in the case
\mathcal{M}=\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
∎

Next, we state a result allows to differentiate functions of the form
\big(\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}f\big)*g
for smooth enough functions
f
and
g
, where
\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}
is the indicator function of the ball
B(0,t)
for some
t>0
. This result is crucial to study convolutions with the kernel
W
defined in (
2.25
); see Lemma
3.6
and
3.11
below.

Lemma 3.5
.

Fix
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
|\alpha|=1
and
0<t\leq 1
. Let
f\in W^{1,1}(B(0,2))\cap C^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2};\mathbb{R})
13
13

13

Here,
W^{1,1}(O)
for an open set
O\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
is the space
W^{1,1}(O)=\{f\in\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(O):(f,\nabla f)\in(L^{1}(O))^{2}\}
endowed with the norm
\|f\|_{W^{1,1}(O)}=\|f\|_{L^{1}(O)}+\|\nabla f\|_{L^{1}(O)}
.
and
g\in W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})\cap C^{0}(\mathbb{R}^{2};\mathbb{R})
. Consider the function
T
given by

T=\big(\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}f\big)*\varphi.

Then, the following formula holds:

(3.60)

\displaystyle\partial^{\alpha}_{x}T(x)=\big(\big(\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f\big)*g\big)(x)-\int_{\mathbb{S}^{1}(t)}f(y)g(x-y)\alpha\cdot y\,d\sigma_{t}(y)

for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and where
d\sigma_{t}
denotes the normalized surface measure on
\mathbb{S}^{1}(t)
.

Note that under the smoothness assumptions on
f
and
g
in Lemma
3.5
, all the terms in (
3.60
) make sense.

Proof.

We fix
t=1
in the proof for convenience. Let
\{\nu_{\varepsilon}\}_{\varepsilon\in(0,1)}
be a sequence of smooth functions such that

(3.61)

\displaystyle 0\leq\nu_{\varepsilon}\leq 1\ \ \text{on}\ \mathbb{R}^{2},\ \nu_{\varepsilon}\equiv 1\ \text{on}\ B(0,1)\ \text{and}\ \text{Supp}(\nu_{\varepsilon})\subset B(0,1+\varepsilon)\ \text{for}\ \varepsilon\in(0,1).

Let
T_{\varepsilon}=\big(\nu_{\varepsilon}f\big)*g
for
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. Then, by (
3.61
) and Hölder’s inequality, we have that

(3.62)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|T_{\varepsilon}-T\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}&=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\Big|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}(\nu_{\varepsilon}(y)-\mathbf{1}_{B(0,1)}(y))f(y)g(x-y)dy\Big|\\
&\leq\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus B(0,1)}|\nu_{\varepsilon}(y)-\mathbf{1}_{B(0,1)}(y)||f(y)||g(x-y)|dy\\
&\leq\|f\|_{L^{1}(B(0,1+\varepsilon)\setminus B(0,1))}\|g\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\longrightarrow 0,\end{split}

as
\varepsilon\to 0
, by dominated convergence. Thus, we have
T_{\varepsilon}\to T
in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
as
\varepsilon\to 0
and hence
\partial_{x}^{\alpha}T_{\varepsilon}\to\partial_{x}^{\alpha}T
in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Moreover, since
|\alpha|=1
, we have

(3.63)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}T_{\varepsilon}&=\big(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\nu_{\varepsilon}f\big)*g+\big(\nu_{\varepsilon}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f\big)*g\\
&=:\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{\varepsilon}+\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}_{\varepsilon}.\end{split}

By an immediate modification of (
3.62
), we get

(3.64)

\displaystyle\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}_{\varepsilon}\to\big(\mathbf{1}_{B(0,1)}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f\big)*g

in
L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. On the other hand, by (
3.61
), we note that
\partial_{x}\nu_{\varepsilon}
is supported in
B(0,1+\varepsilon)\setminus B(0,1)
and
\nu_{\varepsilon}\equiv 0
on
\mathbb{S}^{1}(1+\varepsilon)
. Hence, by Green’s formula

[
34
, Theorem 2 on p. 712]
, we have

(3.65)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{\varepsilon}(x)&=\int_{B(0,1+\varepsilon)\setminus B(0,1)}\partial_{y}^{\alpha}\nu_{\varepsilon}(y)f(y)g(x-y)dy\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{1}(1+\varepsilon)}\nu_{\varepsilon}(y)f(y)g(x-y)\frac{\alpha\cdot y}{1+\varepsilon}\,d\sigma_{1+\varepsilon}(y)\\
&\qquad\qquad-\int_{\mathbb{S}^{1}(1)}\nu_{\varepsilon}(y)f(y)g(x-y)\alpha\cdot y\,d\sigma_{1}(y)\\
&\qquad\qquad-\int_{B(0,1+\varepsilon)\setminus B(0,1)}\nu_{\varepsilon}(y)\partial_{y}^{\alpha}\big(f(y)g(x-y)\big)dy\\
&=-\int_{\mathbb{S}^{1}(1)}f(y)g(x-y)\alpha\cdot y\,d\sigma_{1}(y)\\
&\qquad\qquad-\int_{B(0,1+\varepsilon)\setminus B(0,1)}\nu_{\varepsilon}(y)\partial_{y}^{\alpha}\big(f(y)g(x-y)\big)dy\\
&=:-\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt^{1}(x)-\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt^{2}_{\varepsilon}(x).\end{split}

By (
3.61
), Hölder’s inequality and the dominated convergence theorem, we have

(3.66)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt^{2}_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}&\leq\|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}f\|_{W^{1,1}(B(0,1+\varepsilon)\setminus B(0,1))}\|g\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}\longrightarrow 0,\end{split}

as
\varepsilon\to 0
.

Combining (
3.63
), (
3.64
), (
3.65
) and (
3.66
) shows that
\partial_{x}T_{\varepsilon}
converges to the right-hand-side of (
3.60
) as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Recall that
\partial_{x}T_{\varepsilon}
also converges to
\partial_{x}T
as
\varepsilon\to 0
. Thus, (
3.60
) follows from uniqueness of the limit in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
.
∎

In the following lemma, we obtain bounds on smoothed functions whose derivatives exhibit hyperbolic singularities.

Lemma 3.6
.

Fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\nu_{N}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2};\mathbb{R})
satisfying the estimate (
3.7
). Fix
0<t\leq 1
and let
W_{t}\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\mathbb{S}^{1}(t);\mathbb{R})
be a function so that there exists a constant
C_{t}>0
depending only on
t
such that

(3.67)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|W_{t}(x)|&\lesssim C_{t},\\
|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}W_{t}(x)|&\lesssim(t+|x|)^{-\frac{1}{2}}|t-|x||^{\frac{1}{2}-|\alpha|}\end{split}

for all
x\in B(0,10)\setminus\mathbb{S}^{1}(t)
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
1\leq|\alpha|\leq 2
. Set
{W_{N,t}=(\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}W_{t})*\nu_{N}}
. Then, the following bound holds:

(3.68)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}W_{N,t}(x)|&\lesssim C_{t}\cdot\min\!\big\{N^{|\alpha|},|t-|x||^{-|\alpha|}\big\}\end{split}

for all
x\in B(0,10)
,
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
1\leq|\alpha|\leq 2
. Here, the implicit constant is independent of
N

Note that we state Lemma
3.6
in the full space setting and not on
\mathbb{T}^{2}
. This allows us to avoid technical issues related to the specific spatial localization that is used in the proof; see Remark
3.8
for a more thorough discussion on this point.

Remark 3.7
.

Note that for
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
1\leq|\alpha|\leq 2
, the pointwise bound on
\partial_{x}^{\alpha}W_{N,t}
is no matching that on
\partial_{x}^{\alpha}W_{t}
. This is because the worse contribution to
\partial_{x}^{\alpha}W_{N,t}
comes from the (formal) scenario when the
\partial_{x}^{\alpha}
hits the indicator function
\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}(x)
, which roughly speaking gives the normalized measure
d\sigma_{t}
on the sphere
\mathbb{S}^{1}(t)
; see Lemma
3.5
. Then from (
3.7
), one can prove the estimate

(3.69)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\nu_{N}*d\sigma_{t}(x)|&=\Big|\int_{\mathbb{S}^{1}(t)}\nu_{N}(x-y)d\sigma_{t}(y)\Big|\\
&\lesssim N\cdot\langle N\operatorname{dist}(x,\mathbb{S}^{1}(t))\rangle^{-10}\\
&\lesssim\min\!\big\{N,|t-|x||^{-1}\big\}\end{split}

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Hence, the bound (
3.69
) (formally) justifies the form of the right-hand-side of (
3.68
) for
|\alpha|=1
.

Proof.

Fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
0<t\leq 1
. Let
\varphi
and
\phi
be as in (
2.37
). We define
\{\phi_{N,k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
as follows:

\displaystyle\phi_{N,0}(x)=\varphi(Nx)

and

(3.70)

\displaystyle\phi_{N,k}(x)=\phi(2^{-k}Nx)

for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
. By construction, we have
\phi_{N,k}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2};[0,1])
for all
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\phi_{N,k}(x)=1

for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Moreover, we have
\operatorname{Supp}(\phi_{N,0})\subset\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:|x|\lesssim N^{-1}\}
and
{\operatorname{Supp}(\phi_{N,k})\subset\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:|x|\sim 2^{k}N^{-1}\}}
for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

For all
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, set

(3.71)

\displaystyle W^{k}_{N,t}=(\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}W_{t})*(\phi_{N,k}\,\nu_{N}),

such that

(3.72)

\displaystyle W_{N,t}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}W^{k}_{N,t}.

Fix
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
1\leq|\alpha|\leq 2
. From (
3.72
), we deduce that (
3.68
) follows from the estimate

(3.73)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}W^{k}_{N,t}(x)|&\lesssim 2^{-100k}C_{t}\min\!\big\{N^{|\alpha|},|t-|x||^{-|\alpha|}\big\}\end{split}

for all
x\in B(0,10)
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
.

We further break
W^{k}_{N,t}
into two parts, depending on whether we want to distribute the derivative
\partial^{\alpha}_{x}
to the first or second factor in the convolution (
3.71
). To this end, let
M\gg 1
be a large constant and define
\lambda\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R};[0,1])
such that

(3.74)

\displaystyle\lambda(s)=\begin{cases}1\quad&\text{if $|s|<M$},\\
0\quad&\text{if $|s|\geq M+1$}.\end{cases}

Now, we write

(3.75)

\displaystyle\begin{split}W^{k}_{N,t}&=\big(\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}W_{t}\,\lambda\big(2^{-k}N(t-|\cdot|)\big)\big)*(\phi_{N,k}\,\nu_{N})\\
&\qquad\qquad+\big(\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}W_{t}\,(1-\lambda)\big(2^{-k}N(t-|\cdot|)\big)\big)*(\phi_{N,k}\,\nu_{N})\\
&=:W^{k,1}_{N,t}+W^{k,2}_{N,t}.\end{split}

Therefore, (
3.73
) is a consequence of the bound

(3.76)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}W^{k,j}_{N,t}(x)|&\lesssim 2^{-100k}C_{t}\min\!\big\{N^{|\alpha|},|t-|x||^{-|\alpha|}\big\}\end{split}

for all
x\in B(0,10)
and
j\in\{1,2\}
. We focus on proving (
3.76
) in what follows.

\bullet
Proof of (
3.76
) for
j=1
.
By (
3.75
) and (
3.67
), we have

(3.77)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}W^{k,1}_{N,t}(x)&=\big(\big(\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}W_{t}\,\lambda\big(2^{-k}N(t-|\cdot|)\big)\big)*\big(\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\{\phi_{N,k}\,\nu_{N}\}\big)\big)(x)\\
&=\int_{B(x,t)}W_{t}(x-y)\lambda\big(2^{-k}N(t-|x-y|)\big)\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\{\phi_{N,k}\,\nu_{N}\}(y)dy.\end{split}

Fix
x
in
B(0,10)\setminus\mathbb{S}^{1}(t)
and in the support of
\partial^{\alpha}_{x}W^{k,1}_{N,t}
and let
y
be in the support of the integrand
\partial^{\alpha}_{x}W^{k,1}_{N,t}(x)
.
14
14

14

If
\operatorname{supp}(\partial^{\alpha}_{x}W^{k,1}_{N,t})\cap B(0,10)=\emptyset
, there is nothing to show. We will discard such cases without further mention in this proof.
In view of the definition of
\lambda
, we have

(3.78)

\displaystyle 0<|t-|x-y||\leq(M+1)\cdot 2^{k}N^{-1}.

Since
|y|\lesssim 2^{k}N^{-1}
, we deduce that

(3.79)

\displaystyle|t-|x||\lesssim M\cdot 2^{k}N^{-1}.

By definition of
\phi_{N,k}
and (
3.7
), the following bound holds:

(3.80)

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\{\phi_{N,k}\,\nu_{N}\}(z)\big|\lesssim 2^{-200k}N^{|\alpha|}\mathbf{1}_{|z|\lesssim 2^{k}N^{-1}}

for all
z\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Therefore, by (
3.67
), (
3.77
), (
3.80
) and (
3.79
), we have

(3.81)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}W^{k,1}_{N,t}(x)|&\lesssim 2^{-200k}C_{t}N^{2+|\alpha|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|y|\lesssim 2^{k}N^{-1}}dy\\
&\lesssim 2^{-198k}C_{t}N^{|\alpha|}\\
&\lesssim_{M}2^{-197k}C_{t}\min\!\big\{N^{|\alpha|},|t-|x||^{-|\alpha|}\big\}\end{split}

for any
x\in B(0,10)
. Therefore, (
3.73
) for
j=1
follows from (
3.81
).

\bullet
Proof of (
3.76
) for
j=2
.
Note that the functions

\displaystyle f:x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}

\displaystyle\mapsto W_{t}(x)\,(1-\lambda)\big(2^{-k}N(t-|x|)\big),

\displaystyle g:x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}

\displaystyle\mapsto\phi_{N,k}(x)\,\nu_{N}(x).

satisfy the assumptions in Lemma
3.5
by definition of the bump function
\lambda
in (
3.74
). Moreover, by Lemma
3.5
, we have

(3.82)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}W^{k,2}_{N,t}(x)&=\big(\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\big\{W_{t}\,(1-\lambda)\big(2^{-k}N(t-|\cdot|)\big)\big\}\big)*(\phi_{N,k}\,\nu_{N})\big)(x)\\
&=\int_{B(x,t)}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\big\{W_{t}\,(1-\lambda)\big(2^{-k}N(t-|\cdot|)\big)\big\}(x-y)\,\phi_{N,k}(y)\,\nu_{N}(y)dy.\end{split}

Note that the boundary term in (
3.60
) vanishes in the current setting as
f\equiv 0
on
\mathbb{S}^{1}(t)
. Fix
x
in
B(0,10)\setminus\mathbb{S}^{1}(t)
and in the support of
\partial^{\alpha}_{x}W^{k,2}_{N,t}
and let
y
be in the support of the integrand
\partial^{\alpha}_{x}W^{k,2}_{N,t}(x)
. In view of the definition of
\lambda
, we have

\displaystyle|t-|x-y||\geq M\cdot 2^{k}N^{-1}.

Thus, since
|y|\lesssim 2^{k}N^{-1}
, we deduce that

(3.83)

\displaystyle|t+\varepsilon|x-y||\sim|t+\varepsilon|x||\gtrsim M\cdot 2^{k}N^{-1}

for any
\varepsilon\in\{+1,-1\}
, upon choosing
M
large enough. Besides, by (
3.67
) and definition of
\lambda
, we have

(3.84)

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\big\{W_{t}\,(1-\lambda)\big(2^{-k}N(t-|\cdot|)\big)\big\}(z)\big|\lesssim C_{t}|t-|z||^{-|\alpha|}

for all
z\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Note that here, we used the fact that the support of spatial derivative of
\lambda
is included in
\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:|z|\sim 1\}
so that we may exchange the factor
2^{-k}N
for the term
|t-|z||^{-1}
when a derivative hits the function
(1-\lambda)\big(2^{-k}N(t-|\cdot|)\big)
. Therefore, from (
3.7
), (
3.82
), (
3.84
) and (
3.83
), we have

(3.85)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}W^{k,2}_{N,t}(x)|&\lesssim 2^{-200k}C_{t}|t-|x||^{-|\alpha|}\\
&\lesssim_{M}2^{-198k}C_{t}\min\!\big\{N^{|\alpha|},|t-|x||^{-|\alpha|}\big\}\end{split}

for any
x\in B(0,10)
. Therefore, (
3.73
) for
j=2
follows from (
3.85
).
∎

Remark 3.8
.

In order to prove a periodic version (i.e. on
\mathbb{T}^{2}
and not
\mathbb{R}^{2}
) of Lemma
3.6
with the same arguments as in the proof above, one would need to construct a periodic function
\widetilde{\phi}_{N,k}
which (i) essentially coincides with
\phi_{N,k}
on
[-\pi,\pi)^{2}
and (ii) is smooth (since we need to differentiate
\phi_{N,k}
in our argument; see (
3.77
)). However, for
k
large enough, the support of the bump function
\phi_{N,k}
in (
3.70
) is strictly larger than the box
[-\pi,\pi)^{2}\cong\mathbb{T}^{2}
. Hence, it is not immediate to construct a function
\widetilde{\phi}_{N,k}
which satisfies both (i) and (ii) at the same time. This is the reason why we work in the full space setting in Lemma
3.6
. In practice, Lemma
3.6
will be used to study multipliers defined in the periodic setting via the Poisson formula
2.9
; see Subsection
5.2
.

Next, we consider smoothed an analogue of Lemma
3.6
when
W_{t}
is replaced with a smooth function and a variant of the Green’s function
G
(
2.13
) of the form:
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\{0\}\mapsto\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)^{c}}(x)G(x)
for some fixed
t>0
.
15
15

15

The set
B(0,t)^{c}
denotes the complement of the ball
B(0,t)
in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Corollary 3.9
.

Fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\nu_{N}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2};\mathbb{R})
satisfying the estimate (
3.7
). Fix
0<t\leq 1
and let
G
be the Green’s function (
2.13
) and
F\in C^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{2};\mathbb{R})
. Then, the following bounds hold.

(i)
Set
G_{N,t}=\big(\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)^{c}}G\big)*\nu_{N}
. Then, we have

(3.86)

\displaystyle|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}G_{N,t}(x)|\lesssim\bigl\langle\,\log\!\big(t+|x|+N^{-1}\big)\bigr\rangle\min\!\big\{N^{|\alpha|},|t-|x||^{-|\alpha|}\big\}

for any
x\in B(0,10)
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
1\leq|\alpha|\leq 2
. Here, the implicit constant is independent of
N
.

(ii)
Set
F_{N,t}=\big(\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}F\big)*\nu_{N}
. Then, we have

(3.87)

\displaystyle|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}F_{N,t}(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\min\!\big\{N^{|\alpha|},|t-|x||^{-|\alpha|}\big\}

for any
x\in B(0,10)
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
1\leq|\alpha|\leq 2
. Here, the implicit constant is independent of
N
.

Proof.

Fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
0<t\leq 1
. Let
\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2};[0,1])
be such that

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}(x)=\begin{cases}1\quad&\text{if $|x|<10^{-10}$},\\
0\quad&\text{if $|x|\geq 10^{-9}$}\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle\lambda_{2}(x)=\begin{cases}1\quad&\text{if $|x|<10^{10}$},\\
0\quad&\text{if $|x|\geq 10^{10}+1$}.\end{cases}

Define the functions
\widetilde{G}_{t}
and
\widetilde{F}_{t}
via the formulas

\displaystyle\widetilde{G}_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=G(x)\cdot(1-\lambda_{1})\Big(\frac{x}{t}\Big),

\displaystyle\widetilde{F}_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=F(x)\cdot\lambda_{2}\Big(\frac{x}{t}\Big).

Then, we have

(3.88)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big(\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)^{c}}G\big)*\nu_{N}&=\big(\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)^{c}}\widetilde{G}_{t}\big)*\nu_{N},\\
\big(\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}F\big)*\nu_{N}&=\big(\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}\widetilde{F}_{t}\big)*\nu_{N}.\end{split}

Moreover, by (
2.15
), it is easy to check that
\widetilde{G}_{t}
and
\widetilde{F}_{t}
satisfy the bounds (
3.67
) for
C_{t}=\langle\,\log t\rangle
and
C_{t}=1
, respectively. Therefore, (
3.87
) is a direct consequence of (
3.88
) and Lemma
3.6
. As for (
3.86
), a weaker version (with
\bigl\langle\,\log\!\big(t+|x|+N^{-1}\big)\bigr\rangle
replaced with
\langle\,\log(t)\rangle
) essentially follows from Lemma
3.6
.
16
16

16

Note that replacing
\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}
with
\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)^{c}}
does not change the bounds in Lemma
3.6
.
The improved factor
\bigl\langle\,\log\!\big(t+|x|+N^{-1}\big)\bigr\rangle
comes from a slight modification of the proof of Lemma
3.6
in order to take into account the singularity of
G
at the origin. We omit details.
∎

Remark 3.10
.

We note that there is no reason for the function
\big(\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}G\big)*\nu_{N}
to also satisfy the bound (
3.86
). Indeed, otherwise
G*\nu_{N}
would also satisfy (
3.86
), which is not compatible (uniformly in
N
) with the estimates in Lemma
3.2
(i) in the regime of parameters
|x|\ll t
.

The following result is a variant of Lemma
3.6
.

Lemma 3.11
.

Fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\nu_{N}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2};\mathbb{R})
satisfying the estimate (
3.7
). Fix
0<t\leq 1
and let
W^{1}_{t}\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\mathbb{S}^{1}(t);\mathbb{R})
and
W^{2}_{t}\in C^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\mathbb{\{}0\};\mathbb{R})
be functions such that

(3.89)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|W^{1}_{t}(x)|&\lesssim\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|^{-\frac{1}{2}},\\
|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}W^{1}_{t}(x)|&\lesssim(t+|x|)^{-\frac{1}{2}}|t-|x||^{-\frac{3}{2}}\end{split}

and

(3.90)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|W^{2}_{t}(y)|&\lesssim|y|^{-1},\\
|\partial_{y}^{\alpha}W^{2}_{t}(y)|&\lesssim|y|^{-2}\end{split}

for any
x\in B(0,10)\setminus\mathbb{S}^{1}(t)
,
y\in B(0,10)\setminus\{0\}
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
|\alpha|=1
. Then, the following bounds hold.

(i)
Set
{W^{1}_{N,t}=(\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}W^{1}_{t})*\nu_{N}}
. Then, we have

(3.91)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}W^{1}_{N,t}(x)|&\lesssim\min\!\big\{N^{2},(t+|x|)^{-\frac{1}{2}}|t-|x||^{-\frac{3}{2}}\big\}\bigl\langle\,\log\!\big(\!\min\!\big\{N,|t-|x||^{-1}\big\}\big)\bigr\rangle\end{split}

for any
x\in B(0,10)
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
|\alpha|=1
. Here, the implicit constant is independent of
N
.

(ii)
Set
{W^{2}_{N,t}=(\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}W^{2}_{t})*\nu_{N}}
. Then, we have

(3.92)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}W^{2}_{N,t}(x)|&\lesssim\big(\!\min\!\big\{N^{2},|x|^{-1}|t-|x||^{-1}\big\}+\min\!\big\{N^{2},|x|^{-2}\big\}\big)\\
&\qquad\quad\times\bigl\langle\,\log\!\big(\!\min\!\big\{N,|x|^{-1},|t-|x||^{-1}\big\}\big)\bigr\rangle\end{split}

for any
x\in B(0,10)\setminus\{0\}
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
|\alpha|=1
. Here, the implicit constant is independent of
N
.

Proof.

The proof follows from arguments which are similar to those in the proof of Lemma
3.6
and we omit details.
∎

Lastly, in the next lemma, we look at (smoothed) spatial convolutions of the wave kernel (
2.25
) with the Green’s function (
2.13
).

Lemma 3.12
.

Fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\nu_{N}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2};\mathbb{R})
satisfying the estimate (
3.7
). Fix
0<t\leq 1
and let
G
and
W(t,\cdot)
be the Green’s function (
2.13
) and the wave kernel (
2.25
), respectively. Set
Q_{N,t}=W(t,\cdot)*G*\nu_{N}
. Then, the following bounds hold:

(3.93)

\displaystyle|Q_{N,t}(x)|\lesssim 1

and

(3.94)

\displaystyle|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}Q_{N,t}(x)|\lesssim\min\!\big\{N^{|\alpha|-1},\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|^{-\frac{1}{2}(|\alpha|-1)}\big\}\,\bigl\langle\,\log\!\big(\!\min\!\big\{N,|t-|x||^{-1}\big\}\big)\bigr\rangle^{2}

for any
x\in B(0,10)
and multi-index
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
1\leq|\alpha|\leq 2
. Here, the implicit constant is independent of
N
.

Proof.

Fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
0<t\leq 1
. Set
G_{N}=G*\nu_{N}
. Note that (
3.93
) is immediate from the bounds on
G_{N}
in Lemma
3.2
(i). Thus, it remains to prove (
3.94
).

Let
\varphi,\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{2};[0,1])
be as in (
2.37
). We define
\chi_{0}
and
\chi_{\ell}
for
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
as follows:

\displaystyle\chi_{0}(x)=1-\varphi(2x)

and

\displaystyle\chi_{\ell}(x)=\phi(2^{\ell}x)

for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
. Note that
\chi_{0}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2};[0,1])
,
\chi_{\ell}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{2};[0,1])
for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and

\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\chi_{\ell}(x)=1

for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus\{0\}
. Moreover, we have
\operatorname{Supp}(\chi_{0})\subset\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:|x|\gtrsim 1\}
and
{\operatorname{Supp}(\chi_{\ell})\subset\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:|x|\sim 2^{-\ell}\}}
for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
.

Set
Q^{\alpha,\ell}_{N,t}=W(t,\cdot)*(\chi_{\ell}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}G_{N})
for each
\ell\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
. Then, by construction, we have

(3.95)

\displaystyle\partial_{x}^{\alpha}Q_{N,t}=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}Q^{\alpha,\ell}_{N,t}.

From (
3.9
) in Lemma
3.2
(i) and since the support of
\chi_{0}
is away from the origin, we have that

(3.96)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|Q_{N,t}^{\alpha,0}(x)|&\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}(x-y)}{\sqrt{t^{2}-|x-y|^{2}}}\chi_{0}(y)|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}G_{N}(y)|dy\\
&\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}(x-y)}{\sqrt{t^{2}-|x-y|^{2}}}\langle y\rangle^{-10}dy\\
&\lesssim 1\end{split}

for all
x\in B(0,10)
and all multi-index
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
|\alpha|\leq 2
.

Fix
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
|\alpha|=1
and
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
. Then, from (
3.9
) in Lemma
3.2
(i) and since
{\operatorname{supp}(\chi_{\ell})\subset\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:|x|\sim 2^{-\ell}\}\subset B(0,2)}
, we have

(3.97)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|Q_{N,t}^{\alpha,\ell}(x)|&\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}(x-y)}{\sqrt{t^{2}-|x-y|^{2}}}\chi_{\ell}(y)|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}G_{N}(y)|dy\\
&\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}(x-y)}{\sqrt{t^{2}-|x-y|^{2}}}\chi_{\ell}(y)\big(|y|+N^{-1}\big)^{-1}dy\\
&\lesssim 2^{\ell}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}(x-y)}{\sqrt{t^{2}-|x-y|^{2}}}\,\mathbf{1}_{|y|\lesssim 2^{-\ell}}dy\\
&\lesssim 2^{-\ell}\cdot\widetilde{H}^{0}_{2^{\ell},t,\frac{1}{2}}(x)\end{split}

for all
x\in B(0,10)
. Here,
\widetilde{H}^{0}_{2^{\ell},t,\frac{1}{2}}
is as in (
3.36
). Therefore, we deduce from (
3.97
) and (
3.38
) in Lemma
3.4
that

(3.98)

\displaystyle|Q_{N,t}^{\alpha_{1},\ell}(x)|\lesssim 2^{-\ell}\min\!\big\{2^{\ell},\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big\}\,\bigl\langle\,\log\!\big(\!\min\!\big\{2^{\ell},|t-|x||^{-1}\big\}\big)\bigr\rangle

for all
x\in B(0,10)
. Hence, by (
3.95
), (
3.96
) and summing (
3.98
) over
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, we deduce that

(3.99)

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}Q_{N,t}(x)|\lesssim\bigl\langle\,\log\big(|t-|x||^{-1}\big)\bigr\rangle^{2}

for any
x\in B(0,10)
.

An integration by parts argument with (
3.7
) shows

(3.100)

\displaystyle|\widehat{\nu}_{N}(\xi)|\lesssim\bigl\langle\frac{\xi}{N}\bigr\rangle^{-A}

for any
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
A\geq 1
. Hence, by (
2.24
), (
2.13
), (
3.100
) and the Hausdorff-Young inequality, we have

(3.101)

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}Q_{N,t}(x)|\lesssim\langle\,\log N\rangle.

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. The desired bound (
3.94
) in the case
|\alpha|=1
thus follows from (
3.99
) and (
3.101
).

We now prove (
3.94
) for
|\alpha|=2
. Here, we need to proceed with care in view of the lack of integrability of
\partial_{x}^{\alpha}G_{N}
near the origin (uniformly in
N
); see Lemma
3.2
(i). Fix
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
|\alpha|=2
and let
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
such that
\alpha=\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}
and
|\alpha_{1}|=|\alpha_{2}|=1
. Then, with the same notations as above (i.e. as in (
3.95
)), we have

(3.102)

\displaystyle\partial_{x}^{\alpha}Q_{N,t}=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\big\{W(t,\cdot)*(\chi_{\ell}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{1}}G_{N})\big\}=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}Q^{\alpha_{1},\ell}_{N,t}.

Fix
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and
\kappa\in(0,1)
to be chosen later. Let
\lambda\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R};[0,1])
be as in (
3.74
) and decompose
Q^{\alpha_{1},\ell}_{N,t}
as follows:

(3.103)

\displaystyle\begin{split}Q^{\alpha_{1},\ell}_{N,t}&=\big(W(t,\cdot)\,\lambda\big(2^{\ell\kappa}(t-|\cdot|)\big)\big)*(\chi_{\ell}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{1}}G_{N})\\
&\qquad+\big(W(t,\cdot)\,(1-\lambda)\big(2^{\ell\kappa}(t-|\cdot|)\big)\big)*(\chi_{\ell}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{1}}G_{N})\\
&=:\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{\ell}+\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}_{\ell}.\end{split}

We first bound
\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{\ell}
. Note that
\operatorname{supp}(\chi_{\ell})\subset\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:|x|\sim 2^{-\ell}\}
and hence by the chain rule and (
3.9
) in Lemma
3.2
(i), we have

(3.104)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big|\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\{\chi_{\ell}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{1}}G_{N}\}(z)\big|\lesssim 2^{2\ell}\mathbf{1}_{|z|\sim 2^{-\ell}}\end{split}

for all
z\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Therefore, by (
3.104
) and moving the derivative to the second factor in the convolution
\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{\ell}
, we have

(3.105)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big|\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{\ell}(x)\big|&\lesssim\big|\big(\big(W(t,\cdot)\lambda\big(2^{\ell\kappa}(t-|\cdot|)\big)\big)*\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\{\chi_{\ell}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{1}}G_{N}\}\big)(x)\big|\\
&\lesssim 2^{2\ell}\int_{B(x,t)}\frac{\lambda\big(2^{\ell\kappa}(t-|x-y|)\big)}{\sqrt{t^{2}-|x-y|^{2}}}\,\mathbf{1}_{|y|\lesssim 2^{-\ell}}dy\\
&\lesssim\widetilde{H}^{0}_{2^{\ell},t,\frac{1}{2}}(x)\\
&\lesssim\min\!\big\{2^{\ell},\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big\}\,\bigl\langle\,\log\!\big(\!\min\!\big\{2^{\ell},|t-|x||^{-1}\big\}\big)\bigr\rangle\end{split}

for all
x\in B(0,10)\setminus\mathbb{S}^{1}(t)
. Here,
\widetilde{H}^{0}_{2^{\ell},t,\frac{1}{2}}
is as in (
3.36
). Now, fix
x\in\big(B(0,10)\setminus\mathbb{S}^{1}(t)\big)\cap\operatorname{supp}(\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{\ell})
(if such a
x
does not exist, then there is nothing to show) and write

\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{\ell}(x)=\int_{B(x,t)}\frac{\lambda\big(2^{\ell\kappa}(t-|x-y|)\big)}{\sqrt{t^{2}-|x-y|^{2}}}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\{\chi_{\ell}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{1}}G_{N}\}(y)dy.

Therefore, there must exists
y
in the support of the integrand of
\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{\ell}(x)
such that

|t-|x-y||\lesssim 2^{-\ell\kappa},

by definition of
\lambda
. Since
|y|\lesssim 2^{-\ell}
and
\kappa\in(0,1]
, we must have

(3.106)

\displaystyle|t-|x||\lesssim 2^{-\ell\kappa}.

Therefore, combining (
3.105
) and (
3.106
) yields

(3.107)

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{\ell}(x)\big|\lesssim\ell 2^{\ell}\,\mathbf{1}_{2^{\ell}\lesssim|t^{2}-|x|^{2}|^{-\frac{1}{2}}}+\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bigl\langle\,\log\big(|t-|x||^{-1}\big)\bigr\rangle\mathbf{1}_{2^{\ell}\lesssim|t-|x||^{-\frac{1}{\kappa}}}

for any
x\in B(0,10)\setminus\mathbb{S}^{1}(t)
.

Now, we consider the contribution of the term
\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}_{\ell}
. Note that the functions

\displaystyle f:x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}

\displaystyle\mapsto\frac{W(t,\cdot)(1-\lambda)\big(2^{\ell\kappa}(t-|x|)\big)}{\sqrt{t^{2}-|x|^{2}}},

\displaystyle g:x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}

\displaystyle\mapsto G_{N}(x).

satisfy the assumptions in Lemma
3.5
by definition of the bump function
\lambda
in (
3.74
). Thus, by moving the
\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}
derivative to the first factor in the convolution
\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}_{\ell}
and Lemma
3.5
, we have

(3.108)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}_{\ell}(x)&=\big(\big(\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}(\cdot)\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\big\{(t^{2}-|\cdot|^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,(1-\lambda)\big(2^{\ell\kappa}(t-|\cdot|)\big)\big\}\big)*(\chi_{\ell}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{1}}G_{N})\big)(x)\\
&=\int_{B(x,t)}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\big\{(t^{2}-|\cdot|^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,(1-\lambda)\big(2^{\ell\kappa}(t-|\cdot|)\big)\big\}(x-y)(\chi_{\ell}\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{1}}G_{N})(y)dy.\end{split}

Note that the boundary term in (
3.60
) vanishes since
f\equiv 0
on
\mathbb{S}^{1}(t)
. Fix
x
in
B(0,10)\setminus\mathbb{S}^{1}(t)
and in the support of
\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}^{\ell}
and let
y
be in the support of the integrand
\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}^{\ell}(x)
. In view of the definition of
\lambda
, we have

\displaystyle|t-|x-y||\gtrsim 2^{-\ell\kappa}.

Thus, since
|y|\lesssim 2^{-\ell}
and
\kappa\in(0,1)
small enough, we deduce that

(3.109)

\displaystyle|t+\varepsilon|x-y||\sim|t+\varepsilon|x||\gtrsim 2^{-\ell\kappa}

for any
\varepsilon\in\{+1,-1\}
. Besides, by definition of
\lambda
, we have

(3.110)

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\big\{(t^{2}-|\cdot|^{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\,(1-\lambda)\big(2^{\ell\kappa}(t-|\cdot|)\big)\big\}(z)\big|\lesssim|t+|z||^{-\frac{1}{2}}|t-|z||^{-\frac{3}{2}}

for all
z\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Hence, by (
3.108
), (
3.109
), (
3.110
) and Lemma
3.2
(i), we have

(3.111)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}_{\ell}(x)|&\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{|t-|x||^{\frac{1}{\kappa}}\gtrsim 2^{-\ell}}\,|t+|x||^{-\frac{1}{2}}|t-|x||^{-\frac{3}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|y|\lesssim 2^{-\ell}}|y|^{-1}dy\\
&\lesssim|t+|x||^{-\frac{1}{2}}|t-|x||^{-\frac{3}{2}}\,2^{-\ell}\,\mathbf{1}_{|t-|x||^{\frac{1}{\kappa}}\gtrsim 2^{-\ell}}\end{split}

for all
x\in B(0,10)\setminus\mathbb{S}^{1}(t)
.

Therefore, from (
3.102
), (
3.103
), (
3.96
) and summing (
3.107
) and (
3.111
), we get

(3.112)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}Q_{N,t}(x)\big|&\lesssim_{\kappa}\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bigl\langle\,\log\big(|t-|x||^{-1}\big)\bigr\rangle^{2}+|t+|x||^{-\frac{1}{2}}|t-|x||^{-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{1}{\kappa}}\\
&\lesssim_{\kappa}\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\bigl\langle\,\log\big(|t-|x||^{-1}\big)\bigr\rangle^{2}\end{split}

for all
x\in B(0,10)\setminus\mathbb{S}^{1}(t)
and upon choosing
\kappa
small enough. By working on the Fourier side and arguing as in (
3.99
)-(
3.101
) above, we also get

(3.113)

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}Q_{N,t}(x)\big|\lesssim N.

Thus, the bound (
3.94
) for
|\alpha|=2
follows from (
3.112
) and (
3.113
).
∎

4.
Nonlinear analysis

In this section, we state key bilinear estimates for our well-posedness argument in Section
6
.

4.1.
Basic product estimates and fractional chain rules

Here, we recall standard product estimates and the fractional chain rule.

Our first estimate is a product estimate in Sobolev spaces. See
[
44
]
for a proof.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mathcal{M}=\mathbb{R}^{d}
or
\mathbb{T}^{d}
. Fix
0<s<1
and
1<p_{j},q_{j},r<\infty
with
\frac{1}{p_{j}}+\frac{1}{q_{j}}=\frac{1}{r}
,
j=1,2
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\big\|\langle\nabla\rangle^{\alpha}(fg)\big\|_{L^{r}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\lesssim\big\|\langle\nabla\rangle^{s}f\big\|_{L^{p_{1}}(\mathcal{M})}\|g\|_{L^{q_{1}}(\mathcal{M})}+\|f\|_{L^{p_{2}}(\mathcal{M})}\big\|\langle\nabla\rangle^{s}g\big\|_{L^{q_{2}}(\mathcal{M})}.

Next, we recall the fractional chain rule in Sobolev spaces.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mathcal{M}=\mathbb{R}^{d}
or
\mathbb{T}^{d}
. Fix
0<s<1
and
F
a Lipschitz function on
\mathbb{R}
such that
\|F^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\leq L
.
Then, for any
1<p<\infty
, we have

\displaystyle\big\||\nabla|^{\alpha}F(f)\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathcal{M})}\lesssim L\big\||\nabla|^{\alpha}f\big\|_{L^{p}(\mathcal{M})}.

The fractional chain rule on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
was essentially proved in
[
20
]
.
17
17

17

As pointed out in
[
88
]
,
the argument in
[
20
]
needs
a small correction, which yields the fractional chain rule in a
less general context.
See
[
54
,
88
,
95
]
.

As for the estimate on
\mathbb{T}^{d}
, see
[
39
]
.

4.2.
Weighted estimates

In this subsection, we record several weighted estimates. Namely, we study here the boundedness properties of convolution operators on spaces of the form
L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};w(z)dz)
,
18
18

18

That is, the space of functions whose
p^{\text{th}}
power is integrable with respect to the measure
w(z)dz
.
where
d\in\mathbb{N}
,
1<p<\infty
and
w
is non-negative function on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We will also consider spaces of the form
L^{p}_{t,x}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{2};w(t,x)dtdx)
for space-time weights
w=w(t,x)
.

Although in this work we mainly consider specific (time) weights of the form
w:t\in\mathbb{R}\mapsto\langle t\rangle^{a}
, with
a\in\mathbb{R}
, we introduce next a general class of weights which is standard in the literature on harmonic analysis.

Definition 4.3

(
A_{p}
weights)
.

Let
d\in\mathbb{N}
and
1<p<\infty
. We denote by
A_{p}
the set of non-negative locally integrable functions
w
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
for which there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\Big(\frac{1}{|B|}\int_{B}w\Big)\cdot\Big(\frac{1}{|B|}\int_{B}w^{-\frac{p^{\prime}}{p}}\Big)^{\frac{p}{p^{\prime}}}\leq C

for all balls
B\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Here,
p^{\prime}
denotes the dual Lebesgue exponent to
p
; i.e.
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}=1
.

We subsequently state a result regarding weighted estimates for singular integrals. See
[
89
, Theorem 2 in Chapter V]
for a proof.

Lemma 4.4
.

Fix
d\in\mathbb{N}
and
1<p<\infty
. Let
T
be a convolution operator with distribution kernel
K
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Namely,
T(f)=K*f
for any
f\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. We assume that the kernel
K
satisfies the following:

(i)

\displaystyle\ \ |\partial_{x}^{\alpha}K(x)|\lesssim|x|^{-d-|\alpha|}\quad\text{for all $x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\setminus\{0\}$ and $\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}^{d}$ with $|\alpha|\leq 1$},

(ii)

\displaystyle\ \ \widehat{K}\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Then, the operator
T
maps
L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d};w(z)dz)
into itself.

In practice, we use the following specific version of Lemma
4.4
.

Corollary 4.5
.

Fix
a\in(-1,1)
,
r\in\mathbb{R}
,
N_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
and a bump function
\lambda\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R})
. Let
\mathcal{H}_{0}
and
T
be the Fourier multiplier given by

(4.1)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widehat{\mathcal{H}_{0}(f)}(\tau)&=-i\,\textup{sgn}(\tau)\cdot\widehat{f}(\tau),\\
\widehat{T(f)}(\tau)&=\lambda\Big(\frac{|\tau|+r}{N_{0}}\Big)\cdot\widehat{f}(\tau)\end{split}

for any
\tau\in\mathbb{R}
. Here,
sgn
is as in (
2.28
).
19
19

19

The projection onto the
\tau
-coordinate of the Fourier multiplier
\mathcal{H}
defined in (
2.30
) basically gives
\mathcal{H}_{0}
.
Then, the operators
\mathcal{H}_{0}
and
T
map
L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\langle t\rangle^{a}dt)
into itself.

Proof.

We first observe that for
a\in(-1,1)
,
t\mapsto\langle t\rangle^{a}
is a
A_{2}
weight following Definition
4.3
. The convolution kernel
K_{0}
of the Hilbert transform
\mathcal{H}_{0}
coincides with the function
t\in\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}\mapsto\frac{c}{t}
, where
c>0
is a constant, on
\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
. See for instance
[
90
, Chapter III]
. Hence,
K_{0}
satisfies the assumptions in Lemma
4.4
and
\mathcal{H}_{0}
is bounded on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\langle t\rangle^{a}dt)
.

On the other hand, note that the symbols
\mathbf{1}_{\tau>0}
and
\mathbf{1}_{\tau<0}
are of the form

c_{1}+c_{1}m(\tau),

where
m
is the symbol of the Hilbert transform
\mathcal{H}_{0}
and
c_{1},c_{2}\in\mathbb{C}
. Therefore, the boundedness of
T
on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\langle t\rangle^{a}dt)
reduces to that of the multipliers

\displaystyle\widehat{T_{+}(f)}(\tau)

\displaystyle=\lambda\Big(\frac{\tau+r}{N_{0}}\Big)\cdot\widehat{f}(\tau),

\displaystyle\widehat{T_{-}(f)}(\tau)

\displaystyle=\lambda\Big(\frac{-\tau+r}{N_{0}}\Big)\cdot\widehat{f}(\tau)

on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\langle t\rangle^{a}dt)
. For each
\varepsilon\in\{+,-\}
, an integration by parts argument shows that the kernel
K_{\varepsilon}
of
T_{\varepsilon}
satisfies the bound

\displaystyle|K_{\varepsilon}^{\prime}(t)|\lesssim N_{0}^{1+|\alpha|}\langle N_{0}t\rangle^{-10}\lesssim|t|^{-1-|\alpha|}

for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, with implicit constants uniform in
r
. Hence, by Lemma
4.4
,
T_{\varepsilon}
is bounded on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\langle t\rangle^{a}dt)
.
∎

Next, for a dyadic triplet
(N,R,L)\in(2^{\mathbb{N}})^{2}\times 2^{\mathbb{Z}}
and
b\in\mathbb{R}
, we consider the Fourier multiplier
\mathcal{C}^{b}_{N,R,L}
on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}
given by

(4.2)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{t,x}\big[\mathcal{C}^{b}_{N,R,L}u\big](\tau,n)=\phi\Big(\frac{n}{N}\Big)m^{b}_{R,L}(\tau,n)\cdot\widehat{u}(\tau,n),\quad(\tau,n)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}^{2},

where
m_{b}
is the symbol

(4.3)

\displaystyle m^{b}_{R,L}(\tau,n)=\big||\tau|-|n|\big|^{b}\eta\Big(\frac{\tau}{R}\Big)\psi\Big(\frac{|\tau|-|n|}{L}\Big).

Here, the bump functions
\phi
,
\eta
and
\psi
are as in (
2.37
).
Recall in particular that the supports of
\eta
and
\psi
are away from the origin. Hence, the map

(4.4)

\displaystyle m^{0}_{R,L}(\cdot,n):\tau\mapsto\eta\Big(\frac{\tau}{R}\Big)\psi\Big(\frac{|\tau|-|n|}{L}\Big)

is smooth on
\mathbb{R}
for each
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}
, with derivative bounded by

(4.5)

\displaystyle|\partial_{\tau}m^{0}_{R,L}(\tau,n)|\lesssim(R^{-1}+L^{-1})\cdot\mathbf{1}_{||\tau|-|n||\sim L}.

We also note that
\mathcal{C}^{b}_{N,R,L}
is related to the multiplier
\mathbf{M}_{N,R,L}
defined in (
2.40
) via the formula:

(4.6)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{b}_{N,R,L}=\big||\partial_{t}|-|\nabla|\big|^{b}\mathbf{M}_{N,R,L}.

Operators of the form
\mathcal{C}^{b}_{N,R,L}
are referred to as
cone multipliers
in the Euclidean harmonic analysis literature and have been heavily studied; see for instance
[
9
,
49
,
53
,
62
,
68
,
93
,
94
,
98
]
and references therein.

The following weighted
L^{2}
estimate on
\mathcal{C}^{b}_{N,R,L}
(and variants) plays a crucial role in our bilinear analysis; see Section
4.3
below.

Lemma 4.6
.

Fix
a\in(0,1)
,
b\in\mathbb{R}
and
\mathcal{A}\in\{\mathrm{Id},\mathcal{C},\mathcal{H}\mathcal{C}\}
, where
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{C}
are as in
(
2.30
) and (
2.31
), respectively.
20
20

20

Here, with a slight abuse of notation, we also denote by
\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{C}
the natural spatially periodic versions of the multipliers in (
2.30
) and (
2.31
).
Fix
(N,R,L)\in(2^{\mathbb{N}})^{2}\times 2^{\mathbb{Z}}
and let
\mathcal{C}^{b}_{N,R,L}
be as in (
4.2
)-(
4.3
). Then,
\mathcal{A}\mathcal{C}^{b}_{N,R,L}
maps
{L^{2}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{2};\langle t\rangle^{a}dtdx)}
into itself and we have the bound

(4.7)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|\langle t\rangle^{a}\mathcal{A}\mathcal{C}^{b}_{N,R,L}u\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}&\lesssim L^{b}\big(1+L^{-(1-\delta_{\circ})(a+\frac{\delta_{\circ}}{10})}\big)\cdot\|\langle t\rangle^{a}u\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\\
&\qquad+L^{b}\big(1+L^{-a(1-\delta_{\circ})-\delta_{\circ}}\big)\cdot\big\|\mathcal{F}_{t,x}^{-1}[\mathbf{1}_{||\tau|-|n||\lesssim L^{1-\delta_{\circ}}}\,\widehat{u}(\tau,n)]\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\end{split}

for any small enough
\delta_{\circ}>0
.

Proof.

The boundedness of
\mathcal{H}_{0}
defined in (
4.1
) on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R};\langle t\rangle^{a}dt)
immediately implies that of
\mathcal{H}
on
L^{2}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{2};\langle t\rangle^{a}dtdx)
by Plancherel’s identity since

\mathcal{F}_{x}[\mathcal{H}(u)](t,n)=\mathcal{H}_{0}(\mathcal{F}_{x}[u](\cdot,n))(t)

for each
(t,n)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}^{2}
. Therefore, by Corollary
4.5
, it suffices to prove the statement with
\mathcal{A}=\mathrm{Id}
or
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{C}
. Next, noting that the symbol
(\tau,n)\mapsto\mathbf{1}_{|\tau|>|n|}
of
\mathcal{C}
is a
0
-homogeneous, we have

\psi\Big(\frac{|\tau|-|n|}{L}\Big)\mathbf{1}_{|\tau|>|n|}=\widetilde{\psi}\Big(\frac{|\tau|-|n|}{L}\Big),

where
\widetilde{\psi}(\tau)=\psi(\tau)\mathbf{1}_{\tau>0}
is bump function which is smooth on
\mathbb{R}\setminus\{0\}
and whose support is away from the origin. Thus, the map (
4.4
) where
\psi
is replaced with
\widetilde{\psi}
is smooth on
\mathbb{R}
. Hence, up to changing the bump function
\psi
, it suffices to prove (
4.7
) for
\mathcal{A}=\mathrm{Id}
. Similarly, since
(\tau,n)\mapsto\big||\tau|-|n|\big|^{b}
is
b
-homogeneous it suffices to prove (
4.7
) for
b=0
. Our goal is thus to prove

(4.8)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|\langle t\rangle^{a}\mathcal{C}^{0}_{N,R,L}u\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}&\lesssim\big(1+L^{-(1-\delta_{\circ})(a+\frac{\delta_{\circ}}{10})}\big)\cdot\|\langle t\rangle^{a}u\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\\
&\qquad+\big(1+L^{-a(1-\delta_{\circ})-\delta_{\circ}}\big)\cdot\big\|\mathcal{F}_{t,x}^{-1}[\mathbf{1}_{||\tau|-|n||\lesssim L^{1-\delta_{\circ}}}\,\widehat{u}(\tau,n)]\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}.\end{split}

By Plancherel’s identity, we have

(4.9)

\displaystyle\big\|\langle t\rangle^{a}\mathcal{C}^{0}_{N,R,L}u\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\lesssim\|u\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}+\Big\|\phi\Big(\frac{n}{N}\Big)\big\|(-\partial^{2}_{\tau})^{\frac{a}{2}}\{m^{0}_{R,L}(\cdot,n)\widehat{u}(\cdot,n)\}(\tau)\big\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}\Big\|_{\ell^{2}_{n}}.

Thus, since
\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1
, (
4.8
) follows from (
4.9
) and the following estimates:

(4.10)

\displaystyle\big\|(-\partial^{2}_{\tau})^{\frac{a}{2}}\{m^{0}_{R,L}(\cdot,n)\widehat{u}(\cdot,n)\}(\tau)\big\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}\lesssim\|\widehat{u}(\tau,n)\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}+\big\|(-\partial^{2}_{\tau})^{\frac{a}{2}}\widehat{u}(\cdot,n)\big\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}

for
L\gtrsim 1
and

(4.11)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|(-\partial^{2}_{\tau})^{\frac{a}{2}}\{m^{0}_{R,L}(\cdot,n)\widehat{u}(\cdot,n)\}(\tau)\big\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}&\lesssim L^{-(1-\delta_{\circ})(a+\frac{\delta_{\circ}}{10})}\cdot\|\widehat{u}(\tau,n)\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}+\big\|(-\partial^{2}_{\tau})^{\frac{a}{2}}\widehat{u}(\cdot,n)\big\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}\\
&\qquad+L^{-a(1-\delta_{\circ})-\delta_{\circ}}\cdot\|\mathbf{1}_{||\tau|-|n||\lesssim L^{1-\delta_{\circ}}}\,\widehat{u}(\tau,n)\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}\end{split}

for
L\ll 1
and any small enough
\delta_{\circ}>0
.

From (
2.23
), we have

(4.12)

\displaystyle(-\partial^{2}_{\tau})^{\frac{a}{2}}\{m^{0}_{R,L}(\cdot,n)\widehat{u}(\cdot,n)\}(\tau)=\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt(\tau)+\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}(\tau),

where

\displaystyle\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt(\tau)

\displaystyle=c_{a}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{m^{0}_{R,L}(\tau+h,n)-m^{0}_{R,L}(\tau,n)}{|h|^{1+a}}\widehat{u}(\tau+h,n)dh,

\displaystyle\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}(\tau)

\displaystyle=c_{a}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\frac{\widehat{u}(\tau+h,n)-\widehat{u}(\tau,n)}{|h|^{1+a}}dh\cdot m^{0}_{R,L}(\tau,n).

We immediately note the estimate:

(4.13)

\displaystyle\|\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}\lesssim\big\|(-\partial^{2}_{\tau})^{\frac{a}{2}}\widehat{u}(\cdot,n)\big\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}},

since
\|m^{0}_{R,L}(\tau,n)\|_{L^{\infty}_{\tau}}\lesssim 1
. Next, we bound the
L^{2}_{\tau}
-norm of the term
\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt(\tau)
in different regimes of the integrand parameter
h
, depending on the size of
L
.

\bullet
Case 1:
L\gtrsim 1
.
If
|h|\gtrsim 1
on the integrand of
\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt(\tau)
, then by Minkowski’s inequality, we have

(4.14)

\displaystyle\|\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}\lesssim\|\widehat{u}(\tau,n)\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}},

since
\|m^{0}_{R,L}(\tau,n)\|_{L^{\infty}_{\tau}}\lesssim 1
and
a>0
. By the mean value theorem, the smoothness of
m^{0}_{R,L}
and (
4.5
), we have

\displaystyle|m^{0}_{R,L}(\tau+h,n)-m^{0}_{R,L}(\tau,n)|=|h|\cdot\Big|\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{\tau}m^{0}_{R,L}(\tau+sh,n)ds\Big|\lesssim|h|.

Therefore, if
|h|\lesssim 1
on the integrand of
\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt(\tau)
, we deduce that

(4.15)

\displaystyle\|\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}\lesssim\|\widehat{u}(\tau,n)\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}},

since
a<1
.

\bullet
Case 2:
L\ll 1
.
If
|h|\gtrsim 1
on the integrand of
\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt(\tau)
then the bound (
4.14
) holds. Otherwise, fix
0<\delta_{\circ}\ll 1
. In the case where
L^{1-\delta_{\circ}}\lesssim|h|\ll 1
holds on the integrand of
\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt(\tau)
, we use the bound

|h|^{-1-a}\lesssim L^{-(1-\delta_{\circ})(a+\frac{\delta_{\circ}}{10})}\cdot|h|^{-1+\frac{\delta_{\circ}}{10}}

and Minkowski’s inequality to get

(4.16)

\displaystyle\|\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}\lesssim L^{-(1-\delta_{\circ})(a+\frac{\delta_{\circ}}{10})}\cdot\|\widehat{u}(\tau,n)\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}.

Otherwise, we have
|h|\ll L^{1-\delta_{\circ}}\ll 1
on the integrand of
\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt(\tau)
. From the mean value theorem and (
4.5
), we have

(4.17)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|m^{0}_{R,L}(\tau+h,n)-m^{0}_{R,L}(\tau,n)|&=|h|\cdot\Big|\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{\tau}m^{0}_{R,L}(\tau+sh,n)ds\Big|\\
&\lesssim L^{-1}|h|\int_{0}^{1}\mathbf{1}_{||\tau+sh|-|n||\sim L}ds.\end{split}

Let
s\in[0,1]
. Note that in view of the estimate

\big|||\tau+sh|-|n|| –||\tau|-|n||\big|\lesssim|h|\lesssim L^{1-\delta_{\circ}},

we deduce the bound

(4.18)

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{||\tau+sh|-|n||\sim L}\lesssim\mathbf{1}_{||\tau|-|n||\lesssim L^{1-\delta_{\circ}}}.

Hence, from (
4.17
) and (
4.18
), we get

(4.19)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt(\tau)\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}&\lesssim L^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{R}}|h|^{-a}\mathbf{1}_{|h|\lesssim L^{1-\delta_{\circ}}}dh\cdot\|\mathbf{1}_{||\tau|-|n||\lesssim L^{1-\delta_{\circ}}}\,\widehat{u}(\tau,n)\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}\\
&\lesssim L^{-a(1-\delta_{\circ})-\delta_{\circ}}\cdot\|\mathbf{1}_{||\tau|-|n||\lesssim L^{1-\delta_{\circ}}}\,\widehat{u}(\tau,n)\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}.\end{split}

∎

We conclude this section with weighted variants of Bernstein-type inequalities; see
[
92
, Appendix A]
.

Lemma 4.7
.

Fix
R\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\mathbf{T}_{R}
be as in (
2.39
). Fix
1\leq p,q,r\leq\infty
with
p\leq q
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
. Then, the following estimates holds:

(4.20)

\displaystyle\|\langle t\rangle\mathbf{T}_{R}f\|_{L^{q}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim R^{\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q}}\|\langle t\rangle f\|_{L^{p}_{t}},

(4.21)

\displaystyle R^{s}\|\langle t\rangle\mathbf{T}_{R}f\|_{L^{r}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{W_{t}^{s,r}}+\|t\cdot f\|_{W^{s,r}_{t}}.

Proof.

Let us first prove (
4.20
). We note that

(4.22)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{t}[t\cdot\mathbf{T}_{R}f](\tau)=i\partial_{\tau}\Big\{\eta\Big(\frac{\tau}{R}\Big)\widehat{f}(\tau)\Big\}=iR^{-1}(\partial_{\tau}\eta)\Big(\frac{\tau}{R}\Big)\widehat{f}(\tau)+\eta\Big(\frac{\tau}{R}\Big)\big(i\partial_{\tau}\widehat{f}\,\big)(\tau).

Thereofore, denoting by
\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}_{R}
the multiplier defined as
\mathbf{T}_{R}
, but with
\eta
replaced with
\partial_{\tau}\eta
, from (
4.22
) and the standard Bernstein inequality, we have that

\displaystyle\|\langle t\rangle\mathbf{T}_{R}f\|_{L^{q}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathbf{T}_{R}f\|_{L^{q}_{t}}+\|t\cdot\mathbf{T}_{R}f\|_{L^{q}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathbf{T}_{R}f\|_{L^{q}_{t}}+R^{-1}\big\|\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}_{R}f\big\|_{L^{q}_{t}}+\|\mathbf{T}_{R}(t\cdot f)\|_{L^{q}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim R^{\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q}}\|\langle t\rangle f\|_{L^{p}_{t}}.

As for (
4.21
), we use a dyadic decomposition as follows:

(4.23)

\displaystyle\|t\cdot\mathbf{T}_{R}f\|_{L^{r}_{t}}\leq\sum_{R_{0}\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}}}\|\mathbf{T}_{R_{0}}(t\cdot\mathbf{T}_{R}f)\|_{L^{r}_{t}}.

For any fixed
R_{0}\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}}
, we have

(4.24)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{F}_{t}\big[\mathbf{T}_{R_{0}}(t\cdot\mathbf{T}_{R}f)\big](\tau)&=\eta\Big(\frac{\tau}{R_{0}}\Big)\cdot i\partial_{\tau}\Big\{\eta\Big(\frac{\tau}{R}\Big)\widehat{f}(\tau)\Big\}\\
&=iR^{-1}\eta\Big(\frac{\tau}{R_{0}}\Big)(\partial_{\tau}\eta)\Big(\frac{\tau}{R}\Big)\widehat{f}(\tau)+\eta\Big(\frac{\tau}{R_{0}}\Big)\eta\Big(\frac{\tau}{R}\Big)\big(i\partial_{\tau}\widehat{f}\,\big)(\tau).\end{split}

Therefore,
\mathbf{T}_{R_{0}}(t\cdot\mathbf{T}_{R}f)\neq 0
if and only if
R\sim R_{0}
. Hence, from (
4.23
), (
4.24
) and using the notation
\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}_{R}
as in the above, we have

\displaystyle R^{s}\|\langle t\rangle\mathbf{T}_{R}f\|_{L^{r}_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq R^{s}\|\mathbf{T}_{R}f\|_{L^{r}_{t}}+R^{s}\|t\cdot\mathbf{T}_{R}f\|_{L^{r}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{W^{s,r}_{t}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R_{0}\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}}\\
R_{0}\sim R\end{subarray}}R_{0}^{s}\,\|\mathbf{T}_{R_{0}}(t\cdot\mathbf{T}_{R}f)\|_{L^{r}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{W^{s,r}_{t}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}R_{0}\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}}\\
R_{0}\sim R\end{subarray}}R^{-1}R_{0}^{s}\,\big\|\mathbf{T}_{R_{0}}\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}_{R}f\big\|_{L^{r}_{t}}+R_{0}^{s}\,\|\mathbf{T}_{R_{0}}\mathbf{T}_{R}(t\cdot f)\|_{L^{r}_{t}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|f\|_{W_{t}^{s,r}}+\|t\cdot f\|_{W^{s,r}_{t}},

as claimed. This finishes the proof.
∎

4.3.
Bilinear estimates

In this section, we establish key bilinear estimates for products of the from
\lambda(t)uv
, where
\lambda
is a smooth bump function of the time variable and
u
and
v
are space-time functions. Roughly speaking, our strategy is to estimate this product in different space-time regions: either
(\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt)
close or
(\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I})
far away from the light cone
\{(\tau,n)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}^{2}:|\tau|=|n|\}
, where
\tau
and
n
are respectively the output time and spatial frequencies of
\lambda(t)uv
. Whilst the analysis of
(\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt)
and
(\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I})
are markedly different as we use different norms for these regions, the main goal in both cases is to place
u
in a space of low integrability which satisfies the fractional chain rule and
v
in a Fourier restriction norm space.

In the next result, we deal with product estimates in the space-time region close to the light cone
{(\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt):\{(\tau,n)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}^{2}=|\tau|\sim|n|\}}
.

Proposition 4.8
.

Let
0<\alpha<\frac{3\sqrt{241}-41}{244}

and

\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}
,

\mathcal{P}^{>}_{\gamma}
and
\mathcal{P}^{<}_{\gamma}
be
as in (
2.43
), (
2.44
) and (
2.45
), respectively. Fix
\lambda\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R})
.
Then, there exists
0<\gamma<1
and small
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(\alpha)>0

such that, with
\delta=\alpha+10\varepsilon
,
\delta_{1}=\alpha+5\varepsilon
and
\delta_{2}=\alpha+15\varepsilon
, we have

(4.25)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}\mathcal{P}^{>}_{\gamma}(\lambda(t)u,v)\|_{L^{1}_{t}W_{x}^{\alpha+\frac{1}{2},1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|u\|_{\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{1},0}_{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})}}}\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}},

(4.26)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{A}\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}\mathcal{P}^{<}_{\gamma}(\lambda(t)u,v)\|_{Y_{\frac{1}{2}+3\varepsilon}^{\alpha,\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\|u\|_{\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{1},0}_{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})}}}+\|u\|_{\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\frac{3}{2+\delta_{2}}}}\big)\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}

for any
\mathcal{A}\in\{\mathrm{Id},\mathcal{C},\mathcal{H}\mathcal{C}\}
,
where

\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{C}
are as in
(
2.30
) and (
2.31
), respectively. Here, the implicit constants may depend on the bump function
\lambda
.

Before proceeding to the proof of Proposition
4.8
,
we first recall the following hyperbolic Leibniz rule;
see
[
22
, Subsection 4.2]
. See also
[
58
, Proof of Lemma 3.4]
.

Lemma 4.9

(hyperbolic Leibniz rule)
.

Let
\tau,\tau_{1},\tau_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\xi,\xi_{1},\xi_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
such that
\tau=\tau_{1}+\tau_{2}
and
\xi=\xi_{1}+\xi_{2}
. Let
\pm_{1}
and
\pm_{2}
be the signs of
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\big||\tau|-|\xi|\big|\lesssim\big|-\tau_{1}\pm_{1}|\xi_{1}|\big|+\big|-\tau_{2}\pm_{2}|\xi_{2}|\big|+b_{\pm_{1},\pm_{2}}(\xi,\xi_{1},\xi_{2}),

where

\displaystyle|b_{\pm_{1},\pm_{2}}(\xi,\xi_{1},\xi_{2})|\lesssim\min(|\xi_{1}|,|\xi_{2}|).

In order to prove Proposition
4.8
, we consider bounds of the form (
4.25
) and (
4.26
) in the two next lemmas. First, we find a range of
\gamma
for which the estimate (
4.25
) holds (for fixed
\alpha
).

Lemma 4.10
.

Let
0<\alpha<\frac{1}{4}
and
\gamma>0
such that

(4.27)

\displaystyle\gamma>\frac{12\alpha}{1+14\alpha}.

Let

\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}
and

\mathcal{P}^{>}_{\gamma}
be
as in (
2.43
) and (
2.44
), respectively. Fix
\lambda\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R})
.
Then, given small
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(\alpha,\gamma)>0
satisfying

(4.28)

\displaystyle\delta:=\alpha+10\varepsilon<\frac{1}{4}\qquad\text{and}\qquad\gamma>\frac{12\alpha+60\varepsilon}{1+14\alpha+140\varepsilon},

we have

(4.29)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}\mathcal{P}^{>}_{\gamma}(\lambda(t)u,v)\|_{L^{1}_{t}W_{x}^{\alpha+\frac{1}{2},1}}\lesssim\|u\|_{\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{1},0}_{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})}}}\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}},

where
\delta_{1}=\alpha+5\varepsilon
.

Proof.

By a dyadic decomposition together with (
2.43
), we have

(4.30)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}\mathcal{P}_{\gamma}^{>}(\tilde{u},v)&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(N,R)\in(2^{\mathbb{Z}})^{2}\\
R\sim N\geq 1\end{subarray}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N_{1},N_{2}\in(2^{\mathbb{Z}})^{2}\\
N_{1}\geq N_{2}^{\gamma}\end{subarray}}\mathbf{P}_{N}\mathbf{T}_{R}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\tilde{u}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}v),\end{split}

where
\tilde{u}=\lambda(t)u
.
Thus, it suffices to prove that there exists
\theta>0
such that

(4.31)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}\mathbf{T}_{R}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\tilde{u}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}v)\|_{L^{1}_{t}W_{x}^{\alpha+\frac{1}{2},1}}\lesssim N_{1}^{-\theta}\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{1},0}_{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})}}}\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}\end{split}

for any
N_{1},R_{1},N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}\geq 1
with
N_{1}\geq N_{2}^{\gamma}
and

N\sim R\gtrsim\max(L,1)
,
where
\delta
and
\gamma
satisfy (
4.28
).
Indeed,
the conditions

N\sim R

and
N_{1}\geq N_{2}^{\gamma}
,
the decaying factor

N_{1}^{-\theta}

in (
4.31
) allows
us to sum over dyadic numbers

N
,
R
,
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
.
Hence, the desired bound (
4.29
) follows
from (
4.31
), (
4.30
) and Lemma
2.12
(i) (in order to remove the function
\lambda(t)
on the right-hand-side of (
4.31
)).

\bullet
Case 1:

N_{2}\leq N_{1}
.
By the bound
N\lesssim N_{1}
, Hölder’s and
Sobolev’s inequalities (with
\delta<\frac{1}{4}
)
and
(\delta,\delta_{1})=(\alpha+10\varepsilon,\alpha+5\varepsilon)
,
we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{N}\mathbf{T}_{R}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\tilde{u}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}v)\|_{L^{1}_{t}W_{x}^{\alpha+\frac{1}{2},1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{\alpha+\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\tilde{u}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}v\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{1}^{\alpha+\frac{1}{2}}N_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\delta}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{1},0}_{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}v\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{3}{1+2\delta}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{1}^{\alpha-\delta}\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{1},0}_{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})}}}\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1-4\delta}{3},\frac{1-4\delta}{6}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{1}^{-5\varepsilon}\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{1},0}_{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})}}}\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}},

since

\frac{1-4\delta}{3}\leq\frac{1}{2}-\delta

and
\frac{1-4\delta}{6}\leq\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon
.

\bullet
Case 2:

N_{2}^{\gamma}\leq N_{1}\leq N_{2}
.
Proceeding as in Case 1 with
N\lesssim N_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{N}\mathbf{T}_{R}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\tilde{u}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}v)\|_{L^{1}_{t}W_{x}^{\alpha+\frac{1}{2},1}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{\alpha+\frac{1}{2}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\tilde{u}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}v\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{2}^{\alpha+\frac{1}{2}}N_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\delta_{1}}N_{2}^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})}}_{t}H_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{1}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}v\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{3}{1+2\delta}}H^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta}_{x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\delta_{1}+\frac{1-4\delta_{1}}{3}}N_{2}^{\alpha+\delta}\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{1},0}_{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})}}}\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{1}^{-\theta}\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{1},0}_{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})}}}\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}

for some small
\theta>0
, provided that

\displaystyle\gamma>\frac{12\alpha+60\varepsilon}{1+14\alpha+140\varepsilon}.

This concludes the proof of Lemma
4.10
.
∎

Next, we prove that bounds of the form (
4.25
) hold for some range of parameters
\gamma
(for fixed
\alpha
).

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
0<\alpha<\frac{1}{4}
and
0<\gamma<1
such that

(4.32)

\displaystyle\gamma<\min\bigg(\frac{1-4\alpha}{3-6\alpha},\frac{1-7\alpha}{4+2\alpha}\bigg).

Let

\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}
and

\mathcal{P}^{<}_{\gamma}
be
as in (
2.43
) and (
2.45
), respectively. Fix
\lambda\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R})
.
Then, given small
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(\alpha,\gamma)>0
satisfying

(4.33)

\displaystyle\delta:=\alpha+10\varepsilon<\frac{1}{4}\quad\text{and}\quad\gamma<\min\bigg(\frac{1-4\alpha-20\varepsilon}{3-6\alpha-54\varepsilon},\frac{1-7\alpha-58\varepsilon}{4+2\alpha+20\varepsilon}\bigg),

we have

(4.34)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{A}\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}\mathcal{P}^{<}_{\gamma}(\lambda(t)u,v)\|_{Y_{\frac{1}{2}+3\varepsilon}^{\alpha,\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}}\lesssim\big(\|u\|_{\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{1},0}_{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})}}}+\|u\|_{\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\frac{3}{2+\delta_{2}}}}\big)\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}},

where
\delta_{1}=\alpha+5\varepsilon
,
\delta_{2}=\alpha+15\varepsilon
