and for any
\mathcal{A}\in\{\mathrm{Id},\mathcal{C},\mathcal{H}\mathcal{C}\}
,
where

\mathcal{H}
and
\mathcal{C}
are as in
(
2.30
) and (
2.31
), respectively.

Remark 4.12
.

We note that bilinear estimates in
X^{s,b}
type spaces (which are closely related to the norm on the left-hand-side of (
4.34
)) have appeared in the literature on dispersive PDEs; see
[
36
,
57
,
21
,
22
]
. They however do not seem to be helpful in the context at hand, since they place both input functions
u
and
v
in
L^{2}
-based spaces, while we wish to put
u
in a space of low integrability which satisfies a fractional chain rule.

Proof.

By a dyadic decomposition together with (
2.40
) and (
2.43
), we have

(4.35)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{A}\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}\mathcal{P}_{\gamma}^{<}(\tilde{u},v)&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(N,R,L)\in(2^{\mathbb{Z}})^{3}\\
R\sim N\geq 1\end{subarray}}\sum_{(N_{1},R_{1})\in(2^{\mathbb{Z}})^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(N_{2},R_{2},L_{2})\in(2^{\mathbb{Z}})^{3}\\
N_{1}<N_{2}^{\gamma}\end{subarray}}\\
&\hphantom{XXXXXX}\mathcal{A}\mathbf{M}_{N,R,L}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}\tilde{u}\cdot\mathbf{M}_{N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}}v),\end{split}

where
\tilde{u}=\lambda(t)u
. Note that by the triangle inequality with
N\sim R
, we have
N\sim R\gtrsim L
.
In the following,
we only consider the contributions
to (
4.35
)
from

N_{1},N_{2},R_{1},R_{2},L_{2}\geq 1
.
When
\min(N_{1},N_{2},R_{1},R_{2},L_{2})<1
, we first sum over dyadic
N_{1},N_{2},R_{1},R_{2},L_{2}<1
(on the right-hand-side of (
4.35
))
and apply the argument presented below.
(Namely, the homogeneous dyadic decompositions
in
N_{1},N_{2},R_{1},R_{2},L_{2}<1
are not needed.) We however, point out that
the cases
L\leq 1
and
L>1
need to be treated separately.

It follows from
(
2.47
),
(
4.2
) and (
4.6
)
that

(4.36)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{A}\mathbf{M}_{N,R,L}w\|_{Y_{\frac{1}{2}+3\varepsilon}^{\alpha,\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}}=\|\mathcal{A}\mathcal{C}_{N,R,L}^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}w\|_{Y_{\frac{1}{2}+3\varepsilon}^{\alpha,0}}.

We now claim that the desired bound (
4.34
) follows if we prove
that there exists
\theta>0
such that

(4.37)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big\|\mathcal{A}\mathcal{C}^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}_{N,R,L}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}\tilde{u}\cdot\mathbf{M}_{N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}}v)\big\|_{Y_{\frac{1}{2}+3\varepsilon}^{\alpha,0}}\\
&\quad\lesssim\min(L,1)^{\theta}N_{2}^{-\theta}L_{2}^{-\theta}\big(\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{1},0}_{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})}}}+\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\frac{3}{2+\delta_{2}}}}\big)\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}\end{split}

for any
N,R,N_{1},R_{1},N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}\geq 1
with
N_{1}<N_{2}^{\gamma}
and

N\sim R\gtrsim\max(L,1)
and
\delta
and
\gamma
satisfy (
4.33
). Indeed,
the conditions

N\sim R\gtrsim\max(L,1)

and
N_{1}<N_{2}^{\gamma}
, the triangle inequality

(4.38)

\displaystyle R_{2}\lesssim N_{2}+L_{2},

and the decaying factor

\min(L,1)^{\theta}N_{1}^{-\theta}L_{2}^{-\theta}

in (
4.37
) allows
us to sum over dyadic numbers

N
,
R
,
L
,
N_{1}
,
N_{2}
,
R_{2}
, and
L_{2}
.
As for the summation in
R_{1}
, we
split it into two parts.
When

R_{1}\lesssim R_{2}
,
we use a small negative power of
R_{2}

to sum over
R_{1}
.
Otherwise, we have

R_{2}\ll R_{1}\sim R\sim N\lesssim N_{2}

and
thus we use a small negative power of
N_{2}

to sum over
R_{1}
.
Hence, the desired bound (
4.34
) follows
from (
4.37
)
under the assumption that

N_{1},N_{2},R_{1},R_{2},L_{2}\geq 1
and Lemma
2.12
(in order to remove the function
\lambda(t)
on the right-hand-side of (
4.37
)).

We note that the right-hand-side of (
4.37
) is given by
L^{p}_{t,x}
-based norms (for
1<p<\infty
) of
\tilde{u}
and
v
. Thus, in view of the boundedness of the temporal Hilbert transform
\mathcal{H}
in (
2.30
), we may assume that the temporal frequencies
\tau_{1}
and
\tau_{2}
of
\tilde{u}
and
v
are signed (such that Lemma
4.9
is applicable).

\bullet
Case 1:
L\geq 1
.
Using Lemma
4.6
with the parameters
(a,b)=(\frac{1}{2}+3\varepsilon,\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon)
and
\delta_{\circ}\ll 1
, the bound (
4.7
) simply reads

(4.39)

\displaystyle\big\|\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}+3\varepsilon}\mathcal{C}^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}_{N,R,L}w\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim L^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}\|\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}+3\varepsilon}w\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

in this case. Since
\gamma<1
, we have
N_{1}<N_{2}^{\gamma}\leq N_{2}
.
Then,
by (
4.39
) and Lemma
4.9

with
L\lesssim L_{1}+L_{2}+N_{1}
and
L_{1}\leq N_{1}+R_{1}
,
we have

(4.40)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big\|\mathcal{A}\mathcal{C}^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}_{N,R,L}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}\tilde{u}\cdot\mathbf{M}_{N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}}v)\big\|_{Y^{\alpha,0}_{\frac{1}{2}+3\varepsilon}}\\
&\quad\lesssim N^{\alpha}L^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}\big\|\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}+3\varepsilon}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}\tilde{u}\cdot\mathbf{M}_{N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}}v)\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\\
&\quad\lesssim N_{2}^{\alpha}L_{\max}^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}\|\langle t\rangle(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}\tilde{u}\cdot\mathbf{M}_{N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}}v)\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}},\end{split}

where
L_{\max}
is given by

L_{\max}=\max(N_{1},R_{1})+L_{2}.

\bullet
Subcase 1.1:

L_{\max}\lesssim N_{1}
.
By Hölder’s, Sobolev’s and Bernstein’s inequalities in space and time, Minkowski’s inequality and (
4.20
) in Lemma
4.7
,
with the conditions
N_{1}<N_{2}^{\gamma}
,
R_{1}\lesssim N_{1}
and
(\delta,\delta_{1})=(\alpha+10\varepsilon,\alpha+5\varepsilon)
, we have

(4.41)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\text{RHS of }\eqref{z4}\lesssim N_{2}^{\alpha}N_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}(\langle t\rangle\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}\tilde{u})\|_{L^{\frac{2}{1-4\varepsilon}}_{t}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{M}_{N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}}v\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{1}{2\varepsilon}}L^{2}_{x}}\\
&\quad\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{4(1-\delta)}{3}-\delta_{1}+\varepsilon}N_{2}^{\alpha+\delta-\frac{1}{2}}\|\langle t\rangle\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}\langle\nabla_{x}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{1}}\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\frac{2}{1-4\varepsilon}}_{t}L_{x}^{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})}}}\|\mathbf{M}_{N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}}v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-2\varepsilon}}\\
&\quad\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{4-7\delta_{1}}{3}+\varepsilon}N_{2}^{\alpha+\delta-\frac{1}{2}}L_{2}^{-\varepsilon}\|\langle t\rangle\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}\langle\nabla_{x}\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{1}}\tilde{u}\|_{L_{x}^{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})}}L^{\frac{2}{1-4\varepsilon}}_{t}}\|\mathbf{M}_{N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}}v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}\\
&\quad\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{4-7\delta_{1}}{3}+\varepsilon}N_{2}^{\alpha+\delta-\frac{1}{2}}R_{1}^{\frac{1-4\delta_{1}+12\varepsilon}{6}}L_{2}^{-\varepsilon}\|\langle t\rangle\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{1},0}_{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})}}}\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}\\
&\quad\lesssim N_{2}^{\alpha+\delta-\frac{1}{2}+\gamma\frac{3-6\alpha-54\varepsilon}{2}}L_{2}^{-\varepsilon}\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{1},0}_{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})}}}\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}\\
&\quad\lesssim N_{2}^{-\theta}L_{2}^{-\varepsilon}\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{1},0}_{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})}}}\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}\end{split}

for some small
\theta>0
, provided that
\alpha<\frac{1}{2}
and

(4.42)

\displaystyle\gamma<\frac{1-4\alpha-20\varepsilon}{3-6\alpha-54\varepsilon}

and
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(\alpha,\gamma)>0
is sufficiently small. We used the condition
\delta_{1}<\frac{1}{4}
in order to apply Minkowski’s inequality in going from the second to the third line of (
4.41
) and took advantage of the time localization of
\tilde{u}
to remove the weight
\langle t\rangle
in the second to last line of (
4.41
).

\bullet
Subcase 1.2:

L_{\max}\lesssim R_{1}
.
Let
\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}_{R}
be defined as in
\mathbf{T}_{R}
in (
2.39
), but with a symbol whose support is slightly larger than
\eta(\cdot/R)
so that
\mathcal{C}^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}_{N,R,L}\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}_{R}\equiv\mathcal{C}^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}_{N,R,L}
. Then, from (
4.39
), (
4.20
) in Lemma
4.7
, Lemma
4.9
and Bernstein’s and Minkowski’s inequalities, with
L\lesssim L_{\max}\lesssim R_{1}
and
R\sim N\lesssim N_{2}
, we have

(4.43)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big\|\mathcal{A}\mathcal{C}^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}_{N,R,L}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}\tilde{u}\cdot\mathbf{M}_{N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}}v)\big\|_{Y^{\alpha,0}_{\frac{1}{2}+3\varepsilon}}\\
&\qquad=\big\|\mathcal{A}\mathcal{C}^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}_{N,R,L}\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}_{R}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}\tilde{u}\cdot\mathbf{M}_{N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}}v)\big\|_{Y^{\alpha,0}_{\frac{1}{2}+3\varepsilon}}\\
&\qquad\lesssim N^{\alpha}L^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}\big\|\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}+3\varepsilon}\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}_{R}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}\tilde{u}\cdot\mathbf{M}_{N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}}v)\big\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{t}}\\
&\qquad\lesssim N_{2}^{\alpha}R_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}R^{\frac{1+2\delta_{2}+24\varepsilon}{6}}\|\langle t\rangle(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}\tilde{u}\cdot\mathbf{M}_{N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}}v)\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\frac{3}{2+\delta_{2}+12\varepsilon}}_{t}}\\
&\qquad\lesssim N_{2}^{\alpha+\frac{1+2\delta_{2}+24\varepsilon}{6}}R_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}\|\langle t\rangle(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}\tilde{u}\cdot\mathbf{M}_{N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}}v)\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2+\delta_{2}+12\varepsilon}}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

Note that we have
R_{1}\lesssim\max(N_{2},R_{2})
. Indeed, otherwise, we would have
R\sim R_{1}\gg\max(N_{2},R_{2})\gtrsim N
, which is
a contradiction to
N\sim R
under the projector
\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}
. Therfore, we have
R_{1}\lesssim\max(N_{2},L_{2})
since
R_{2}\lesssim\max(N_{2},L_{2})
. Next, by Hölder’s, Sobolev’s and Bernstein’s inequalities, (
4.21
) in Lemma
4.7
, with
N_{1}<N_{2}^{\gamma}
,
R_{1}^{2\varepsilon}\lesssim N_{2}^{2\varepsilon}\cdot L_{2}^{2\varepsilon}
and
(\delta,\delta_{2})=(\alpha+10\varepsilon,\alpha+15\varepsilon)
, we have

(4.44)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\text{RHS of }\eqref{Ysgcond1}\lesssim N_{2}^{\alpha+\frac{1+2\delta_{2}+24\varepsilon}{6}}R_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}\|\langle t\rangle\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}\tilde{u}\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{3}{2+\delta_{2}}}L^{\infty}_{x}}\|\mathbf{M}_{N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}}v\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{1}{4\varepsilon}}L^{2}_{x}}\\
&\quad\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{4+2\delta_{2}}{3}}N_{2}^{\alpha+\frac{1+2\delta_{2}+24\varepsilon}{6}+\delta_{2}-\frac{1}{2}}L_{2}^{-3\varepsilon}R_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}\|\langle t\rangle\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}\tilde{u}\|_{L_{x}^{\frac{3}{2+\delta_{2}}}L_{t}^{\frac{3}{2+\delta_{2}}}}\|\mathbf{M}_{N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}}v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}\\
&\quad\lesssim N_{2}^{\gamma\frac{4+2\delta_{2}}{3}+\frac{-1+7\alpha+58\varepsilon}{3}}L_{2}^{-\varepsilon}\big(\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\frac{3}{2+\delta_{2}}}}+\|t\cdot\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\frac{3}{2+\delta_{2}}}}\big)\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}\\
&\quad\lesssim N_{2}^{-\theta}L_{2}^{-\varepsilon}\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\frac{3}{2+\delta_{2}}}}\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}\end{split}

for some small
\theta>0
,
provided that

(4.45)

\displaystyle\gamma<\frac{1-7\alpha-58\varepsilon}{4+2\alpha+20\varepsilon}

and
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(\alpha,\gamma)>0
is sufficiently small. Note that in (
4.44
), we used (
2.62
) in Lemma
2.12
to bound

(4.46)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|t\cdot\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\frac{3}{2+\delta_{2}}}}&=\|(t\cdot\tilde{\lambda}(t))\lambda(t)u\|_{\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\frac{3}{2+\delta_{2}}}}\\
&\lesssim\|\lambda(t)u\|_{\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\frac{3}{2+\delta_{2}}}}\\
&\lesssim\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\frac{3}{2+\delta_{2}}}},\end{split}

where
\tilde{\lambda}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R})
equals
1
on the support of
\lambda
.

\bullet
Subcase 1.3:

\max(N_{1},R_{1})\ll L_{\max}\sim L_{2}
.
Note that we have
L_{2}\lesssim N_{2}
in this case.
Indeed, otherwise,
we would have
L_{2}\sim R_{2}\gg\max(N_{2},R_{1})
.
This would in turn imply
R\sim R_{2}\gg N_{2}\sim N
, which is
a contradiction to
N\sim R
under the projector
\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}
. Let
\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}_{R_{1}}
be defined as in
\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}
in (

), but with a symbol whose support is slightly larger than
\eta(\cdot/R_{1})
so that
\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}_{R_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}\equiv\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}
.
By Hölder’s and Sobolev’s inequalities, (
4.20
) and (
4.21
) in Lemma
4.7
and (
4.46
), with
R_{1}\ll L_{2}\lesssim N_{2}
,

N_{1}<N_{2}^{\gamma}
and
(\delta,\delta_{2})=(\alpha+10\varepsilon,\alpha+15\varepsilon)
, we have

\displaystyle\text{RHS of }\eqref{z4}\lesssim N_{2}^{\alpha}L_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}\|\langle t\rangle\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{M}_{N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}}v\|_{L_{t,x}^{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{4+2\delta_{2}}{3}}N_{2}^{\alpha+\delta-\frac{1}{2}+3\varepsilon}L_{2}^{-\varepsilon}\|\langle t\rangle\widetilde{\mathbf{T}}_{R_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}L^{\frac{3}{2+\delta_{2}}}_{x}}\|\mathbf{M}_{N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}}v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{4+2\delta_{2}}{3}}N_{2}^{\alpha+\delta-\frac{1}{2}+3\varepsilon}L_{2}^{-\varepsilon}R_{1}^{\frac{1+2\delta_{2}+6\varepsilon}{6}}\cdot R_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\|\langle t\rangle\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}\tilde{u}\|_{L^{\frac{3}{2+\delta_{2}}}_{x}L^{\frac{3}{2+\delta_{2}}}_{t}}\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim N_{2}^{\gamma\frac{4+2\delta_{2}}{3}+\frac{-1+7\alpha+52\varepsilon}{3}}L_{2}^{-\varepsilon}\big(\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\frac{3}{2+\delta_{2}}}}+\|t\cdot\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\frac{3}{2+\delta_{2}}}}\big)\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim N_{2}^{-\theta}L_{2}^{-\varepsilon}\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\frac{3}{2+\delta_{2}}}}\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}

for some small
\theta>0
,
provided that

(4.47)

\displaystyle\gamma<\frac{1-7\alpha-52\varepsilon}{4+2\alpha+20\varepsilon},

and
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(\alpha,\gamma)>0
is sufficiently small.

\bullet
Case 2:
N_{2}^{-100}\leq L<1
.
Using Lemma
4.6
with the parameters

) reads

Therefore, since (

) but without the factor

, by arguing as in Case 1 (or (

) without the factor

), we have

\displaystyle\big\|\mathcal{A}\mathcal{C}^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}_{N,R,L}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}\tilde{u}\cdot\mathbf{M}_{N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}}v)\big\|_{Y^{\alpha,0}_{\frac{1}{2}+3\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\lesssim N_{2}^{-\theta}L^{\theta}L_{2}^{-\varepsilon}\big(\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{1},0}_{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})}}}+\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\frac{3}{2+\delta_{2}}}}\big)\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}.

for some small
\theta>0
, provided that
\gamma
satisfies the conditions (

), (
4.45
), (
4.47
)and
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(\alpha,\gamma)>0
is sufficiently small.

\bullet

L<N_{2}^{-100}
.
Using Lemma
4.6
with the parameters
(a,b,\delta_{\circ})=(\frac{1}{2}+3\varepsilon,\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon,10\varepsilon)
, the bound (

) reads

(4.49)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}+3\varepsilon}\mathcal{C}^{b}_{N,R,L}w\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}&\lesssim L^{\varepsilon}\|\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}+3\varepsilon}w\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}+L^{-20\varepsilon}\big\|\mathcal{F}_{t,x}^{-1}[\mathbf{1}_{||\tau|-|n||\lesssim L^{1-10\varepsilon}}\,\widehat{w}(\tau,n)]\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}.\end{split}

By Plancherel’s identity and the Hausdorff-Young inequality, we also have

(4.50)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|\mathcal{F}_{t,x}^{-1}[\mathbf{1}_{||\tau|-|n||\lesssim L^{1-10\varepsilon}}\,\widehat{w}(\tau,n)]\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}&=\|\mathbf{1}_{||\tau|-|n||\lesssim L^{1-10\varepsilon}}\,\widehat{w}(\tau,n)\|_{\ell^{2}_{n}L^{2}_{\tau}}\\
&\lesssim\big\|\|\mathbf{1}_{||\tau|-|n||\lesssim L^{1-10\varepsilon}}\|_{L^{2}_{\tau}}\|\widehat{w}(\tau,n)\|_{L^{\infty}_{\tau}}\big\|_{\ell^{2}_{n}}\\
&\lesssim L^{\frac{1}{2}-5\varepsilon}\|\mathcal{F}_{x}[w](t,n)\|_{\ell^{2}_{n}L^{1}_{t}}\\
&\lesssim L^{\frac{1}{2}-5\varepsilon}\|w\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}.\end{split}

Combining (
4.49
) and (
4.50
), with
L<N_{2}^{-100}
, yields

(4.51)

\displaystyle\big\|\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}+3\varepsilon}\mathcal{C}^{b}_{N,R,L}w\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}

\displaystyle\lesssim L^{\varepsilon}\|\langle t\rangle^{\frac{1}{2}+3\varepsilon}w\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}+L^{\varepsilon}N_{2}^{-40}\|w\|_{L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x}}.

Therefore, since (
4.51
) is a much better estimate than (
4.39
) or (
4.43
), by arguing as in Case 1, we have

\displaystyle\big\|\mathcal{A}\mathcal{C}^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}_{N,R,L}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}\tilde{u}\cdot\mathbf{M}_{N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}}v)\big\|_{Y^{\alpha,0}_{\frac{1}{2}+3\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad\lesssim N_{2}^{-\theta}L^{\theta}L_{2}^{-\varepsilon}\big(\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{1},0}_{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})}}}+\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\frac{3}{2+\delta_{2}}}}\big)\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}.

for some small
\theta>0
, provided that
\gamma
satisfies the conditions (
4.42
), (
4.45
), (
4.47
)and
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(\alpha,\gamma)>0
is sufficiently small.

Putting Cases 1, 2, and 3 together,
we obtain the desired bound (

),
provided that (
4.42
),
(
4.45
) and (

)
are satisfied.
∎

We now present the proof of Proposition
4.8
. The proof basically consists of working out values the set of values
\alpha>0
for which the two ranges of parameters
\gamma
obtained in Lemmas

and

have a non-empty intersection.

Proof of Proposition
4.8
.

We first note that

(4.52)

\displaystyle\frac{1-7\alpha}{4+2\alpha}<\frac{1-4\alpha}{3-6\alpha}\ \ \text{and}\ \ \alpha>0\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad 0<\alpha<\frac{13+3\sqrt{41}}{100}\approx 0.3221,

Putting together the restrictions on
\alpha

in Lemmas
4.10
and
4.11

with (
4.53
), we have

(4.53)

\displaystyle\frac{12\alpha}{1+14\alpha}<\frac{1-7\alpha}{4+2\alpha}\ \ \text{and}\ \ 0<\alpha<\frac{1}{4}\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad 0<\alpha<\frac{3\sqrt{241}-41}{244}\approx 0.0228.

Hence,
for
0<\alpha<\frac{3\sqrt{241}-41}{244}
, there exists
0<\gamma<1
such that
both the conditions (
4.27
) and (
4.32
) hold,
and thus
Proposition
4.8
follows
as a direct consequence of Lemmas
4.10
and
4.11
.
∎

In the next two propositions, we consider product estimates in the space-time region
{(\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}):\{(\tau,n)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}^{2}=|\tau|\gg|n|\text{ or }|\tau|\ll|n|\}}
. First, we consider the contribution of the region
\{|\tau|\gg|n|\}
to
(\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I})
.

Proposition 4.13
.

Let
0<\alpha<\delta<\frac{1}{16}
. Fix
\lambda\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R})
.
Then, there exists small
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(\alpha,\delta)>0
such that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,lo}}(\lambda(t)uv)\|_{\Lambda^{\alpha+\frac{1}{2},0}_{1+\varepsilon}}\lesssim\Big(\|u\|_{\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta,0}_{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta)}}}+\|u\|_{\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\frac{3}{2+\delta}}}+\|u\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\Big)\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}

for any
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}
,
where

\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,lo}}
is as in (
2.43
). Here, the implicit constant may depend on the bump function
\lambda
.

Proof.

By a dyadic decomposition and Lemma
2.12
(i),
it suffices to show that there exists
\theta>0
such that

(4.54)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{P}_{N}\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,lo}}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\tilde{u}\cdot\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}v)\|_{\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\alpha,0}_{1+\varepsilon}}\\
&\quad\lesssim\max(N_{1},N_{2})^{-\theta}\Big(\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta,0}_{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta)}}}+\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\frac{3}{2+\delta}}}+\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\Big)\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}\end{split}

for any dyadic
N,N_{1},N_{2}\geq 1
and where
\tilde{u}=\lambda(t)u
.
with
N\ll R
.

\bullet
Case 1:

N_{1}\gtrsim N_{2}
.
In this case, we have
N_{1}\gtrsim N
.
Then, by the boundedness of
\mathbf{P}_{N}
and
\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,lo}}
and Hölder’s and Sobolev’s inequalities, we have

LHS of
(
4.54
)

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}+\alpha}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\tilde{u}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta)}}}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{2}}v\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{3(1+\varepsilon)}{1+2\delta-\varepsilon(2-2\delta)}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{1}^{\alpha-\delta}\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta,0}_{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta)}}}\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}},

yielding (
4.54
),
provided that
\alpha<\delta
,
\frac{1-4\delta}{3}<\frac{1}{2}-\delta

and
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(\delta)>0
is sufficiently small.

\bullet
Case 2:

N_{1}\ll N_{2}
.
In this case, we have
N\sim N_{2}
.
By a further dyadic decomposition, it suffices to show that there exists small
\theta>0

such that
21
21

21

Here,
we only consider
R_{1},R_{2},L_{2}\geq 1
for simplicity.
The other cases can be handled in a similar manner;
see the proof of Lemma
4.10
.

(4.55)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&N^{\frac{1}{2}+\alpha}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}\mathbf{T}_{R}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}\tilde{u}\cdot\mathbf{M}_{N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}}v)\|_{L_{t,x}^{1+\varepsilon}}\\
&\quad\lesssim N_{2}^{-\theta}\max(R_{1},R_{2})^{-\theta}\Big(\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\frac{3}{2+\delta}}}+\|u\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\Big)\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}},\end{split}

for any dyadic

such that
{N_{1}\ll N_{2}\sim N}
and
N\ll R
.

\bullet
Subcase 2.1:

R_{1}\gtrsim R_{2}
.
In this case, we have
N_{2}\sim N\ll R\lesssim R_{1}
.
Then, by Hölder’s and Sobolev’s inequalities, we have

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{2}^{\alpha-\delta+2\varepsilon}R_{1}^{-\varepsilon}\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\frac{3}{2+\delta}}}\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}},

),

,

)

is sufficiently small.

\bullet

R_{1}\ll R_{2}
.
In this case, we have

and thus

.

4.55
),
provided that

and

is sufficiently small.

Note that the restriction

.
∎

Lastly, we consider the contribution of the region
\{|\tau|\ll|n|\}
to
(\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I})
.

Proposition 4.14
.

Let
0<\alpha<\delta\leq\frac{1}{16}
. Fix
\lambda\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R})
.
Then, there exists small
\varepsilon_{0}=\varepsilon_{0}(\delta)>0
such that

(4.56)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{lo,hi}}(\lambda(t)uv)\|_{\Lambda^{\alpha,\frac{1}{2}-2\varepsilon}_{1}}\lesssim\Big(\|u\|_{\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta,0}_{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta)}}}+\|u\|_{\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\frac{3}{2+\delta}}}+\|u\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\Big)\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}

for any
0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}
,
where

\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{lo,hi}}
is as in (
2.43
). Here, the implicit constant may depend on the bump function
\lambda
.

Proof.

By a dyadic decomposition as in the proof of Lemma
4.10
and Lemma
2.12
(i),
it suffices to prove
that there exists
\theta>0
such that

(4.57)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|\mathbf{P}_{N}\mathbf{T}_{R}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}\tilde{u}\cdot\mathbf{M}_{N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}}v)\|_{\Lambda^{\alpha,\frac{1}{2}-2\varepsilon}_{1}}\\
&\quad\lesssim N^{\alpha}R^{\frac{1}{2}-2\varepsilon}\|\mathbf{P}_{N}\mathbf{T}_{R}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}\tilde{u}\cdot\mathbf{M}_{N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}}v)\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}\\
&\quad\lesssim\max(N_{1},N_{2})^{-\theta}R_{2}^{-\theta}\Big(\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta,0}_{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta)}}}+\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\frac{3}{2+\delta}}}+\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\Big)\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}\end{split}

for any dyadic
N,R,N_{1},R_{1},N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}\geq 1

such that
R\ll N
and where
\tilde{u}=\lambda(t)u
.
Owing to the decaying factor
\max(N_{1},N_{2})^{-\theta}R_{2}^{-\theta}
,
we can sum over dyadic
N,R,N_{1},N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}\geq 1
.
If

, then we would have
R\sim R_{1}\gg N
,
leading to a contradiction.
Hence, we have

R,R_{1}\lesssim\max(N_{1},N_{2},R_{2})
, allowing us to also sum over dyadic
R_{1}\geq 1
.

\bullet
Case 1:

N_{1}\gtrsim N_{2}
.
In this case, we have
R\ll N\lesssim N_{1}

and
R_{2}\lesssim N_{1}+L_{2}
.
Then,

LHS of
(
4.57
)

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{1}^{\alpha+\frac{1}{2}-2\varepsilon}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}\tilde{u}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta)}}}\|\mathbf{M}_{N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}}v\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{3}{1+2\delta}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{1}^{\alpha-\delta-\varepsilon}R_{2}^{-\varepsilon}\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta,0}_{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta)}}}\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}},

yielding (
4.57
),
provided that

\alpha\leq\delta
,
\frac{1-4\delta}{3}\leq\frac{1}{2}-\delta
,
and
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(\delta)>0
is sufficiently small.

\bullet
Case 2:

N_{1}\ll N_{2}
and
R_{1}\gtrsim R_{2}
.
In this case, we have
R_{1}\gtrsim R

and thus

LHS of
(
4.57
)

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{2}^{\alpha-\delta}R_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}-2\varepsilon}\|\mathbf{P}_{N_{1}}\mathbf{T}_{R_{1}}\tilde{u}\|_{L_{t,x}^{\frac{3}{2+\delta}}}\|\mathbf{M}_{N_{2},R_{2},L_{2}}v\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{3}{1-\delta}}W_{x}^{\delta,\frac{3}{1-\delta}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{2}^{\alpha-\delta}R_{1}^{-\varepsilon}\|\tilde{u}\|_{\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\frac{3}{2+\delta}}}\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\varepsilon}},

yielding (
4.57
),
provided that

\alpha<\delta
,
\frac{1+2\delta}{3}+\delta\leq\frac{1}{2}-\delta

(namely,
\delta\leq\frac{1}{16}
),
and
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(\delta)>0
is sufficiently small.

\bullet
Case
3
:

N_{1}\ll N_{2}
and
R_{1}\ll R_{2}
.
In this case, we have

.
Then, by Hölder’s and Sobolev’s inequalities, we have

)

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{2}^{\alpha-\delta}R_{2}^{-\varepsilon}\|\tilde{u}\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|v\|_{X^{\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}

yielding (
4.57
),
provided that

\alpha<\delta
and

.

5.
Stochastic objects

5.1.
Gibbs measure

Here, we state the result on the construction of the Gibbs measure
\vec{\rho}
(
1.14
) proved in
[
76
]
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Let
0<\beta^{2}<4\pi
.

(i)
The truncated renormalized density
\{R_{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
in (
1.11
)
is a Cauchy sequence in
L^{p}(\mu_{1})
for any finite
p\geq 1
, thus converging to some limiting random variable
R\in L^{p}(\mu_{1})
.

(ii)

Given any finite
p\geq 1
,
there exists
C_{p}>0
such that

(5.1)

\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\Big\|e^{R_{N}(u)}\Big\|_{L^{p}(\mu_{1})}\leq C_{p}<\infty.

Moreover, we have

(5.2)

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}e^{R_{N}(u)}=e^{R(u)}\qquad\text{in }L^{p}(\mu_{1}).

As a consequence,
the truncated renormalized Gibbs measure
\vec{\rho}_{N}
in (
1.13
) converges, in the sense of (
5.2
),
to the renormalized Gibbs measure
\vec{\rho}
given by

(5.3)

\displaystyle d\vec{\rho}(u,v)=\mathfrak{Z}^{-1}e^{R(u)}d\vec{\mu}_{1}(u,v).

Furthermore,
the resulting Gibbs measure
\vec{\rho}
is equivalent
to the Gaussian measure
\vec{\mu}_{1}
.

Then, a standard argument shows the invariance of the measure
\vec{\rho}_{N}
under the flow of (
1.15
); see for instance
[
78
, Subsection 5.2]
for details in the context of the hyperbolic Liouville model.

Lemma 5.2
.

Fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
with
0<\beta^{2}<4\pi
. The truncated sine-Gordon measure
\vec{\rho}_{N}
in (
1.13
) is invariant under the truncated dynamics (
1.15
).

5.2.
Stochastic convolution
and its space-time covariance

In this subsection,
we study basic properties of the stochastic convolution
\Psi^{\text{wave}}

defined in (
1.28
). In particular, we establish
sharp bounds on the space-time covariance of the
\Psi_{N}^{\text{wave}}
in (
1.29
) and its spatial derivatives that are uniform in the smoothing parameter
N
; see Propositions
5.5
and
5.7
below.

The following lemma provides the (uniform in
N
) regularity properties for
\Psi^{\text{wave}}_{N}
; see
[
46
]
for a proof of (i).

Lemma 5.3
.

Fix any
0\leq T\leq 1
,
\varepsilon>0
and finite
p\geq 1
,

\{\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is a Cauchy sequence in
L^{p}(\Omega;C([0,T];W^{-\varepsilon}(\mathbb{T}^{2})))
,
thus converging to some limiting process
\Psi^{\textup{wave}}\in L^{p}(\Omega;C([0,T];W^{-\varepsilon}(\mathbb{T}^{2})))
.
Moreover,
\{\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
converges almost surely to the same limit
\Psi^{\textup{wave}}
in
C([0,T];W^{-\varepsilon}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))
.

Next, we study the difference of the stochastic convolutions (
1.24
) and (
1.28
).

Lemma 5.4
.

Fix
0<s<1
,
0<b<\frac{1}{2}
and
0<T\leq 1
. Let
Z_{\infty}^{s,b}=\Lambda_{\infty}^{s,0}\cap\Lambda^{0,b}_{\infty}
. Then,
\{\Psi^{\textup{KG}}_{N}-\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is a Cauchy sequence in
L^{p}(\Omega;Z_{\infty}^{s,b}([0,T]))
,
thus converging to some limiting process
{\Psi^{\textup{KG}}-\Psi^{\textup{wave}}\in L^{p}(\Omega;Z_{\infty}^{s,b}([0,T]))}
.
Moreover,
\{\Psi^{\textup{KG}}_{N}-\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
converges almost surely to the same limit
\Psi^{\textup{KG}}-\Psi^{\textup{wave}}
in
Z_{\infty}^{s,b}([0,T])
.

Proof.

Fix
(N,N_{1},N_{2})\in\mathbb{N}^{3}
with
N_{2}\geq N_{1}
,
(t,t_{1},t_{2})\in[0,1]^{2}
and set
\Psi_{M}=\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\big(\Psi^{\textup{KG}}_{M}-\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{M}\big)
for each
M\in\mathbb{N}
. Our goal is to prove the following bounds:

(5.4)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\big|\widehat{\Psi_{N}}(t,n)\big|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle n\rangle^{-4},

(5.5)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\big|\widehat{\Psi_{N}}(t_{1},n)-\widehat{\Psi_{N}}(t_{2},n)\big|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim|t_{1}-t_{2}|\langle n\rangle^{-2},

(5.6)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\big|\widehat{\Psi_{N_{1}}}(t,n)-\widehat{\Psi_{N_{2}}}(t,n)\big|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim N_{1}^{-\theta}\langle n\rangle^{-4+\theta},

for any small constant
\theta>0
. Here, the implicit constants are uniform in the parameters
N,N_{1},N_{2},t,t_{1},t_{2}
.

We start with the proof of (
5.4
). By (
1.23
), (
1.24
), (
1.28
), (
1.30
), we have

(5.7)

\displaystyle\widehat{\Psi_{N}}(t,n)=\big(\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt(t,n)+\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}(t,n)+\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt I \hskip-8.25128pt I}(t,n)\big)\chi_{N}(n),

where

\displaystyle\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt(t,n)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\cos(t[\hskip-1.70717pt[n]\hskip-1.70717pt])-\cos(t|n|)+\frac{\sin(t[\hskip-1.70717pt[n]\hskip-1.70717pt])}{2[\hskip-1.70717pt[n]\hskip-1.70717pt]}-\frac{\sin(t|n|)}{2|n|}\bigg)e^{-\frac{t}{2}}\frac{g_{n}}{\langle n\rangle},

\displaystyle\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}(t,n)

\displaystyle=\bigg(\frac{\sin(t[\hskip-1.70717pt[n]\hskip-1.70717pt])}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[n]\hskip-1.70717pt]}-\frac{\sin(t|n|)}{|n|}\bigg)e^{-\frac{t}{2}}h_{n},

\displaystyle\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt I \hskip-8.25128pt I}(t,n)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}\int_{0}^{t}e^{-\frac{t-t^{\prime}}{2}}\bigg(\frac{\sin((t-t^{\prime})[\hskip-1.70717pt[n]\hskip-1.70717pt])}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[n]\hskip-1.70717pt]}-\frac{\sin((t-t)|n|)}{|n|}\bigg)dB_{n}(t^{\prime}).

The bound (
5.4
) is then a consequence of (
5.7
), the formulas above, the independence of
g_{n}
,
h_{n}
and
B_{n}
, Ito’s isometry, the mean value theorem and the bounds

(5.8)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big|[\hskip-1.70717pt[n]\hskip-1.70717pt]-|n|\big|&\lesssim\langle n\rangle^{-1},\\
\bigg|\frac{1}{[\hskip-1.70717pt[n]\hskip-1.70717pt]}-\frac{1}{|n|}\bigg|&\lesssim\langle n\rangle^{-2}\end{split}

for
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}
. The estimate (
5.6
) then follows from (
5.4
) and observing that the left-hand-side of (
5.6
) is non-zero if and only if
N_{1}\lesssim\langle n\rangle\lesssim N_{2}
.

We turn our attention to (
5.5
). From using the mean value theorem twice, we get

(5.9)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big|\big(\cos(t_{1}[\hskip-1.70717pt[n]\hskip-1.70717pt])-\cos(t_{1}|n|)\big)-\big(\cos(t_{2}[\hskip-1.70717pt[n]\hskip-1.70717pt])-\cos(t_{2}|n|)\big)\big|\\
&\qquad=\bigg|-t_{1}\big([\hskip-1.70717pt[n]\hskip-1.70717pt]-|n|\big)\int_{0}^{1}\sin\big((1-h)t_{1}[\hskip-1.70717pt[n]\hskip-1.70717pt]+ht_{1}|n|\big)dh\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad+t_{2}\big([\hskip-1.70717pt[n]\hskip-1.70717pt]-|n|\big)\int_{0}^{1}\sin\big((1-h)t_{2}[\hskip-1.70717pt[n]\hskip-1.70717pt]+ht_{2}|n|\big)dh\bigg|\\
&\qquad\lesssim|t_{1}-t_{2}|.\end{split}

Similarly, we also have

(5.10)

\displaystyle\big|\big(\sin(t_{1}[\hskip-1.70717pt[n]\hskip-1.70717pt])-\sin(t_{1}|n|)\big)-\big(\sin(t_{2}[\hskip-1.70717pt[n]\hskip-1.70717pt])-\sin(t_{2}|n|)\big)\big|\lesssim|t_{1}-t_{2}|.

Therefore, (
5.5
) follows from (
5.7
), the formulas for
I
,
I I
and
I I I
, (
5.8
) together with the mean value theorem and (
5.9
)-(
5.10
).

By interpolating (
5.4
), (
5.5
) and (
5.6
), hypercontractivity (see
[
86
, Theorem I.22]
) and the Kolmogorov continuity criterion (see
[
4
, Theorem 8.2]
) together with standard arguments,
22
22

22

See for instance
[
70
, Proposition 5]
for a detailed proof of a very similar argument.
we deduce that
\{\Psi^{\textup{KG}}_{N}-\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is a Cauchy sequence in
L^{p}(\Omega;C_{t}^{b}L^{\infty}_{x}\cap C_{t}W^{s,\infty}_{x})
and in
C_{t}^{b}L^{\infty}_{x}\cap C_{t}W^{s,\infty}_{x}
almost surely and for any
0<s<1
and
0<b<\frac{1}{2}
. Here,
C^{b}_{t}(\mathbb{R};X)
for a Banach space
(X,\|\cdot\|)
denotes the space of
b
-Hölder continuous functions defined as the completion of
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};X)
under the norm

\|f\|_{C^{b}_{t}(\mathbb{R};X)}=\sup_{t\in\mathbb{R}}\|f(t)\|+\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(t_{1},t_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\\
t_{1}\neq t_{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{\|f(t_{1})-f(t_{2})\|}{|t_{1}-t_{2}|}.

By
[
67
, Proposition 2.9.5]
, we learn that the spaces
C^{b}(\mathbb{R},\mathbb{R})
and
\mathcal{B}^{b}_{\infty,\infty}
coincide for each
0<b<1
, where
\mathcal{B}^{b}_{\infty,\infty}
is the usual Hölder-Besov space. The desired result hence follows from the continuous embeddings
W^{b,\infty}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{B}^{b}_{\infty,\infty}\hookrightarrow W^{b+\varepsilon,\infty}
for any
\varepsilon>0
.
∎

Our main goal in this subsection is
to study
the space-time covariance
\Gamma_{N}
of
\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{N}
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
, adefined in (
1.45
). Given
N_{1},N_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
, we set

(5.11)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{N_{1},N_{2}}(t_{1},t_{2},x_{1},x_{2})=\mathbb{E}\big[\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{N_{1}}(t_{1},x_{1})\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{N_{2}}(t_{2},x_{2})\big].

for any
(t_{1},x_{1}),(t_{2},x_{2})\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}
. Since
\Psi^{\text{wave}}_{N}
is constructed from the spatially homogeneous processes
23
23

23

A random variable
X
is said to be spatially homogeneous if
X
and
X(\cdot+y)
share the same law for any
y\in\mathbb{T}^{2}
.

u_{0}
,
v_{0}
and
W
and translation invariant operators,
\Psi^{\text{wave}}_{N}
is also spatially homogeneous and we have

\displaystyle\Gamma_{N}(t_{1},t_{2},x_{1},x_{2})

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{N}(t_{1},t_{2},x_{1}-x_{2},0),

\displaystyle\Gamma_{N_{1},N_{2}}(t_{1},t_{2},x_{1},x_{2})

\displaystyle=\Gamma_{N_{1},N_{2}}(t_{1},t_{2},x_{1}-x_{2},0).

In what follows we use, with a slight abuse of notations, the (spatially) “translation-invariant” notations
\Gamma_{N}(t_{1},t_{2},x)
and
\Gamma_{N_{1},N_{2}}(t_{1},t_{2},x)
for
\Gamma_{N}(t_{1},t_{2},x,0)
and
\Gamma_{N_{1},N_{2}}(t_{1},t_{2},x,0)
, respectively. Namely, we write

(5.12)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Gamma_{N}(t_{1},t_{2},x)&=\mathbb{E}\big[\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{N}(t_{1},x)\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{N}(t_{2},0)\big],\\
\Gamma_{N_{1},N_{2}}(t_{1},t_{2},x)&=\mathbb{E}\big[\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{N_{1}}(t_{1},x)\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{N_{2}}(t_{2},0)\big].\end{split}

The following proposition establishes
sharp bounds on the space-time covariance
\Gamma_{N}

and its variant
\Gamma_{N_{1},N_{2}}
,
extending

[
75
, Lemma 2.7]

and

[
76
, (2.2)]
to the time-dependent context.

Proposition 5.5
.

Given
N\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\Gamma_{N}
be as in (
1.45
)-(
5.12
). Then, we have

(5.13)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{N}(t_{1},t_{2},x)\approx-\frac{1}{2\pi}\log\big(|t_{1}-t_{2}|+|x|+N^{-1}\big)

for any
(t_{1},t_{2},x)\in[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}
. Here, the notation “
\approx
” is as in (
2.1
)
and (
2.2
).
Given
N_{1},N_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
,
let
\Gamma_{N_{1},N_{2}}
be as in (
5.11
).
Then, we have

(5.14)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{N_{1},N_{2}}(t_{1},t_{2},x)\approx-\frac{1}{2\pi}\log\big(|t_{1}-t_{2}|+|x|+N_{1}^{-1}\big)

and

(5.15)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&|\Gamma_{N_{j}}(t_{1},t_{2},x)-\Gamma_{N_{1},N_{2}}(t_{1},t_{2},x)|\\
&\quad\lesssim\Big(1\vee\big(-\log\big(|t_{1}-t_{2}|+|x|+N_{2}^{-1}\big)\big)\Big)\wedge(N_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}|x|^{-\frac{1}{2}})+O(N_{1}^{-1})\end{split}

for any
(t_{1},t_{2},x)\in[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}
,

N_{2}\geq N_{1}\geq 1
and
j=1,2
.

Remark 5.6
.

The estimate (
5.13
) in Proposition
5.5
shows that the (smoothed) space-time covariance function
\Gamma_{N}
has a singularity of elliptic type in the sense of Section
3
. This is rather surprising since
\Psi^{\text{wave}}_{N}
is the solution to a linear (damped) wave equation and is due to a key cancellation; see the proof of Lemma
5.10
below. The hyperbolic nature of
\Psi^{\text{wave}}_{N}
however shows up when considering spatial derivatives of
\Gamma_{N}
; see Proposition
5.7
and Remark
5.8
below.

In our physical space approach, it is crucial to also obtain tight bounds on spatial derivatives of the space-time covariance
\Gamma_{N}
; see for instance Subsection
5.5
where such estimates are heavily used. To this end, we introduce a conventient notation. Fix
s>0
,
N\in\mathbb{N}
and define the functions

(5.16)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{N}(t,x;s)

\displaystyle=\min\!\big(N^{s},(|t|+|x|)^{-\frac{1}{2}}||t|-|x||^{\frac{1}{2}-s}\big).

for any
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
x\in\mathbb{T}^{2}
.

In the next proposition, we show how the functions (
5.16
) control the size of spatial derivatives of the space-time covariance
\Gamma_{N}
.

Proposition 5.7
.

Fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\Gamma_{N}
and
\mathcal{H}_{N}
be as in (
1.45
)-(
5.12
) and (
5.16
) respectively. Then, we have

(5.17)

\displaystyle|\partial^{\alpha}_{x}\Gamma_{N}(t_{1},t_{2},x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\varepsilon,s}\mathcal{H}_{N}(t_{1}-t_{2},x;s)+|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{-\varepsilon}

for any
(t_{1},t_{2},x)\in[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}
,
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
1\leq|\alpha|\leq 2
, any
\varepsilon>0
and
s>|\alpha|
. Here, the implicit constant is independent of
N
.

The rest of this section is devoted to the proofs of Propositions
5.5
and
5.7
.

Remark 5.8
.

We make a few remarks.

(i) Note that the derivative of order
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}\setminus\{0\}
of the right-hand-side of (
5.13
) in Proposition
5.5
is essentially given by the elliptic singularity

\big(|t_{1}-t_{2}|+|x|+N^{-1}\big)^{-|\alpha|},

which is in general much better behaved than the hyperbolic singularity
\mathcal{H}_{N}(t_{1}-t_{2},x;s)
in (
5.17
). The latter comes from spatial derivatives of the remainder (hidden in the symbol “
\approx
”) in (
5.13
) and highlights the hyperbolic nature of our problem. The presence of functions
\mathcal{H}_{N}
which are singular along light cones (as opposed to a point in the elliptic case) in (
5.17
) makes the analysis in Subsections
5.5
and
5.6
very challenging.

(ii) In the case of the heat equation,
the space-time covariance of the associated
stochastic convolution is given by

(5.18)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{N}^{\text{heat}}(t_{1}-t_{2},x)=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\log\big(|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}+|x|+N^{-1}\big)+R_{N}(x),

where
R_{N}
is smooth uniformly in
N
in the sense that

\displaystyle\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}R_{N}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\leq C_{\alpha},

for any
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
. Here,
C_{\alpha}>0
is a constant independent of
N
. See, for example,
[
52
, Lemmas 3.7 and 3.8]
.
Therefore in the parabolic setting, we only have to deal with parabolic/elliptic singularities centered at the space-time origin when considering spatial derivatives of
\Gamma_{N}^{\text{heat}}
. This in sharp contrast with the hyperbolic case at hand.

Proposition
5.5
essentially follows from
an analogous estimate on
the following time-dependent variants of the periodic Green function

G

defined in (
2.16
) and (
2.17
):

(5.19)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{N}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\cos\big(t|\nabla|)\mathbf{\Pi}_{\leq N}^{2}G(x),

(5.20)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{N_{1},N_{2}}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\cos\big(t|\nabla|)\mathbf{\Pi}_{\leq N_{1}}\mathbf{\Pi}_{\leq N_{2}}G(x)

for any
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}
.

In what follows, we aim to relate
\Gamma_{N}
to
\Lambda_{N}
. To this end, we first introduce some convenient notations. Fix an integer
k\in\mathbb{N}
. We denote by
C^{k}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
the usual Hölder space of
C^{k}
-functions from
\mathbb{T}^{2}
to
\mathbb{R}
equipped with the norm

\displaystyle\|f\|_{C^{k}_{x}}=\max_{0\leq|\alpha|\leq k}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2})}.

Let
\mathcal{U}^{\infty,k}
be the space
L^{\infty}\big((\mathbb{R}_{+})^{2};C^{k}(\mathbb{T}^{2})\big)
endowed with the norm

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\mathcal{U}^{\infty,k}}=\|u(t_{1},t_{2})\|_{L^{\infty}((\mathbb{R}_{+})^{2};C^{k}_{x})}.

In what follows, we write
u\asymp v
for
u,v:\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
if
u-v\in\mathcal{U}^{\infty,2}
. Similarly, for
\{u_{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{v_{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
two sequences of functions in
(\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}})^{\mathbb{N}}
, we write
u_{N}\asymp v_{N}
if
u_{N}-v_{N}
belongs to
\mathcal{U}^{\infty,2}
uniformly in
N\in\mathbb{N}
. Namely, if we have

\displaystyle\sup_{N\in\mathbb{N}}\|u_{N}-v_{N}\|_{\mathcal{U}^{\infty,2}}<\infty.

Let
F_{1},F_{2},F_{3}:\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}
be the functions given by

(5.21)

\displaystyle\begin{split}F_{1}(t_{1},t_{2},x)&=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\frac{\sin((t_{1}-t_{2})|n|)}{|n|\langle n\rangle^{2}}e_{n}(x),\\
F_{2}(t_{1},t_{2},x)&=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\frac{\cos((t_{1}-t_{2})|n|)}{\langle n\rangle^{4}}e_{n}(x),\\
F_{3}(t_{1},t_{2},x)&=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\frac{\cos((t_{1}+t_{2})|n|)}{\langle n\rangle^{4}}e_{n}(x).\end{split}

Clearly,
F_{1},F_{2}
and
F_{3}
belong to
\mathcal{U}^{\infty,1}
. We define the subspace
\mathcal{U}^{\infty,1}(F_{1},F_{2},F_{3})
of
\mathcal{U}^{\infty,1}
given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}^{\infty,1}(F_{1},F_{2},F_{3})=\big\{g_{1}F_{1}+g_{2}F_{2}+g_{3}F_{3}:(g_{1},g_{2},g_{3})\in L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2};\mathbb{R})\big\}.

Armed with these notations, we can now give a precise description of the covariance function (
1.45
) in terms of the periodic Green function (
5.19
) modulo elements of
\mathcal{U}^{\infty,1}(F_{1},F_{2},F_{3})
and
\mathcal{U}^{\infty,2}
.

Lemma 5.9
.

Fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
. There exists a function
F\in\mathcal{U}^{\infty,1}(F_{1},F_{2},F_{3})
such that we have the following decomposition:

(5.22)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{N}(t_{1},t_{2},x)\asymp e^{-\frac{|t_{1}-t_{2}|}{2}}\Lambda_{N}(t_{1}-t_{2},x)+\mathbf{\Pi}_{\leq N}^{2}F(t_{1},t_{2},x),

for any
(t_{1},t_{2},x)\in[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}
with
|t_{1}-t_{2}|\leq 1
.

Proof.

Fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
(t_{1},t_{2},x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}
with
|t_{1}-t_{2}|\lesssim 1
.
We assume
t_{1}\geq t_{2}
for convenience, but the proof is similar in the case
t_{1}<t_{2}
and leads to a slightly different function
F
.
From (
1.28
) and the independence
of
u_{0}
,
v_{0}
, and the space-time white noise forcing
\xi
, we have

(5.23)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{N}(t_{1},t_{2},x)=\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{N}(t_{1},t_{2},x)+\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}_{N}(t_{1},t_{2},x)+\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt I \hskip-8.25128pt I}_{N}(t_{1},t_{2},x),

where

\displaystyle\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{N}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[(\partial_{t}\mathcal{S}(t_{1})+\mathcal{S}(t_{1}))\mathbf{\Pi}_{\leq N}u_{0}(x)\cdot(\partial_{t}\mathcal{S}(t_{1})+\mathcal{S}(t_{2}))\mathbf{\Pi}_{\leq N}u_{0}(0)\Big],

\displaystyle\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}_{N}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathcal{S}(t_{1})\mathbf{\Pi}_{\leq N}v_{0}(x)\cdot\mathcal{S}(t_{2})\mathbf{\Pi}_{\leq N}v_{0}(0)\Big],

\displaystyle\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt I \hskip-8.25128pt I}_{N}

\displaystyle=2\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(\int_{0}^{t_{1}}\mathcal{S}(t_{1}-t)d\mathbf{\Pi}_{\leq N}\mathcal{W}(t)\Big)(x)\cdot\Big(\int_{0}^{t_{2}}\mathcal{S}(t_{2}-t)d\mathbf{\Pi}_{\leq N}\mathcal{W}(t)\Big)(0)\bigg].

From (
1.28
), (
1.7
), (
1.9
),
and the independence of
g_{n}
, we have

(5.24)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{N}(t_{1},t_{2},x)&=\frac{e^{-\frac{t_{1}+t_{2}}{2}}}{2\pi}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\Big(\cos(t_{1}|n|)+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\sin(t_{1}|n|)}{|n|}\Big)\\
&\quad\times\Big(\cos(t_{2}|n|)+\frac{1}{2}\frac{\sin(t_{2}|n|)}{|n|}\Big)\frac{\chi_{N}^{2}(n)}{\langle n\rangle^{2}}e_{n}(x)\\
&=\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{N}^{a}(t_{1},t_{2},x)+\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{N}^{b}(t_{1},t_{2},x)+\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{N}^{c}(t_{1},t_{2},x),\end{split}

with

(5.25)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{N}^{a}(t_{1},t_{2},x)&=\frac{e^{-\frac{t_{1}+t_{2}}{2}}}{2\pi}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\cos(t_{1}|n|)\cos(t_{2}|n|)\frac{\chi^{2}_{N}(n)}{\langle n\rangle^{2}}e_{n}(x),\\
\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{N}^{b}(t_{1},t_{2},x)&=\frac{e^{-\frac{t_{1}+t_{2}}{2}}}{4\pi}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\frac{\sin((t_{1}+t_{2})|n|)\chi^{2}_{N}(n)}{|n|\langle n\rangle^{2}}e_{n}(x),\\
\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{N}^{c}(t_{1},t_{2},x)&=\frac{e^{-\frac{t_{1}+t_{2}}{2}}}{8\pi}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\frac{\sin(t_{1}|n|)\sin(t_{2}|n|)\chi^{2}_{N}(n)}{|n|^{2}\langle n\rangle^{2}}e_{n}(x)\\
&\asymp\frac{e^{-\frac{t_{1}+t_{2}}{2}}}{16\pi}\mathbf{\Pi}_{\leq N}^{2}\big(F_{2}(t_{1},t_{2},x)-F_{3}(t_{1},t_{2},x)\big),\end{split}

where
F_{2}
and
F_{3}
are as in (
5.21
). In the expression for
\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt^{c}_{N}
, we used the identities
\sin(A)\sin(B)=\frac{1}{2}(\cos(A-B)-\cos(A+B))
and

(5.26)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|n|^{2}}-\frac{1}{\langle n\rangle^{2}}=\frac{1}{\langle n\rangle^{2}|n|^{2}}

for any
n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}
, to replace the factor
|n|^{-2}
with
\langle n\rangle^{-2}
in the Fourier decomposition of
\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt^{c}_{N}
(via the use of the symbol “
\asymp
”). Similarly, we have

(5.27)

\displaystyle\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}_{N}(t_{1},t_{2},x)=\frac{e^{-\frac{t_{1}+t_{2}}{2}}}{2\pi}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\frac{\sin(t_{1}|n|)\sin(t_{2}|n|)}{|n|^{2}}\chi_{N}^{2}(n)e_{n}(x).

By (
1.25
), (
1.28
), the independence of
B_{n}
’s
and the Wiener isometry and since
t_{1}\geq t_{2}\geq 0
, we have

(5.28)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt I \hskip-8.25128pt I}_{N}(t_{1},t_{2},x)&=\frac{e^{-\frac{t_{1}+t_{2}}{2}}}{\pi}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\chi_{N}^{2}(n)e_{n}(x)\int_{0}^{t_{2}}e^{t}\frac{\sin((t_{1}-t)|n|)\sin((t_{2}-t)|n|)}{|n|^{2}}dt\\
&=\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt I \hskip-8.25128pt I}_{N}^{1}(t_{1},t_{2},x)+\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt I \hskip-8.25128pt I}_{N}^{2}(t_{1},t_{2},x),\end{split}

with

(5.29)

\displaystyle\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt I \hskip-8.25128pt I}_{N}^{1}(t_{1},t_{1},x)=\frac{e^{-\frac{t_{1}+t_{2}}{2}}}{\pi}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\frac{\chi_{N}^{2}(n)}{\langle n\rangle^{2}}e_{n}(x)\int_{0}^{t_{2}}e^{t}\sin((t_{1}-t)|n|)\sin((t_{2}-t)|n|)dt,

and

(5.30)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt I \hskip-8.25128pt I}_{N}^{2}(t_{1},t_{2},x)&=\frac{e^{-\frac{t_{1}+t_{2}}{2}}}{\pi}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\chi_{N}^{2}(n)e_{n}(x)\int_{0}^{t_{2}}e^{t}\Big(\frac{\sin((t_{1}-t)|n|)\sin((t_{2}-t)|n|)}{|n|^{2}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{\sin((t_{1}-t)|n|)\sin((t_{2}-t)|n|)}{\langle n\rangle^{2}}\Big)dt.\end{split}

Note that the zero
{}^{\text{th}}
-Fourier mode of
\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt I \hskip-8.25128pt I}^{2}_{N}
is smooth uniformly in
N\in\mathbb{N}
. Hence, by (
5.26
), the identity
\sin(A)\sin(B)=\frac{1}{2}(\cos(A-B)-\cos(A+B))
and integration by parts, we have that

(5.31)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt I \hskip-8.25128pt I}_{N}^{2}(t_{1},t_{2},x)&\asymp\frac{e^{-\frac{t_{1}+t_{2}}{2}}}{\pi}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}}\chi_{N}^{2}(n)e_{n}(x)\int_{0}^{t_{2}}e^{t}\Big(\frac{\sin((t_{1}-t)|n|)\sin((t_{2}-t)|n|)}{|n|^{2}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{\sin((t_{1}-t)|n|)\sin((t_{2}-t)|n|)}{\langle n\rangle^{2}}\Big)dt\\
&\asymp\frac{e^{-\frac{t_{1}+t_{2}}{2}}}{\pi}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}}\frac{\chi_{N}^{2}(n)}{\langle n\rangle^{2}|n|^{2}}e_{n}(x)\int_{0}^{t_{2}}e^{t}\sin((t_{1}-t)|n|)\sin((t_{2}-t)|n|)dt\\
&\asymp\frac{e^{-\frac{t_{1}+t_{2}}{2}}}{2\pi}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}}\frac{\chi_{N}^{2}(n)}{\langle n\rangle^{2}|n|^{2}}e_{n}(x)\\
&\qquad\qquad\quad\times\int_{0}^{t_{2}}e^{t}\big(\cos((t_{1}-t_{2})|n|)-\cos((t_{1}+t_{2}-2t)|n|)\big)dt\\
&\asymp\frac{e^{\frac{t_{2}-t_{1}}{2}}-e^{-\frac{t_{1}+t_{2}}{2}}}{2\pi}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}}\frac{\cos((t_{1}-t_{2})|n|)}{\langle n\rangle^{2}|n|^{2}}\chi_{N}^{2}(n)e_{n}(x)\\
&\asymp\frac{e^{\frac{t_{2}-t_{1}}{2}}-e^{-\frac{t_{1}+t_{2}}{2}}}{2\pi}\mathbf{\Pi}_{\leq N}^{2}F_{2}(t_{1},t_{2},x),\end{split}

where we used (
5.26
) again in the last line to replace
|n|^{-2}
with
\langle n\rangle^{-2}
. Similarly, we also have that

(5.32)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt I \hskip-8.25128pt I}^{1}_{N}(t_{1},t_{2},x)&=\frac{e^{-\frac{t_{1}+t_{2}}{2}}}{2\pi}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\frac{\chi_{N}^{2}(n)}{\langle n\rangle^{2}}e_{n}(x)\\
&\quad\times\int_{0}^{t_{2}}e^{t}\big(\cos((t_{1}-t_{2})|n|)-\cos((t_{1}+t_{2}-2t)|n|)\big)dt\\
&=:\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt I \hskip-8.25128pt I}_{N}^{1,a}(t_{1},t_{2},x)-\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt I \hskip-8.25128pt I}_{N}^{1,b}(t_{1},t_{2},x).\end{split}

By performing the
t
-integration in
\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt I \hskip-8.25128pt I}^{1,a}_{N}
and (
5.19
), we have

(5.33)

\displaystyle\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt I \hskip-8.25128pt I}_{N}^{1,a}(t_{1},t_{2},x)=\big(e^{\frac{t_{2}-t_{1}}{2}}-e^{-\frac{t_{1}+t_{2}}{2}}\big)\Lambda_{N}(t_{1}-t_{2},x).

Hence, from (
5.25
), (
5.27
), (
5.33
), (
5.19
) and the identity
\cos(A-B)=\cos A\cos B+\sin A\sin B
, we obtain

(5.34)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt^{a}_{N}(t_{1},t_{2},x_{1})+\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}_{N}(t_{1},t_{2},x)+\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt I \hskip-8.25128pt I}^{1,a}_{N}(t_{1},t_{2},x)=e^{\frac{t_{2}-t_{1}}{2}}\Lambda_{N}(t_{1}-t_{2},x).\end{split}

Integrating by parts the term
\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt I \hskip-8.25128pt I}^{1,b}_{N}
gives

\displaystyle\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt I \hskip-8.25128pt I}_{N}^{1,b}(t_{1},t_{1},x)

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{e^{-\frac{t_{1}+t_{2}}{2}}}{4\pi}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}}\frac{\chi_{N}^{2}(n)}{\langle n\rangle^{2}|n|}\sin((t_{1}+t_{2})|n|)e_{n}(x)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{e^{\frac{t_{2}-t_{1}}{2}}}{4\pi}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}}\frac{\chi_{N}^{2}(n)}{\langle n\rangle^{2}|n|}\sin((t_{1}-t_{2})|n|)e_{n}(x)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{e^{\frac{t_{2}-t_{1}}{2}}}{8\pi}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}}\frac{\chi_{N}^{2}(n)}{\langle n\rangle^{2}|n|^{2}}\cos((t_{1}-t_{2})|n|)e_{n}(x)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{e^{-\frac{t_{2}+t_{1}}{2}}}{8\pi}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}}\frac{\chi_{N}^{2}(n)}{\langle n\rangle^{2}|n|^{2}}\cos((t_{1}+t_{2})|n|)e_{n}(x)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{e^{-\frac{t_{2}+t_{1}}{2}}}{8\pi}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}}\frac{\chi_{N}^{2}(n)}{\langle n\rangle^{2}|n|^{2}}\int_{0}^{t_{2}}e^{t}\sin((t_{1}+t_{2}-2t)|n|)e_{n}(x).

Thus, with the notations in (
5.21
) and using (
5.26
) as before, we have

(5.35)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt I \hskip-8.25128pt I}_{N}^{1,b}(t_{1},t_{1},x)&\asymp\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt_{N}^{b}(t_{1},t_{2},x)-\frac{e^{\frac{t_{2}-t_{1}}{2}}}{4\pi}\mathbf{\Pi}_{\leq N}^{2}F_{1}(t_{1},t_{2},x)\\
&\quad+\frac{e^{\frac{t_{2}-t_{1}}{2}}}{8\pi}\mathbf{\Pi}_{\leq N}^{2}F_{2}(t_{1},t_{2},x)-\frac{e^{-\frac{t_{2}+t_{1}}{2}}}{8\pi}\mathbf{\Pi}_{\leq N}^{2}F_{3}(t_{1},t_{2},x),\end{split}

Therefore, by (
5.23
), (
5.24
), (
5.25
), (
5.28
), (
5.31
), (
5.32
), (
5.34
) and (
5.35
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\Gamma_{N}(t_{1},t_{2},x_{1}-x_{2})

\displaystyle\asymp e^{\frac{t_{2}-t_{1}}{2}}\Lambda_{N}(t_{1}-t_{2},x_{1}-x_{2})+\frac{e^{\frac{t_{2}-t_{1}}{2}}}{4\pi}\mathbf{\Pi}_{\leq N}^{2}F_{1}(t_{1},t_{2},x_{1}-x_{2})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{16\pi}\big(6e^{\frac{t_{2}-t_{1}}{2}}-7e^{-\frac{t_{1}+t_{2}}{2}}\big)\mathbf{\Pi}_{\leq N}^{2}F_{2}(t_{1},t_{2},x_{1}-x_{2})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{16\pi}e^{-\frac{t_{1}+t_{2}}{2}}\mathbf{\Pi}_{\leq N}^{2}F_{3}(t_{1},t_{2},x_{1}-x_{2}),

as required.
∎

Then, we have the following bound on
\Lambda_{N}
.

Lemma 5.10
.

Given
N\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\Lambda_{N}
be as in (
5.19
).
Then, we have

(5.36)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{N}(t,x)\approx-\frac{1}{2\pi}\log\big(|t|+|x|+N^{-1}\big)

for any
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}
with
0\leq|t|\leq 1
.
Given
N_{1},N_{2}\in\mathbb{N}
, let
\Lambda_{N_{1},N_{2}}
be as in (
5.20
).
Then, we have

(5.37)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{N_{1},N_{2}}(t,x)\approx-\frac{1}{2\pi}\log\big(|t|+|x|+N_{1}^{-1}\big)

and

(5.38)

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{N_{j}}(t,x)-\Lambda_{N_{1},N_{2}}(t,x)|\lesssim\Big(1\vee\big(-\log\big(|t|+|x|+N_{2}^{-1}\big)\big)\Big)\wedge(N_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}|x|^{-\frac{1}{2}})

for any
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}

with
0\leq|t|\leq 1
,

N_{2}\geq N_{1}\geq 1
and
j=1,2
.

See
[
75
, Lemma 2.3 and Remark 2.4]

for analogous results in the time-independent case.
We first present a proof of
Proposition
5.5
by
assuming Lemma
5.10
.

Proof of Proposition
5.5
.

First, note that by Lemma
5.9
, we have that

\displaystyle\Gamma_{N}(t_{1},t_{2},x)\approx e^{\frac{t_{2}-t_{1}}{2}}\Lambda_{N}(t_{1}-t_{2},x).

Therefore,
by noting that

(5.39)

\displaystyle t\big|\log(t+c_{0})\big|\leq t|\log t|+O(1)\lesssim 1,

uniformly in
0<t\lesssim 1
and
0<c_{0}\lesssim 1
, from (
5.36
) in Lemma
5.10
with the mean value theorem
and (
5.39
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\Gamma_{N}(t_{1},t_{2},x)

\displaystyle\approx\Lambda_{N}(t_{1}-t_{2},x)+\Big(e^{\frac{t_{2}-t_{1}}{2}}-1\Big)\Lambda_{N}(t_{1}-t_{2},x)

\displaystyle\approx\bigg(-\frac{1}{2\pi}+O(|t_{1}-t_{2}|)\bigg)\log\big(|t_{1}-t_{2}|+|x|+N^{-1}\big)

\displaystyle\approx-\frac{1}{2\pi}\log\big(|t_{1}-t_{2}|+|x|+N^{-1}\big),

provided that
|t_{1}-t_{2}|\lesssim 1
.
A similar computation with
(
5.37
) in Lemma
5.10
.
yields (
5.14
).

From
a slight modification of the computations in the proofs of Lemma
5.9
, we have

(5.40)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Gamma_{N_{j}}(t_{1},t_{2},x)-\Gamma_{N_{1},N_{2}}(t_{1},t_{2},x)\\
&\quad=e^{\frac{t_{2}-t_{1}}{2}}\Big\{\Lambda_{N_{j}}(t_{1}-t_{2},x)-\Lambda_{N_{1},N_{2}}(t_{1}-t_{2},x)\Big\}+O(N_{1}^{-1}).\end{split}

Then, the bound (
5.15
)
follows from (
5.40
)
and
(
5.38
)
in Lemma
5.10
.
∎

We now prove Lemma
5.10
.

Proof of Lemma
5.10
.

Fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
. In view of the parity of the cosine function, we fix
0\leq t\leq 1
in what follows without any loss of generality.

We first prove (
5.36
). It is easy to see that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{N}(t,x)\approx(\Lambda_{N}-\Lambda_{10})(t,x)

for any
x\in\mathbb{T}^{2}
. Hence, from (
5.19
) and
the Poisson summation formula (
2.9
), we have

(5.41)

\displaystyle(\Lambda_{N}-\Lambda_{10})(t,x)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}(f_{N}-f_{10})(t,x+2\pi k)

for any
x\in\mathbb{T}^{2}\cong[-\pi,\pi)^{2}
and where
f_{K}
is given by

(5.42)

\displaystyle f_{K}(t,x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\cos(t|\xi|)\frac{\chi_{K}^{2}(\xi)}{\langle\xi\rangle^{2}}e^{i\xi\cdot x}d\xi

for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
K\in\mathbb{N}
.

\bullet
Step 1: analysis of summands with
|k|\geq 1
in (
5.41
).
We first prove a bound on
f_{N}
. Let
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Then, by trigonometric identities, a polar change of variables with (
2.10
) and (
2.11
), we have

(5.43)

\displaystyle\begin{split}(f_{N}-f_{10})(t,x)&=\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\sum_{\varepsilon_{1}\in\{+,-\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{(\chi_{N}^{2}-\chi_{10}^{2})(\xi)}{\langle\xi\rangle^{2}}e^{i\xi\cdot x+i\varepsilon_{1}t|\xi|}d\xi\\
&=\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\sum_{\varepsilon_{1}\in\{+,-\}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{S}^{1}}\frac{(\chi_{N}^{2}-\chi_{10}^{2})(r)}{\langle r\rangle^{2}}e^{irx\cdot\omega+i\varepsilon_{1}tr}rdrd\sigma(\omega)\\
&=\frac{1}{4\pi}\sum_{\varepsilon_{1}\in\{+,-\}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{(\chi_{N}^{2}-\chi_{10}^{2})(r)}{\langle r\rangle^{2}}e^{i\varepsilon_{1}tr}\widecheck{d\sigma}(rx)rdr\\
&=\frac{1}{4\pi}\sum_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\in\{+,-\}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{(\chi_{N}^{2}-\chi_{10}^{2})(r)}{\langle r\rangle^{2}}e^{ir(\varepsilon_{1}t+\varepsilon_{2}|x|)}a_{\varepsilon_{2}}(rx)rdr.\end{split}

For fixed
r\in[0,\infty)
, the function
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus B(0,\pi)\mapsto e^{ir\varepsilon_{2}|x|}a_{\varepsilon_{2}}(rx)
is smooth and by the Leibniz rule, its
\alpha^{\textup{th}}
-order derivative, for
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
|\alpha|\leq 2
, is a finite sum (over
\alpha_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
) of terms of the form

F_{\alpha_{0}}(x)e^{ir\varepsilon_{2}|x|}\cdot(\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{0}}a_{\varepsilon_{2}})(rx)r^{|\alpha|},

where
\alpha_{0}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
|\alpha_{0}|\leq|\alpha|
and
F_{\alpha_{0}}
is a function bounded away from the origin. By integration by parts in the variable
r
and (
2.12
), noting that
\chi_{N}^{2}-\chi_{10}^{2}
(and its derivatives) vanishes near the origin, we get

(5.44)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big|\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{ir(\varepsilon_{1}t+\varepsilon_{2}|x|)}\frac{(\chi_{N}^{2}-\chi_{10}^{2})(r)}{\langle r\rangle^{2}}(\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{0}}a_{\varepsilon_{2}})(rx)r^{|\alpha_{0}|+1}dr\Big|\\
&\qquad\lesssim\big|\varepsilon_{1}t+\varepsilon_{2}|x|\big|^{-10}\int_{0}^{\infty}\bigg|\Big(\frac{\partial}{\partial r}\Big)^{10}\bigg\{\frac{(\chi_{N}^{2}-\chi_{10}^{2})(r)}{\langle r\rangle^{2}}(\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{0}}a_{\varepsilon_{2}})(rx)r^{|\alpha|+1}\bigg\}\bigg|dr\\
&\qquad\lesssim\big|\varepsilon_{1}t+\varepsilon_{2}|x|\big|^{-10}\int_{0}^{\infty}\langle r\rangle^{-2}dr\\
&\qquad\lesssim|x|^{-10}\end{split}

provided that
|x|\geq\pi
and
0\leq t\leq 1
. Thus, by (
5.43
), (
5.44
) and the discussion above, we have

(5.45)

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}(f_{N}-f_{10})(t,x)|\lesssim|x|^{-10},

for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}\setminus B(0,\pi)
,
0\leq t\leq 1
and
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
|\alpha|\leq 2
. Hence, by (
5.45
), we deduce

(5.46)

\displaystyle\bigg\|\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(f_{N}-f_{10})(t,x+2\pi k)\bigg\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}([-\pi,\pi)^{2})}\lesssim 1

for any
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
|\alpha|\leq 2
, uniformly in

0\leq t\leq 1
.
24
24

24

In the current proof, we only need (
5.46
) for
\alpha=0
, but we proved (
5.46
) for all
|\alpha|\leq 2
for future reference; see Lemma
5.11
.
Hence, by (
5.41
), (
5.42
) and (
5.46
), we have

(5.47)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\Lambda_{N}(t,x)&\approx(\Lambda_{N}-\Lambda_{10})(t,x)\\
&\approx(f_{N}-f_{10})(t,x)\\
&\approx f_{N}(t,x)\end{split}

for any
x\in\mathbb{T}^{2}\cong[-\pi,\pi)^{2}
. Thus, from (
5.41
), (
5.46
) and (
5.47
), (
5.36
) reduces to proving

(5.48)

\displaystyle f_{N}(t,x)\approx-\frac{1}{2\pi}\log\big(|t|+|x|+N^{-1}\big).

for any
x\in\mathbb{T}^{2}\cong[-\pi,\pi)^{2}
.

\bullet
Step 2: proof of (
5.48
).
Define a function
\eta_{N}
on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
by setting

(5.49)

\displaystyle\eta_{N}(x)=2N^{2}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{B(0,N^{-1})}(x),\quad x\in\mathbb{R}^{2},

where
B(x,r)\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
denotes the ball of radius
r>0

centered at
x
. On the Fourier side, we have

(5.50)

\displaystyle\widehat{\eta}_{N}(\xi)=\frac{N^{2}}{\pi}\int_{B(0,N^{-1})}e^{-i\xi\cdot x}dx=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{B(0,1)}e^{-i\frac{\xi}{N}\cdot x}dx,\quad\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{2}.

We claim that

(5.51)

\displaystyle|\chi_{N}^{2}(\xi)-\widehat{\eta}_{N}(\xi)|\lesssim\min\bigg(\frac{|\xi|}{N},\frac{N}{|\xi|}\bigg)

for any
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
Indeed, when
|\xi|\sim N
, the bound (
5.51
) trivially follows since
|\chi_{N}(\xi)|,|\widehat{\eta}_{N}(\xi)|\lesssim 1
,
uniformly in
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
.
When
|\xi|\ll N
, it follows from (
5.50
) and the mean value theorem that

\displaystyle|\chi_{N}^{2}(\xi)-\widehat{\eta}_{N}(\xi)|=\frac{1}{\pi}\bigg|\int_{B(0,1)}(1-e^{-i\frac{\xi}{N}\cdot x})dx\bigg|\lesssim\frac{|\xi|}{N},

yielding (
5.51
).
When
|\xi|\gg N
, we have
\chi_{N}(\xi)=0

and thus (
5.51
) follows from Green’s formula

[
34
, Theorem 3 (i) on p. 712]
.
Hence, from
(
5.47
) and (
5.51
), we obtain

\displaystyle f_{N}(t,x)\approx\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\cos(t|\xi|)\frac{\widehat{\eta}_{N}(\xi)}{\langle\xi\rangle^{2}}e^{i\xi\cdot x}d\xi.

Let
G_{N}=\eta_{N}*G_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}
, where
G_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}
is the Green function for
1-\Delta
on
\mathbb{R}^{2}

defined in (
2.13
)
and (
2.14
).
Then, we have

(5.52)

\displaystyle\begin{split}f_{N}(t,x)&\approx\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\cos(t|\xi|)\frac{\widehat{\eta}_{N}(\xi)}{\langle\xi\rangle^{2}}e^{i\xi\cdot x}d\xi\\
&=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\widehat{\eta}_{N}(\xi)}{\langle\xi\rangle^{2}}e^{i\xi\cdot x}d\xi+\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big(\cos(t|\xi|)-1\big)\frac{\widehat{\eta}_{N}(\xi)}{\langle\xi\rangle^{2}}e^{i\xi\cdot x}d\xi\\
&=G_{N}(t,x)-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\sin(t^{\prime}|\xi|)}{|\xi|}\frac{|\xi|^{2}}{\langle\xi\rangle^{2}}\widehat{\eta}_{N}(\xi)e^{i\xi\cdot x}d\xi dt^{\prime}\\
&\approx G_{N}(t,x)-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\sin(t^{\prime}|\xi|)}{|\xi|}\widehat{\eta}_{N}(\xi)e^{i\xi\cdot x}d\xi dt^{\prime}.\end{split}

Using (
2.24
)-(
2.25
), we can write the second term on the right-hand side of (
5.52
) as

(5.53)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\sin(t^{\prime}|\xi|)}{|\xi|}\widehat{\eta}_{N}(\xi)e^{i\xi\cdot x}d\xi dt^{\prime}\\
&\quad=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{B(x,t^{\prime})}\frac{\eta_{N}(y)}{\sqrt{(t^{\prime})^{2}-|x-y|^{2}}}dydt^{\prime}\\
&\quad=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{B(x,t)}\eta_{N}(y)\int_{|x-y|}^{t}\frac{1}{\sqrt{(t^{\prime})^{2}-|x-y|^{2}}}dt^{\prime}dy\\
&\quad=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{B(x,t)}\eta_{N}(y)\Big[\log\big(t^{\prime}+\sqrt{(t^{\prime})^{2}-|x-y|^{2}}\big)\Big]\bigg|^{t}_{|x-y|}dy\\
&\quad=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{B(x,t)}\eta_{N}(y)\log\big(t+\sqrt{t^{2}-|x-y|^{2}}\big)dy\\
&\quad\quad-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{B(x,t)}\eta_{N}(y)\log|x-y|\,dy\\
&\quad=:A_{N}(t,x)-B_{N}(t,x).\end{split}

We first deal with
G_{N}
in (
5.52
). In view of (
5.49
), (
2.14
), and the smoothness of
G_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}
away from the origin, we have

(5.54)

\displaystyle\begin{split}G_{N}(t,x)&=-\frac{N^{2}}{2\pi^{2}}\int_{B(0,N^{-1})}\log|x-y|\,dy\\
&=-\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\int_{B(0,1)}\log\big(N^{-1}|Nx-y|\big)dy\\
&\approx-\frac{1}{2\pi}\log(N^{-1})+R(Nx),\end{split}

where

(5.55)

\displaystyle R(z)=-\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\int_{B(0,1)}\log|z-y|\,dy,\quad z\in\mathbb{R}^{2}.

For
|z|\lesssim 1
, we have
|R(z)|\lesssim 1
.
On the other hand, for
|z|\gg 1
, we have

(5.56)

\displaystyle R(z)=-\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\int_{B(0,1)}\log|z|\,dy+O(1)=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\log|z|+O(1).

Hence, we conclude that

(5.57)

\displaystyle G_{N}(t,x)\approx-\frac{1}{2\pi}\log\big(|x|+N^{-1}\big).

Next, we treat
A_{N}
and
B_{N}
in (
5.53
).
By noting

t\leq t+\sqrt{t^{2}-N^{-2}|Nx-y|^{2}}\leq 2t
,
it follows from (
5.53
) and a change of variables that

(5.58)

\displaystyle A_{N}(t,x)

\displaystyle\approx\log t\cdot\frac{\big|B(0,1)\cap B(Nx,Nt)\big|}{2\pi^{2}}.

From (
5.53
) and a change of variables, we have

(5.59)

\displaystyle B_{N}(t,x)=\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\int_{B(0,1)\cap B(Nx,Nt)}\log\big(N^{-1}|Nx-y|\big)dy.

Then, by arguing as in (
5.54
)-(
5.57
), we have

(5.60)

\displaystyle B_{N}(t,x)

\displaystyle\approx\log\big(|x|+N^{-1}\big)\cdot\frac{\big|B(0,1)\cap B(Nx,Nt)\big|}{2\pi^{2}}.

In the following,
we
may assume that

(5.61)

\displaystyle N(|x|-t)\leq 1,

since, otherwise, we
would have
B(0,1)\cap B(Nx,Nt)=\varnothing

and thus
A_{N}(t,x)=B_{N}(t,x)=0
. Thus, by (
5.52
), (
5.53
) and (
5.57
), we would have

\displaystyle f_{N}(t,x)\approx-\frac{1}{2\pi}\log\big(|x|+N^{-1}\big)\approx-\frac{1}{2\pi}\log\big(t+|x|+N^{-1}\big),

proving (
5.48
) in that case.

\bullet
Case 1:

|x|+N^{-1}\gtrsim t
.
In this case, from (
5.58
) and (
5.60
), we have

(5.62)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|A_{N}(t,x)-B_{N}(t,x)|&\lesssim\bigg|\log\bigg(\frac{|x|+N^{-1}}{t}\bigg)\bigg|\cdot\big|B(0,1)\cap B(Nx,Nt)\big|\\
&\lesssim\bigg(\frac{N|x|+1}{Nt}\bigg)\cdot\big|B(0,1)\cap B(Nx,Nt)\big|\\
&\lesssim\bigg(\frac{N|x|+1}{Nt}\bigg)\cdot\min\big(1,(Nt)^{2}\big).\end{split}

If
N|x|\lesssim 1
, then we have

\displaystyle\eqref{c16}\lesssim(Nt)^{-1}\cdot(Nt)\lesssim 1.

Otherwise, i.e. if
N|x|\gg 1
, then we have

\displaystyle N|x|\sim Nt,

in view of the conditions
|x|+N^{-1}\gtrsim t
and (
5.61
).
This shows that
\eqref{c16}\lesssim 1
in this case as well.
Therefore,
from (
5.52
), (
5.53
), and (
5.57
)
with
\eqref{c16}\lesssim 1
, we conclude that

\displaystyle f_{N}(t,x)\approx-\frac{1}{2\pi}\log\big(|x|+N^{-1}\big)\approx-\frac{1}{2\pi}\log\big(t+|x|+N^{-1}\big),

yielding
(
5.48
) in this case.

\bullet
Case 2:

|x|+N^{-1}\ll t
.
In this case, we have

B(0,1)\subset B(Nx,Nt)

and thus it follows from (
5.57
) and (
5.60
)
that
B_{N}(t,x)\approx-G_{N}(t,x)
.
Hence,
from (
5.52
), (
5.53
), and (
5.58
),
we have

\displaystyle f_{N}(t,x)\approx-\frac{1}{2\pi}\log t\approx-\frac{1}{2\pi}\log\big(t+|x|+N^{-1}\big),

yielding
(
5.48
)

The second bound (
5.37
)
follows from
a slight modification
of the proof of (
5.36
),
once we replace
(
5.51
) by

\displaystyle|\chi_{N_{1}}(\xi)\chi_{N_{2}}(\xi)-\widehat{\eta}_{N_{1}}(\xi)|\lesssim\min\bigg(\frac{|\xi|}{N_{1}},\frac{N_{1}}{|\xi|}\bigg).

Lastly, we discuss the third bound (
5.38
).
The bound
by
1\vee\big(-\log\big(t+|x|+N_{1}^{-1}\big)\big)

in (
5.38
)
follows from (
5.37
).
In the following, we briefly discuss how to obtain the bound
by
N_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}|x|^{-\frac{1}{2}}
in (
5.38
)
when
j=1
.
When
j=2
, a similar computation holds and thus we omit details.

As in (
5.41
),
it follows from
the Poisson summation formula (
2.9
) that

(5.63)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{N_{1}}(t,x)-\Lambda_{N_{1},N_{2}}(t,x)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}f_{N_{1},N_{2}}(t,x+2\pi k),

for
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}
, where

f_{N_{1},N_{2}}
is given by

\displaystyle f_{N_{1},N_{2}}(t,x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\cos(t|\xi|)\frac{\chi_{N_{1}}(\xi)(\chi_{N_{1}}(\xi)-\chi_{N_{2}}(\xi))}{\langle\xi\rangle^{2}}e^{i\xi\cdot x}d\xi

for any
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Then, by arguing as in (
5.43
)-(
5.45
), we have

(5.64)

\displaystyle f_{N_{1},N_{2}}(t,x)=\frac{1}{4\pi}\sum_{\varepsilon_{1},\varepsilon_{2}\in\{+,-\}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\chi_{N_{1}}(r)(\chi_{N_{1}}(\xi)-\chi_{N_{2}}(r))}{\langle r\rangle^{2}}e^{ir(\varepsilon_{1}t+\varepsilon_{2}|x|)}a_{\varepsilon_{2}}(rx)rdr,

where
\{a_{\varepsilon_{2}}\}_{\varepsilon\in\{+,-\}}
is as in (
2.11
) and

(5.65)

\displaystyle|f_{N_{1},N_{2}}(t,x)|\lesssim|x|^{-10}N_{1}^{-2}

provided
|t|\leq 1
and
|x|\geq\pi
. In (
5.65
), the factor
N_{1}^{-2}
comes from the restriction
\{r\sim N_{1}\}
on the right-hand-side of (
5.64
).

Furthermore, by (
2.12
) and (
5.43
), we get

(5.66)

\displaystyle|f_{N_{1},N_{2}}(t,x)|\lesssim\int_{r\sim N_{1}}r^{-\frac{3}{2}}|x|^{-\frac{1}{2}}dr\lesssim N_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}|x|^{-\frac{1}{2}}.

Hence, we have

(5.67)

\displaystyle\bigg|\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}f_{N_{1},N_{2}}(t,x+2\pi k)\bigg|\lesssim N_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}|x|^{-\frac{1}{2}},

where we used (
5.66
) and (
5.65
) to estimate the contributions of the summands corresponding to
k=1
and
|k|\geq 1
, respectively. Therefore,
the bound
by
N_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}|x|^{-\frac{1}{2}}
in (
5.38
)
follows from (
5.63
) and (
5.67
). This concludes the proof of Lemma
5.10
.
∎

We now turn our attention to the proof of Proposition
5.7
which is a consequence of the two following lemmas.

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
F_{1},F_{2},F_{3}
be as in (
5.21
). Fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\mathcal{H}_{N}
be as in (
5.16
). Then, the following estimate holds:

(5.68)

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\mathbf{\Pi}_{\leq N}^{2}F_{j}(t_{1},t_{2},x)\big|\lesssim_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{H}_{N}(t_{1}-t_{2},x;s)+|x|^{-\varepsilon}

for any
(t_{1},t_{2},x)\in[0,1]^{2}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}
,
j\in\{1,2,3\}
,
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
1\leq|\alpha|\leq 2
and all
s>1
and
\varepsilon>0
, with an implicit constant independent of
N
.

Proof.

Fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
(t_{1},t_{2})\in[0,1]^{2}
. From (
5.21
) and
the Poisson summation formula (
2.9
), we have

(5.69)

\displaystyle\big(\mathbf{\Pi}_{\leq N}^{2}-\mathbf{\Pi}^{2}_{\leq 10}\big)F_{j}(t_{1},t_{2},x)(t_{1},t_{2},x)=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}(f_{N,j}-f_{10,j})(t_{1},t_{2},x+2\pi k)

for any
x\in\mathbb{T}^{2}\cong[-\pi,\pi)^{2}
, where
f_{K,j}
for
j\in\{1,2,3\}
are the functions given by

(5.70)

\displaystyle\begin{split}f_{K,1}(t_{1},t_{2},x)&=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\sin((t_{1}-t_{2})|\xi|)}{|\xi|\langle\xi\rangle^{2}}\chi_{K}^{2}(\xi)e^{i\xi\cdot x}d\xi\\
f_{K,2}(t_{1},t_{2},x)&=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\cos((t_{1}-t_{2})|\xi|)}{\langle\xi\rangle^{4}}\chi_{K}^{2}(\xi)e^{i\xi\cdot x}d\xi\\
f_{K,3}(t_{1},t_{2},x)&=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\cos((t_{1}+t_{2})|\xi|)}{\langle\xi\rangle^{4}}\chi_{K}^{2}(\xi)e^{i\xi\cdot x}d\xi\end{split}

for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
K\in\mathbb{N}
. By arguing as in (
5.43
)-(
5.46
) in Step 1 in the proof of Proposition
5.5
, we have

(5.71)

\displaystyle\bigg\|\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\setminus\{0\}}\partial^{\alpha}_{x}(f_{N,j}-f_{10,j})(t_{1},t_{2},x+2\pi k)\bigg\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}([-\pi,\pi)^{2})}\lesssim 1

for each
j\in\{1,2,3\}
and any multi-index
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
|\alpha|\leq 2
, uniformly in

0\leq t_{1},t_{2}\leq 1
. Therefore, since the functions
\mathbf{\Pi}_{\leq 10}^{2}F_{j}
and
f_{10,j}
are smooth, (
5.68
) follows from (
5.69
), (
5.71
) and the bound

(5.72)

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f_{N,j}(t_{1},t_{2},x)\big|\lesssim_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{H}_{N}(t_{1}-t_{2},x;s)+|x|^{-\varepsilon}

for any
x\in[-\pi,\pi)^{2}\cong\mathbb{T}^{2}
, any
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
1\leq|\alpha|\leq 2
,
j\in\{1,2,3\}
and all
s>1
and
\varepsilon>0
.

Fix
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
1\leq|\alpha|\leq 2
. We first consider the contribution of
f_{N,1}
. By Poisson’s formula (
2.24
)-(
2.25
), we have

(5.73)

\displaystyle f_{N,1}(t_{1},t_{2},\cdot)=W(|t_{1}-t_{2}|,\cdot)*G*\nu_{N},

where
G
is as in (
2.13
) and
\nu_{N}=\mathcal{F}_{x}^{-1}[\chi_{N}^{2}]
. Note that by integration by parts,
\nu_{N}
satisfies (
3.7
). Hence, (
5.72
) for
j=1
follows immediately from (
3.93
) and (
3.94
) in Lemma
3.12
.

Now, we look at the contribution of
f_{N,2}
and
f_{N,3}
. By proceeding as in (
5.52
) (where
\widehat{\eta}_{N}
is replaced with
\chi_{N}^{2}
) and from (
5.26
) and (
2.24
)-(
2.25
), we have

(5.74)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&f_{N,2}(t_{1},t_{2},x)\\
&\quad=2\pi(G*G*\nu_{N})(x)-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{0}^{|t_{1}-t_{2}|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\sin(t^{\prime}|\xi|)}{|\xi|}\frac{1}{\langle\xi\rangle^{2}}\frac{|\xi|^{2}}{\langle\xi\rangle^{2}}\,\chi_{N}^{2}(\xi)e^{i\xi\cdot x}d\xi dt^{\prime}\\
&\quad=2\pi(G*G*\nu_{N})(x)-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{0}^{|t_{1}-t_{2}|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\sin(t^{\prime}|\xi|)}{|\xi|}\frac{1}{\langle\xi\rangle^{2}}\,\chi_{N}^{2}(\xi)e^{i\xi\cdot x}d\xi dt^{\prime}\\
&\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{0}^{|t_{1}-t_{2}|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\sin(t^{\prime}|\xi|)}{|\xi|\langle\xi\rangle^{4}}\,\chi_{N}^{2}(\xi)e^{i\xi\cdot x}d\xi dt^{\prime}\\
&\quad=2\pi(G*G*\nu_{N})(x)-\int_{0}^{|t_{1}-t_{2}|}(W(t^{\prime},\cdot)*G*\nu_{N})(x)dt^{\prime}\\
&\qquad\qquad+2\pi\int_{0}^{|t_{1}-t_{2}|}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\sin(t^{\prime}|\xi|)}{|\xi|\langle\xi\rangle^{4}}\,\chi_{N}^{2}(\xi)e^{i\xi\cdot x}d\xi dt^{\prime}\\
&=:\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt(x)-\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}(x)+\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt I \hskip-8.25128pt I}(x).\end{split}

By (
3.11
) in Lemma
3.2
(ii), we have

(5.75)

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\hskip 0.56905pt\text{I}\hskip 0.56905pt(x)|\lesssim_{\varepsilon}|x|^{-\varepsilon}

for any
x\in[-\pi,\pi)^{2}
and
\varepsilon>0
. From Lemma
3.12
, we have

(5.76)

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}(x)|\lesssim\int_{0}^{|t_{1}-t_{2}|}|x|^{-\varepsilon}\cdot|t^{\prime}-|x||^{-1+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}dt^{\prime}\lesssim_{\varepsilon}|x|^{-\varepsilon}

for any
x\in[-\pi,\pi)^{2}
and
\varepsilon>0
. Since
I I I
decays rapidly on the Fourier side, we immediately have that

(5.77)

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\text{I \hskip-8.25128pt I \hskip-8.25128pt I}(x)|\lesssim 1

for any
x\in[-\pi,\pi)^{2}
. Therefore, by (
5.74
), (
5.75
), (
5.76
) and (
5.77
) we deduce that
f_{N,2}
satisfies (
5.72
). By arguing similarly, one shows that
f_{N,3}
also satisfies (
5.72
). This finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 5.12
.

Fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
and let
\Lambda_{N}
and
\mathcal{H}_{N}
be as in (
5.19
) and (
5.16
), respectively. Then, the following bound holds:

(5.78)

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\Lambda_{N}(t,x)\big|\lesssim_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{H}_{N}(t,x;s)+|x|^{-\varepsilon}

for any
(t,x)\in[0,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{2}
,
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
1\leq|\alpha|\leq 2
,
s>|\alpha|
and all
\varepsilon>0
, with an implicit constant independent of
N
.

Proof.

Fix
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
t\in[0,1]
. By arguing as in the proof of Lemma
5.11
, it suffices to prove the following estimate

(5.79)

\displaystyle\big|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}f_{N}(t,x)\big|\lesssim_{\varepsilon}\mathcal{H}_{N}(t,x;s)+|x|^{-\varepsilon}

for any
x\in[-\pi,\pi)^{2}\cong\mathbb{T}^{2}
,
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
1\leq|\alpha|\leq 2
,
s>|\alpha|
and all
\varepsilon>0
. Here,
f_{N}
is as in (
5.42
).

Fix
\alpha\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
1\leq|\alpha|\leq 2
. By proceeding as in (
5.52
) (where
\widehat{\eta}_{N}
is replaced with
\chi_{N}^{2}
), we have

(5.80)

\displaystyle f_{N}(t,x)=(G*\nu_{N})(t,x)-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\sin(t^{\prime}|\xi|)}{|\xi|}\cdot\frac{|\xi|^{2}}{\langle\xi\rangle^{2}}\,\chi_{N}^{2}(\xi)e^{i\xi\cdot x}d\xi dt^{\prime},

where
G
is as in (
2.13
) and
\nu_{N}=\mathcal{F}_{x}^{-1}[\chi_{N}^{2}]
. By arguing as in (
5.53
) and from (
5.26
) and (
2.24
)-(
2.25
), we have

(5.81)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\sin(t^{\prime}|\xi|)}{|\xi|}\cdot\frac{|\xi|^{2}}{\langle\xi\rangle^{2}}\chi_{N}^{2}(\xi)e^{i\xi\cdot x}d\xi dt^{\prime}\\
&\qquad=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\sin(t^{\prime}|\xi|)}{|\xi|}\chi_{N}^{2}(\xi)e^{i\xi\cdot x}d\xi dt^{\prime}-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\frac{\sin(t^{\prime}|\xi|)}{|\xi|\langle\xi\rangle^{2}}\chi_{N}^{2}(\xi)e^{i\xi\cdot x}d\xi dt^{\prime}\\
&\qquad=:A_{N}(t,x)-B_{N}(t,x)-\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}(t,x),\end{split}

where
I I
is as in (
5.74
) and
A_{N}
and
B_{N}
are given by

\displaystyle A_{N}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\big(\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}\log\big(t+\sqrt{t^{2}-|\cdot|^{2}}\big)\big)*\nu_{N},

\displaystyle B_{N}(t,x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi}\big(\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}\log|\cdot|\big)*\nu_{N}.

Combining (
5.80
) and (
5.81
) gives

(5.82)

\displaystyle f_{N}(t,x)=G_{N}(x)-A_{N}(t,x)+B_{N}(t,x)+\text{I \hskip-7.96677pt I}(t,x),

where
G_{N}=G*\nu_{N}
. Let us now fix
x\in[-\pi,\pi)^{2}
. We divide our analysis in several cases.

\bullet
Case 1:
|x|\gg t
.
In this case, we write

G_{N}(t,x)+B_{N}(t,x)=G_{N,t}(x)+F_{N,t}(x),

where
G_{N,t}
and
F_{N,t}
are the functions

\displaystyle G_{N,t}

\displaystyle=\big(\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)^{c}}G\big)*\nu_{N},

\displaystyle F_{N,t}

\displaystyle=\big(\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}\big(G+\frac{1}{2\pi}\log|\cdot|\big)\big)*\nu_{N}.

Note that by Corollary
3.9
(ii) and since the function
G+\frac{1}{2\pi}\log|\cdot|
is smooth on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, we have

(5.83)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}G_{N,t}(x)|+|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}F_{N,t}(x)|&\lesssim\bigl\langle\,\log\!\big(t+|x|+N^{-1}\big)\bigr\rangle\min\!\big\{N^{|\alpha|},|t-|x||^{-|\alpha|}\big\}\\
&\lesssim_{\varepsilon}\min\!\big\{N^{|\alpha|},(t+|x|)^{-\frac{1}{2}}|t-|x||^{\frac{1}{2}-|\alpha|}\big\}\\
&\lesssim_{s}\mathcal{H}_{N}(t,x;|\alpha|)\end{split}

for any
\varepsilon>0
, as
|x|\gg t
. Similarly, by applying Lemma
3.6
to the function
W_{t}=\log\big(t+\sqrt{|t^{2}-|\cdot|^{2}|}\,\big)
, we also have

(5.84)

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}A_{N}(t,x)|\lesssim_{s}\mathcal{H}_{N}(t,x;|\alpha|)

for any
\varepsilon>0
. Therefore (
5.79
) follows from (
5.82
), (
5.83
), (
5.84
) and (
5.76
) in this case.

\bullet
Case 2:
|x|\ll t
.
The proof of (
5.79
) in this case is identical to that of Case 1 and we omit details.

\bullet
Case 3:
|x|\sim t
.
Here, we first consider the case
|\alpha|=1
. Then, by Lemma
3.5
, we have that

(5.85)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}A_{N}(t,x)&=\big(\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\big\{\log\big(t+\sqrt{|t^{2}-|\cdot|^{2}|}\big)\big)\big\}\big)*\nu_{N}\\
&\qquad\quad-\log(t)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{1}(t)}\nu_{N}(x-y)\alpha\cdot y\,d\sigma_{t}(y)\\
&=:A_{N}^{\alpha}(t,x)-\log(t)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{1}(t)}\nu_{N}(x-y)\alpha\cdot y\,d\sigma_{t}(y),\\
\partial_{x}^{\alpha}B_{N}(t,x)&=\big(\mathbf{1}_{B(0,t)}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\{\log|\cdot|\}\big)*\nu_{N}\\
&\qquad\quad-\log(t)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{1}(t)}\nu_{N}(x-y)\alpha\cdot y\,d\sigma_{t}(y)\\
&=:B_{N}^{\alpha}(t,x)-\log(t)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{1}(t)}\nu_{N}(x-y)\alpha\cdot y\,d\sigma_{t}(y).\end{split}

Thus, the contribution of the boundary terms in (
5.85
) to (
5.82
) vanish and by Lemmas
3.2
(i) and
3.3
, we have

(5.86)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}A_{N}(t,x)-\partial_{x}^{\alpha}B_{N}(t,x)|=|A_{N}^{\alpha}(t,x)-B_{N}^{\alpha}(t,x)|\\
&\qquad\lesssim\min\!\big\{N,\big|t^{2}-|x|^{2}\big|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big\}\,\bigl\langle\,\log\!\big(\!\min\!\big\{N,|t-|x||^{-1}\big\}\big)\bigr\rangle+\big(|x|+N^{-1}\big)^{-1}\\
&\qquad\lesssim_{s}\mathcal{H}_{N}(t,x;s)\end{split}

for any
s>1
, as
|x|\sim t
. Therefore, (
5.79
) for
|\alpha|=1
follows from (
5.82
), (
5.86
), the bound on
G_{N}
provided by Lemma
3.2
(i) (using
|x|\sim t
) and (
5.76
).

Now assume
|\alpha|=2
and write
\alpha=\alpha_{1}+\alpha_{2}
for
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}_{\geq 0}
with
|\alpha_{1}|=|\alpha_{2}|=1
. From Lemma
3.11
, we have

(5.87)

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}A_{N}^{\alpha_{1}}(t,x)|\lesssim_{s}\mathcal{H}_{N}(t,x;s)

and

(5.88)
