\displaystyle\begin{split}|\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}B_{N}^{\alpha_{1}}(t,x)|&\lesssim\big(\!\min\!\big\{N^{2},|x|^{-1}|t-|x||^{-1}\big\}+\min\!\big\{N^{2},|x|^{-2}\big\}\big)\\
&\qquad\qquad\times\bigl\langle\,\log\!\big(\!\min\!\big\{N,|x|^{-1},|t-|x||^{-1}\big\}\big)\bigr\rangle\\
&\lesssim\min\!\big\{N^{2},(t+|x|)^{-\frac{1}{2}}|t-|x||^{-\frac{3}{2}}\big\}\bigl\langle\,\log\!\big(\!\min\!\big\{N,|t-|x||^{-1}\big\}\big)\bigr\rangle\\
&\lesssim_{s}\mathcal{H}_{N}(t,x;s)\end{split}

for any
s>2
, as
|x|\sim t
. Thus, by (
5.85
), (
5.86
), (
5.87
) and (
5.88
), we have that

(5.89)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}A_{N}(t,x)-\partial_{x}^{\alpha}B_{N}(t,x)|&=|\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}A_{N}^{\alpha_{1}}(t,x)-\partial_{x}^{\alpha_{2}}B_{N}^{\alpha_{1}}(t,x)|\\
&\lesssim_{s}\mathcal{H}_{N}(t,x;s)\end{split}

for any
s>2
. Therefore, (
5.79
) for
|\alpha|=2
follows from (
5.82
), (
5.89
), the bound on
G_{N}
provided by Lemma
3.2
(i) (using
|x|\sim t
) and (
5.76
).
∎

5.3.
Imaginary Gaussian multiplicative chaos

In this subsection,
we establish various regularity properties
of the (truncated) imaginary Gaussian multiplicative chaos
\Theta_{N}^{\varepsilon_{0}}
defined in (
1.34
). Recall the definitions of the space-time localizations in (
2.42
), (
2.42
), (
2.43
), (
2.43
) and (
2.43
). For
\alpha,\varepsilon>0
, we define the space
\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha,\varepsilon}([0,1])
by the norm

(5.90)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\Theta\|_{\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha,\varepsilon}([0,1])}&:=\|\mathbf{P}_{\textup{lo}}\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\Theta\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}}+\|\mathfrak{q}_{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{hi}}\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\Theta\|_{Y_{-\frac{1}{2}-3\varepsilon}^{-\alpha,-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}\\
&\qquad\qquad+\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\Theta\|_{\Lambda_{\infty}^{-\alpha,-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}}+\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\Theta\|_{\Lambda_{\frac{1+\varepsilon}{\varepsilon}}^{-\alpha-\frac{1}{2},0}}.\end{split}

We emphasize that the restriction here is as in Remark
2.5
.

The main result of this subsection is as follows.

Proposition 5.13
.

Let
0<\beta^{2}<4\pi
,
\varepsilon_{0}\in\{+1,-1\}
. Then, for any
\varepsilon>0
and
\alpha>\frac{\beta^{2}}{4\pi}-\frac{1}{2}+2\varepsilon
,
\{\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is a Cauchy sequence in
\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha,\varepsilon}([0,1])
,
\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P}
-almost surely. We denote by
\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}
its limit.

In the remainder of this section, we establish Proposition
5.13
. Its proof is a straightforward consequence of the following results.

Lemma 5.14
.

Let
0<\beta^{2}<4\pi
,
\alpha>\frac{\beta^{2}}{4\pi}
and
\varepsilon_{0}\in\{+1,-1\}
. Then,
\{\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is a Cauchy sequence in
C([0,1];W^{-\alpha,\infty}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))
,
\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

The proof of Lemma
5.14
can be found in
[
99
, Proposition 5.7]
. See also
[
76
, Lemma 2.2]
and
[
75
, Proposition 1.1]
.

The following two propositions establish nonlinear smoothing
for the imaginary Gaussian multiplicative chaos.

Proposition 5.15
.

Let
0<\beta^{2}<6\pi
and
\varepsilon_{0}\in\{+1,-1\}
. Then, for any small
\varepsilon>0
and

\alpha>\frac{\beta^{2}}{4\pi}-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon
,
\{\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is a Cauchy sequence in
\Lambda^{-\alpha,-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}_{\infty}([0,1])
,
\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

Proposition
5.15
is proved in Subsection
5.4
.

Proposition 5.16
.

Let
0<\beta^{2}<4\pi
and
\varepsilon_{0}\in\{+1,-1\}
. Then, for any small
\varepsilon=>0
and
{\alpha>\frac{\beta^{2}}{4\pi}-\frac{1}{2}+2\varepsilon}
,
\{\mathfrak{q}_{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{hi}}\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
is a Cauchy sequence in
Y_{-\frac{1}{2}-3\varepsilon}^{-\alpha,-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}([0,1])
,
\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P}
-almost surely.

The main step in the proof of Proposition
5.16
is the following pointwise moment estimate. For
x=(x^{1},x^{2})\in\mathbb{T}^{2}
, we denote by
\partial_{x^{\ell}}
for
\ell\in\{1,2\}
the derivative with respect to the
\ell^{\text{th}}
coordinate of
x

Proposition 5.17
.

Let
0<\beta^{2}<4\pi
,
\varepsilon_{0}\in\{+1,-1\}
,
N_{0}\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}
and
(N,N_{1},N_{2})\in\mathbb{N}^{3}
with
N_{2}\geq N_{1}
. Then, the following bounds holds:

(5.91)

\displaystyle\max_{\ell\in\{1,2\}}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\big|\big(\Box^{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\,\partial_{x^{\ell}}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N})\big)(t,x)\big|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\varepsilon}N_{0}^{\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}+\varepsilon}\langle t\rangle^{4\varepsilon},

(5.92)

\displaystyle\max_{\ell\in\{1,2\}}\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\big|\big(\Box^{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\,\partial_{x^{\ell}}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}(\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N_{1}}-\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N_{2}})\big)(t,x)\big|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\varepsilon}N_{0}^{6}\,\langle t\rangle^{4\varepsilon}N_{1}^{-\delta}

for any small
\varepsilon>0
and small
\delta>0
, with implicit constants independent of
N
,
N_{1}
,
N_{2}
and
N_{0}
. Here,
\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}
is the indicator function of the time interval
[0,1]
and
\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}
is as in (
2.38
).

5.4.
Proof of Proposition
5.15

In this subsection, we present a proof of Proposition
5.15
.
We first state
a charge cancellation lemma adapted to the time-dependent damped wave setting.
Given
N\in\mathbb{N}
, we introduce the potential
function
\mathcal{J}_{N}
by

(5.93)

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{N}(t,x)=\big(|t|+|x|+N^{-1}\big),\quad(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}.

We state in the next lemma the key charge cancellation identity observed in
[
52
,
75
]
adapted to our setting.

Lemma 5.18
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
p\in\mathbb{N}
, and
\lambda>0
.
Let
\{\varepsilon_{j}\}_{j=1,...,2p}\in\{\pm 1\}^{2p}
be a collection of signs such that

\varepsilon_{j}=1
if
j
is even and
\varepsilon_{j}=-1
if
j
is odd. Then, the following estimate holds:

(5.94)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\prod_{1\leq j<k\leq 2p}\mathcal{J}_{N}\big(z_{j}-z_{k}\big)^{\varepsilon_{j}\varepsilon_{k}\lambda}\lesssim\max_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{p}}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\mathcal{J}_{N}\big(z_{2j}-z_{2\sigma(j)-1}\big)^{-\lambda},\end{split}

for any set of
2p
space-time points
\{z_{j}=(t_{j},x_{j})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}:1\leq j\leq 2p\}
,
where
\mathfrak{S}_{p}
denotes the symmetric group on
\{1,\dots,p\}
.

Proof.

The proof follows from Proposition
5.5
and a slight variation of the presentation in
[
75
]
. We omit details.

∎

We now present a proof of Proposition
5.15
.

Proof of Proposition
5.15
.

Fix
0<\beta^{2}<6\pi
and let
p\in\mathbb{N}
, finite
q\geq 1

and
\alpha>\frac{\beta^{2}}{4\pi}-\frac{1}{2}
.
Without loss of generality, we assume
\alpha<2
in the following. In this proof, we fix
\varepsilon_{0}=+
for convenience and write
\Theta_{N}
for
\Theta^{+}_{N}
.

\bullet
Step
I
: boundedness.
Fix small
\delta>0
(to be chosen later).
From (
2.49
), Sobolev’s inequality (with
q\delta>2
),
and (
2.21
),
we have

(5.95)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\Theta_{N}&\|_{L^{2p}(\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P})\Lambda^{-\alpha,-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}_{\infty}([0,1])}=\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\cdot\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{2p}(\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P})\Lambda^{-\alpha,-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}_{\infty}(\mathbb{R})}\\
&\lesssim\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\cdot\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{2p}(\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P})\Lambda^{-\alpha+\delta,-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon+\delta}_{q}(\mathbb{R})}\\
&\lesssim\big\|J^{(t)}_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}*_{t}\langle\nabla_{x}\rangle^{-\alpha+\delta}(\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\cdot\Theta_{N})\big\|_{L^{2p}_{\omega}L^{q}_{t,x}}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{2}\big\|J^{(t),j}_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}*_{t}\langle\nabla_{x}\rangle^{-\alpha+\delta}(\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\cdot\Theta_{N})\big\|_{L^{2p}(\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P})L^{q}_{t,x}}\\
&=:\sum_{j=1}^{2}A_{N,j},\end{split}

where
*_{t}
denotes a convolution in the temporal variable
and

(5.96)

\displaystyle J^{(t),1}_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}=\mathbf{1}_{|t|\leq 3}\cdot J^{(t)}_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}\qquad\text{and}\qquad J^{(t),2}_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}=\mathbf{1}_{|t|>3}\cdot J^{(t)}_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta},

where
J^{(t)}_{b}
is as in (
2.21
).

We first estimate
A_{N,1}

on the right-hand side of (
5.95
).
By Minkowski’s inequality (with
2p\geq q
)
with (
5.96
), we have

(5.97)

\displaystyle A_{N,1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big\|\|J^{(t),1}_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}*_{t}\langle\nabla_{x}\rangle^{-\alpha+\delta}(\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\cdot\Theta_{N})(t,x)\|_{L^{2p}(\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P})}\Big\|_{L^{q}_{t}([-3,4];L^{q}_{x})}.

Fix
t\in[-3,4]
and
x\in\mathbb{T}^{2}
.
Then,
from (
2.18
)
and
(
1.34
),
we have

(5.98)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}_{\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P}}&\Big[\big|J^{(t),1}_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}*_{t}\langle\nabla\rangle^{-\alpha+\delta}(\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\cdot\Theta_{N})(t,x)\big|^{2p}\Big]\\
&=e^{p\beta^{2}\sigma_{N}}\mathbb{E}_{\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Bigg[\bigg|\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}J_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}^{(t),1}(t-s)J_{\alpha-\delta}(x-y)e^{i\beta\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{N}(s,y)}dyds\bigg|^{2p}\Bigg]\\
&=e^{p\beta^{2}\sigma_{N}}\int_{[0,1]^{2p}}\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2p}}\mathbb{E}_{\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P}}\bigg[e^{i\beta\sum_{j=1}^{p}(\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{N}(s_{2j},y_{2j})-\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{N}(s_{2j-1},y_{2j-1}))}\bigg]\\
&\hphantom{XXXXX}\times\prod_{k=1}^{2p}J_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}^{(t),1}(t-s_{k})J_{\alpha-\delta}(x-y_{k})\,d\vec{y}d\vec{s},\end{split}

where

d\vec{s}:=ds_{1}\cdots ds_{2p}

and

d\vec{y}:=dy_{1}\cdots dy_{2p}
.
Noting that
\sum_{j=1}^{p}(\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{N}(s_{2j},y_{2j})-\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{N}(s_{2j-1},y_{2j-1}))
is a mean-zero Gaussian random variable,
the explicit formula for the characteristic function of a Gaussian random variable yields

(5.99)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\bigg[e^{i\beta\sum_{j=1}^{p}(\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{N}(s_{2j},y_{2j})-\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{N}(s_{2j-1},y_{2j-1}))}\bigg]\\
&\quad=e^{-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}\big[|\sum_{j=1}^{p}(\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{N}(s_{2j},y_{2j})-\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{N}(s_{2j-1},y_{2j-1}))|^{2}\big]}.\end{split}

Let
\{\varepsilon_{j}\}_{j=1,...,2p}
be as in Lemma
5.18
.
Then, we can rewrite the expectation in the exponent on the right-hand side
of (
5.99
) as

(5.100)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{2p}\varepsilon_{j}\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{N}(s_{j},y_{j})\Big|^{2}\bigg]=\sum_{j,k=1}^{2p}\varepsilon_{j}\varepsilon_{k}\Gamma_{N}(s_{j}-s_{k},y_{j}-y_{k}),

where
\Gamma_{N}
is the space-time covariance defined in (
1.45
).
From (
1.31
), we have
\Gamma_{N}(0,0)=\sigma_{N}+O(1)
,
and thus

(5.101)

\eqref{E1}\sim e^{-p\beta^{2}\sigma_{N}}e^{-\beta^{2}\sum_{1\leq j<k\leq 2p}\varepsilon_{j}\varepsilon_{k}\Gamma_{N}(s_{j}-s_{k},y_{j}-y_{k})}.

Then, from (
5.99
), (
5.101
),
the two-sided bound (
5.13
) in Proposition
5.5
,
and Lemma
5.18
, we obtain

(5.102)

\begin{split}e^{p\beta^{2}\sigma_{N}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[&e^{i\beta\sum_{j=1}^{p}(\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{N}(s_{2j},y_{2j})-\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{N}(s_{2j-1},y_{2j-1}))}\bigg]\\
&\sim\prod_{1\leq j<k\leq 2p}\big(|s_{j}-s_{k}|+|y_{j}-y_{k}|+N^{-1}\big)^{\varepsilon_{j}\varepsilon_{k}\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}\\
&\lesssim\max_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{p}}\prod_{1\leq j\leq p}\big(|s_{2j}-s_{2\sigma(j)-1}|+|y_{2j}-y_{2\sigma(j)-1}|+N^{-1}\big)^{-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}\\
&\leq\sum_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{p}}\prod_{1\leq j\leq p}\big(|s_{2j}-s_{2\sigma(j)-1}|+|y_{2j}-y_{2\tau(j)-1}|+N^{-1}\big)^{-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}.\end{split}

Hence, from (
5.98
) and (
5.102
)
we obtain

(5.103)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}&\Big[\big|J^{(t),1}_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}*_{t}\langle\nabla\rangle^{-\alpha+\delta}(\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\cdot\Theta_{N})(t,x)\big|^{2p}\Big]\\
&\lesssim\sum_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{p}}\int_{[0,1]^{2p}}\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2p}}\prod_{1\leq j\leq p}\big(|s_{2j}-s_{2\sigma(j)-1}|+|y_{2j}-y_{2\sigma(j)-1}|+N^{-1}\big)^{-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}\\
&\hphantom{XXXX}\times\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{2p}|J_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}^{(t),1}(t-s_{k})||J_{\alpha-\delta}(x-y_{k})|\bigg)d\vec{y}d\vec{s}.\end{split}

In the following, we fix
\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{p}
.
Then, it suffices to bound each pair of integrals:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\big(|s_{j}-s_{k}|+|y_{j}-y_{k}|+N^{-1}\big)^{-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{XXXXX}\times\bigg(\prod_{\ell\in\{j,k\}}|J_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}^{(t),1}(t-s_{\ell})||J_{\alpha-\delta}(x-y_{\ell})|\bigg)dy_{j}dy_{k}ds_{j}ds_{k}

for an even integer
j=2,...,2p
and
k=2\sigma(\tfrac{j}{2})-1
.
From (
2.20
), (
2.22
), and (
5.96
) with
0<\alpha-\delta<2
,
we can bound this integral by

(5.104)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\big(|s_{j}-s_{k}|+|y_{j}-y_{k}|+N^{-1}\big)^{-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}\\
&\hphantom{XXXX}\times\bigg(\prod_{\ell\in\{j,k\}}|t-s_{\ell}|^{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}|x-y_{\ell}|^{\alpha-\delta-2}\bigg)dy_{j}dy_{k}ds_{j}ds_{k}\\
&\leq\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\big(|s_{j}-s_{k}|+|y_{j}-y_{k}|\big)^{-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}\\
&\hphantom{XXXX}\times\bigg(\prod_{\ell\in\{j,k\}}|t-s_{\ell}|^{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}|x-y_{\ell}|^{\alpha-\delta-2}\bigg)dy_{j}dy_{k}ds_{j}ds_{k},\end{split}

uniformly in
N\in\mathbb{N}
.

\bullet

Case 1:

|y_{j}-y_{k}|\sim|x-y_{k}|\gtrsim|x-y_{j}|
.
The symmetry allows us to handle the case

|y_{j}-y_{k}|\sim|x-y_{j}|\gtrsim|x-y_{k}|
.

We first consider the case

|s_{j}-s_{k}|\sim|t-s_{k}|\gtrsim|t-s_{j}|
.
In this case, we have

(5.105)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}|t-s_{k}|^{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}|t-s_{k}|^{-1+2\varepsilon+3\delta}\int_{|t-s_{j}|\lesssim|t-s_{k}|}|t-s_{j}|^{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}ds_{j}ds_{k}\lesssim 1

and thus

(5.106)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{RHS of \eqref{E4}}&\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}|x-y_{k}|^{\alpha-\delta-2-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}+1-2\varepsilon-3\delta}\int_{|x-y_{j}|\lesssim|x-y_{k}|}|x-y_{j}|^{\alpha-\delta-2}dy_{j}dy_{k}\\
&\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}|x-y_{k}|^{2\alpha-1-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}-2\varepsilon-5\delta}dy_{k}\lesssim 1,\end{split}

provided that
\alpha>\frac{\beta^{2}}{4\pi}-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon

(by choosing
\delta>0
sufficiently small).
By symmetry,
the same conclusion holds when

|s_{j}-s_{k}|\sim|t-s_{j}|\gtrsim|t-s_{k}|
.

Next, we consider the case

|t-s_{j}|\sim|t-s_{k}|\gg|s_{j}-s_{k}|
.
In this case, we have

(5.107)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}|t-s_{k}|^{-1-2\varepsilon-2\delta}\int_{|s_{j}-s_{k}|\lesssim|t-s_{k}|}|s_{j}-s_{k}|^{-1+2\varepsilon+3\delta}ds_{j}ds_{k}\lesssim 1.

Then, (
5.106
) holds,
provided that
\alpha>\frac{\beta^{2}}{4\pi}-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon

(by choosing
\delta>0
sufficiently small).

\bullet

Case 2:

|x-y_{j}|\sim|x-y_{k}|\gtrsim|y_{j}-y_{k}|
.
From (
5.105
) and (
5.107
), we have

(5.108)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\text{RHS of \eqref{E4}}&\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}|x-y_{k}|^{2(\alpha-\delta)-4}\int_{|y_{j}-y_{k}|\lesssim|x-y_{k}|}|y_{j}-y_{k}|^{-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}+1-2\varepsilon-3\delta}dy_{j}dy_{k}\\
&\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}|x-y_{k}|^{2\alpha-1-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}-2\varepsilon-5\delta}dy_{k}\lesssim 1,\end{split}

provided that
\alpha>\frac{\beta^{2}}{4\pi}-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon

and
\beta^{2}<6\pi-4\pi\varepsilon

(by choosing
\delta>0
sufficiently small).

Hence, (
5.103
) and
the estimates on (
5.104
), we obtain

(5.109)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\Big[\big|J^{(t),1}_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}*_{t}\langle\nabla\rangle^{-\alpha+\delta}(\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\cdot\Theta_{N})(t,x)\big|^{2p}\Big]\lesssim 1,

uniformly in
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
provided that

(5.110)

\displaystyle\alpha>\frac{\beta^{2}}{4\pi}-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon\qquad\text{and}\qquad\beta^{2}<6\pi-4\pi\varepsilon

(by choosing
\delta>0
sufficiently small).
Putting together (
5.97
) and (
5.109
),
we conclude that

(5.111)

\displaystyle A_{N,1}\lesssim 1,

uniformly in
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
under the same condition.

Let us now briefly discuss how to handle
A_{N,2}

on the right-hand side of (
5.95
). We observe from (
2.22
) that
|J_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}^{(t),2}(t-s)|\lesssim e^{-|t|}
for
s\in[0,1]
. Hence, by proceeding as in the case of
A_{N,1}
, we have

(5.112)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}&\Big[\big|J^{(t),2}_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}*_{t}\langle\nabla\rangle^{-\alpha+\delta}(\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\cdot\Theta_{N})(t,x)\big|^{2p}\Big]\\
&\lesssim\sum_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{p}}\int_{[0,1]^{2p}}\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2p}}\prod_{1\leq j\leq p}\big(|s_{2j}-s_{2\sigma(j)-1}|+|y_{2j}-y_{2\sigma(j)-1}|+N^{-1}\big)^{-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}\\
&\hphantom{XXXXXXX}\times\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{2p}|J_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}^{(t),2}(t-s_{k})||J_{\alpha-\delta}(x-y_{k})|\bigg)d\vec{y}d\vec{s}\\
&\lesssim e^{-2p|t|}\sum_{\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{p}}\int_{[0,1]^{2p}}\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2p}}\prod_{1\leq j\leq p}\big(|s_{2j}-s_{2\sigma(j)-1}|+|y_{2j}-y_{2\sigma(j)-1}|+N^{-1}\big)^{-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}\\
&\hphantom{XXXXXXX}\times\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{2p}|J_{\alpha-\delta}(x-y_{k})|\bigg)d\vec{y}d\vec{s}\\
&\lesssim e^{-2p|t|},\end{split}

uniformly in
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
provided that
\alpha>\frac{\beta^{2}}{4\pi}-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon

and
\beta^{2}<6\pi-4\pi\varepsilon

(by choosing
\delta>0
sufficiently small). This is due to the fact that the integrand on the right-hand-side of second-to-last line of (
5.112
) is less singular than than that of (
5.104
).

Therefore, from (
5.96
) and Minkowski’s inequality,
we have

(5.113)

\displaystyle\begin{split}A_{N,2}&\lesssim\big\|J^{(t),2}_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}*_{t}\langle\nabla_{x}\rangle^{-\alpha+\delta}(\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\cdot\Theta_{N})\big\|_{L^{2p}(\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P})L^{q}_{x}L^{q}_{t}}\\
&\lesssim\Big\|\|J^{(t),2}_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}*_{t}\langle\nabla_{x}\rangle^{-\alpha+\delta}(\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\cdot\Theta_{N})(t,x)\|_{L^{2p}(\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P})}\Big\|_{L^{q}_{t}(\mathbb{R};L^{q}_{x})}\\
&\lesssim\|e^{-2p|t|}\|_{L^{q}_{t}}\\
&\lesssim 1.\end{split}

uniformly in
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
provided that
\alpha>\frac{\beta^{2}}{4\pi}-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon

and
\beta^{2}<6\pi-4\pi\varepsilon
.

Therefore, from (
5.95
), (
5.111
), and (
5.113
),
we obtain

(5.114)

\displaystyle\|\Theta_{N}\|_{L^{2p}(\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P})\Lambda^{-\alpha,-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}_{\infty}([0,1])}\lesssim 1.

uniformly in
N\in\mathbb{N}
,
under the condition (
5.110
).

\bullet
Step
I I
: convergence.
Next, we discuss convergence of
\Theta_{N}
.
We first estimate the contribution
from

J^{(t),1}_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}

in (
5.96
).
Let
N_{2}\geq N_{1}\geq 1
.
By
repeating the computation in Step
I
with
p=1
and
\Theta_{N_{1}}-\Theta_{N_{2}}
in place of
\Theta_{N}
,
we have

(5.115)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{E}&\Big[\big|J^{(t),1}_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}*_{t}\langle\nabla\rangle^{-\alpha+\delta}\big\{\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}(\Theta_{N_{1}}-\Theta_{N_{2}})\big\}(t,x)\big|^{2}\Big]\\
&=\int_{[0,1]^{2}}\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}}J^{(t),1}_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}(t-s_{1})J^{(t)}_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}(t-s_{2})J_{\alpha-\delta}(x-y_{1})J_{\alpha-\delta}(x-y_{2})\\
&\hphantom{XXXX}\times\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Big(e^{\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}\sigma_{N_{1}}}e^{i\beta\Psi_{N_{1}}(s_{1},y_{1})}-e^{\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}\sigma_{N_{2}}}e^{i\beta\Psi_{N_{2}}(s_{1},y_{1})}\Big)\\
&\hphantom{XXXX}\times\Big(e^{\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}\sigma_{N_{1}}}e^{-i\beta\Psi_{N_{1}}(s_{2},y_{2})}-e^{\frac{\beta^{2}}{2}\sigma_{N_{2}}}e^{-i\beta\Psi_{N_{2}}(s_{2},y_{2})}\Big)\bigg]d\vec{y}d\vec{s}\\
&=\sum_{j=1}^{2}\int_{[0,1]^{2}}\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}}\bigg(\prod_{k=1}^{2}J^{(t),1}_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}(t-s_{k})J_{\alpha-\delta}(x-y_{k})\bigg)\\
&\hphantom{XXXX}\times\Big(e^{\beta^{2}\Gamma_{N_{j}}(s_{1}-s_{2},y_{1}-y_{2})}-e^{\beta^{2}\Gamma_{N_{1},N_{2}}(s_{1}-s_{2},y_{1}-y_{2})}\Big)d\vec{y}d\vec{s},\end{split}

where
\Gamma_{N_{1},N_{2}}
is as in (
5.11
).
Given
\delta>0
, there exists
C_{\delta}>0
such that

(5.116)

\displaystyle|\log y|\leq C_{\delta}y^{-\delta}

for any
0<y\lesssim 1
.
Then,
by the fundamental theorem of calculus
and (
5.15
) in Proposition
5.5

with (
5.116
),
we have

(5.117)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big|e^{\beta^{2}\Gamma_{N_{j}}(t,x)}-e^{\beta^{2}\Gamma_{N_{1},N_{2}}(t,x)}\Big|\\
&\quad=\bigg|\int_{0}^{1}\beta^{2}\exp\Big(\beta^{2}\big(\tau\Gamma_{N_{j}}(t,x)+(1-\tau)\Gamma_{N_{1},N_{2}}(t,x)\big)\Big)d\tau\\
&\hphantom{XXXX}\times\big(\Gamma_{N_{j}}(t,x)-\Gamma_{N_{1},N_{2}}(t,x)\big)\bigg|\\
&\quad\lesssim\big(|t|+|x|+N_{2}^{-1}\big)^{-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}\Big\{\big(|t|+|x|+N_{2}^{-1}\big)^{-\delta}\wedge\big(N_{1}^{-\frac{1}{2}}|x|^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big)+O(N_{1}^{-1})\Big\}\\
&\quad\lesssim N_{1}^{-\delta}|x|^{-2\delta}\big(|t|+|x|+N_{2}^{-1}\big)^{-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}.\end{split}

Hence, from
proceeding as in Step
I
with (
5.117
),
we obtain

(5.118)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|J^{(t),1}_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}*_{t}\langle\nabla\rangle^{-\alpha+\delta}\big\{\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}(\Theta_{N_{1}}-\Theta_{N_{2}})\big\}(t,x)\big|^{2}\Big]\\
&\quad\lesssim N_{1}^{-\delta}\int_{[0,1]^{2}}\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}}|y_{j}-y_{k}|^{-2\delta}\big(|s_{j}-s_{k}|+|y_{j}-y_{k}|\big)^{-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}\\
&\hphantom{XXXX}\times\bigg(\prod_{\ell\in\{j,k\}}|t-s_{\ell}|^{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}|x-y_{\ell}|^{\alpha-\delta-2}\bigg)d\vec{y}d\vec{s}\\
&\quad\lesssim N_{1}^{-\delta}\end{split}

for any
N_{2}\geq N_{1}\geq 1
and
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}
,
provided that
(
5.110
) holds
(and for
\delta>0
sufficiently small).

Fix
p\geq 1
.
By interpolating (
5.118
) with (
5.109
),
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\big|J^{(t),1}_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}*_{t}\langle\nabla\rangle^{-\alpha+\delta}\big\{\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}(\Theta_{N_{1}}-\Theta_{N_{2}})\big\}(t,x)\big|^{p}\Big]\lesssim N_{1}^{-\delta}

for any
N_{2}\geq N_{1}\geq 1
and
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}
,
provided that
(
5.110
) holds
(and for
\delta>0
sufficiently small).
A similar (but simpler) computation
allows us to bound the contribution from

J^{(t),2}_{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}
in (
5.96
),
and
therefore, we conclude that

\|\Theta_{N_{1}}-\Theta_{N_{2}}\|_{L^{p}(\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P})\Lambda^{-\alpha,-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}_{\infty}([0,1])}\leq N_{1}^{-\tfrac{\delta}{p}}.

Namely,

\Theta_{N}
is a Cauchy sequence in
L^{p}(\Omega;\Lambda^{-\alpha,-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}_{\infty}([0,1])
. This concludes the proof of Proposition
5.15
.
∎

Remark 5.19
.

In the case of the heat equation,
the space-time covariance of the associated
stochastic convolution is given by

(5.119)

\displaystyle\Gamma_{N}^{\text{heat}}(t_{1}-t_{2},x_{1}-x_{2})\approx-\frac{1}{2\pi}\log\big(|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}+|x_{1}-x_{2}|+N^{-1}\big).

See, for example,
[
52
, Lemmas 3.7 and 3.8]
.
Compare this with
(
5.13
) in Proposition
5.5
.
By repeating the proof of Proposition
5.15
,
the main goal is then to bound

(5.120)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{1}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\big(|s_{j}-s_{k}|^{\frac{1}{2}}+|y_{j}-y_{k}|\big)^{-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}\\
&\hphantom{XXXX}\times\bigg(\prod_{\ell\in\{j,k\}}|t-s_{\ell}|^{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon-\delta}|x-y_{\ell}|^{\alpha-\delta-2}\bigg)dy_{j}dy_{k}ds_{j}ds_{k},\end{split}

where there is an extra
\frac{1}{2}
-power
on
|s_{j}-s_{k}|
as compared to (
5.104
). By adapting the computations in the proof above (see in particular Cases 1 and 2), one observes that
\{\Theta_{N}\}_{N\in\mathbb{N}}
converges in the anisotropic space
L^{p}(\Omega,\Lambda^{-\alpha,-b}_{\infty}([0,1]))
for
\alpha,b>0
if the condition

\alpha+2b>\frac{\beta^{2}}{4\pi}\quad\text{and}\quad\beta^{2}<8\pi

is met. See
[
52
, Theorem 2.1]
for a construction of the imaginary Gaussian multiplicative chaos in isotropic spaces.

Remark 5.20
.

Let us now consider the case
\beta^{2}\geq 6\pi
.
Given a test function
\phi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{T}^{2})\setminus\{0\}
,
it follows from a slight modification of
the computation in the proof of
Proposition
5.15

that

\displaystyle\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\bigg|\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\phi(t,x)\Theta_{N}(t,x)dxdt\bigg|^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\quad=\lim_{N\to\infty}\int_{(\mathbb{R}_{+})^{2}}\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}}\phi(t_{1},x_{1})\overline{\phi(t_{2},x_{2})}\mathbb{E}[\Theta_{N}(t_{1},x_{1})\overline{\Theta_{N}(t_{2},x_{2})}]dx_{1}dx_{2}dt_{1}dt_{2}

\displaystyle\quad\sim\lim_{N\to\infty}\int_{(\mathbb{R}_{+})^{2}}\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}}\phi(t_{1},x_{1})\overline{\phi(t_{2},x_{2})}

\displaystyle\hphantom{XXXXXXXXXX}\times\big(|t_{1}-t_{2}|+|x_{1}-x_{2}|+N^{-1}\big)^{-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}dx_{1}dx_{2}dt_{1}dt_{2}

\displaystyle\quad=\infty

for
\beta^{2}\geq 6\pi
,
since

\big(|t|+|x|\big)^{-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}

is not locally integrable in this case.
In particular,
the truncated imaginary Gaussian multiplicative chaos

\Theta_{N}
does not converge even as a space-time distribution
when
\beta^{2}\geq 6\pi
.

5.5.
A Sobolev type lemma

We first introduce some notations. Let
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
with
0<\beta^{2}<4\pi
. We define the function
f_{N}=f_{N,\beta}
on
(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{2})^{4}
by

(5.121)

\displaystyle f_{N}({\mathbf{z}_{1}},{\mathbf{z}_{2}})=\exp\Big(-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}\Gamma_{N}(t_{1},t_{2},y_{1}-y_{2})\Big)

for every
\mathbf{z}_{j}=(t_{j},y_{j})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}
,
1=1,2
. Here,
\Gamma_{N}
is as in (
1.45
).

Given a function
f:(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}\mapsto\mathbb{R}
and
N_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
, define the function
\mathfrak{F}_{N_{0}}[f]
on
(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}
:

(5.122)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{N_{0}}[f](z_{1},z_{2})=\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}}dy_{j}\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{1}-y_{1})\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{2}-y_{2})f(\mathbf{z}_{1},\mathbf{z}_{2})dy_{1}dy_{2}

for any
z_{j}=(t_{j},x_{j})\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}
,
j=1,2
. In (
5.122
),
\mathbf{z}_{j}=(t_{j},y_{j})
for any
j=1,2
and
\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}
denotes the convolution kernel associated to the spatial frequency projection
\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}
defined in (
2.38
).

Let
\theta:(0,4\pi)\to\mathbb{R}_{+}^{*}
be the function given by

(5.123)

\displaystyle\theta(\beta^{2})=\begin{cases}\frac{3}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}&\quad\text{if $\beta^{2}\in[2\pi,3\pi)$},\\
2-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}&\quad\text{if $\beta^{2}\in[3\pi,4\pi)$}.\end{cases}

Recall that for
x=(x^{1},x^{2})\in\mathbb{T}^{2}
, we denote by
\partial_{x^{\ell}}
for
\ell\in\{1,2\}
the derivative with respect to the
\ell^{\text{th}}
coordinate of
x
Here, it will also be convenient to use the following notations:
|z|_{+}=|t|+|x|_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}
and
|z|_{-}=||t|-|x|_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}|
for a space-time point
z=(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}
.

The goal of this subsection is to bound the expression
\partial_{x_{1}^{\ell}}\partial_{x_{2}^{\ell}}\mathfrak{F}_{N_{0}}[f_{N}]
for
\ell\in\{1,2\}
. Note that by moving the derivatives to the kernels and Proposition
5.5
, we get

(5.124)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&|\partial_{x_{1}^{\ell}}\partial_{x_{2}^{\ell}}\mathfrak{F}_{N_{0}}[f_{N}](z_{1},z_{2})|\\
&\qquad\qquad=\Big|\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}}dy_{j}\partial_{x_{1}^{\ell}}\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{1}-y_{1})\partial_{x_{2}^{\ell}}\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{2}-y_{2})f(\mathbf{z}_{1},\mathbf{z}_{2})dy_{1}dy_{2} \Big|\\
&\qquad\qquad\lesssim N_{0}^{2}\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}}|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|_{+}^{-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\lesssim N_{0}^{2}.\end{split}

The bound (
5.124
) is too crude for our purposes, as we are only allowed a smaller power of
N_{0}
in (
5.91
). Alternatively, if we move the derivatives to the function
f_{N}
, Proposition
5.7
gives the bound

(5.125)

\displaystyle|\partial_{x_{1}^{\ell}}\partial_{x_{2}^{\ell}}\mathfrak{F}_{N_{0}}[f_{N}](\mathbf{z}_{1},\mathbf{z}_{2})| \lesssim|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{-\varepsilon}|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|_{+}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|_{-}^{-\frac{3}{2}-\varepsilon}.

Unfortunately, the right-hand-side of (
5.125
) is not locally integrable. In the next lemma, we craft an interpolation argument by hand between the scenarios (
5.124
) and (
5.125
) which gives the appropriate power of
N_{0}
allowed in the bound (
5.91
). This argument can also be viewed as a “Sobolev inequality” as we basically trade derivatives (i.e. powers of
N_{0}
) for integrability, which is lacking on the right-hand-side of (
5.125
).

Lemma 5.21

(potential-Sobolev argument)
.

Fix
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
with
\beta^{2}\in[2\pi,4\pi)
and
0<\kappa_{\circ}=\kappa_{\circ}(\beta)\ll 1
satisfying
\kappa_{\circ}<\theta(\beta^{2})
, where
\theta
is as in (
5.123
),
N_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
and
\ell\in\{0,1\}
. Let
f_{N}
be as in (
5.121
) and define
\mathfrak{F}_{N_{0}}[f_{N}]
as in (
5.122
). Then, there exists an absolute constant
C>0
such that the following pointwise estimates hold.

(i)
If
\beta^{2}\in[2\pi,3\pi)
, then we have

(5.126)

\displaystyle|\partial_{x_{1}^{\ell}}\partial_{x_{2}^{\ell}}\mathfrak{F}_{N_{0}}[f_{N}](z_{1},z_{2})|\lesssim_{\kappa_{\circ}}N_{0}^{\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}+C\kappa_{\circ}}\cdot|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{+}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}+\kappa_{\circ}}\,|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{-}^{-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}+\kappa_{\circ}}

for any
z_{j}=(t_{j},x_{j})\in[0,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{2}
,
j=1,2
and uniformly in
N\in 1
.

(ii)
If
\beta^{2}\in[3\pi,4\pi)
, then we have

(5.127)

\displaystyle|\partial_{x_{1}^{\ell}}\partial_{x_{2}^{\ell}}\mathfrak{F}_{N_{0}}[f_{N}](z_{1},z_{2})|\lesssim_{\kappa_{\circ}}N_{0}^{\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}+C\kappa_{\circ}}\cdot|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{+}^{-2+\kappa_{\circ}}

for any
z_{j}=(t_{j},x_{j})\in[0,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{2}
,
j=1,2
and uniformly in
N\in 1
.

In Subsection
5.6
below, we discuss the integrability of the functions on the right-hand-side of (
5.126
)-(
5.127
) when convolved with convolution kernels of the operator
\Box^{-b}
,
b>\frac{1}{2}
. It turns out that proving the relevant integrability results for the hyperbolic type singularities (i.e. the right-hand-side of (
5.126
)) is much more involved than those for the elliptic type singularities (i.e. the right-hand-side of (
5.127
)) since dealing with singularities along light-cones requires a careful geometric analysis; see Lemma
5.23
. This is why the estimates (i) and (ii) in Lemma
5.21
above are rather surprising. Indeed, above
\beta^{2}=3\pi
, our estimates do not see the hyperbolicity of the problem at hand even though this case corresponds, at the level of the dynamics, to a more singular equation (
1.1
).

Proof.

In this proof, we write
\partial_{j}
for
\partial_{x^{\ell}_{j}}
for
j=1,2
for the sake of notational convenience. Recall for
j=1,2
, we denote by
\mathbf{z}_{j}=(t_{j},y_{j})
the “input” variables in the integrand of
\mathfrak{F}_{N_{0}}[f_{N}]
and by
z_{j}=(t_{j},x_{j})
the “output” variables (namely, the arguments of
\mathfrak{F}_{N_{0}}[f_{N}]
on the left-hand-side of (
5.122
)).

Fix
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
such that
0<\beta^{2}<4\pi
. Fix
0<\kappa_{\circ}\ll 1
such that
\kappa_{\circ}<\theta(\beta^{2})
. We first note that the inequalities (
5.126
) and (
5.127
) are straightforward for
N_{0}\lesssim_{\kappa_{\circ}}1
by (
2.41
). Hence, in what follows we assume that
N_{0}\gg_{\kappa_{\circ}}1
.

\bullet
Step
I
: basic restrictions on input variables.
Assume that
|x_{1}-y_{1}|>N_{0}^{-1+\kappa_{\circ}}
on the integrand of
\mathfrak{F}_{N_{0}}[f_{N}]
. Then, we have

\displaystyle|\partial_{1}\partial_{2}\mathfrak{F}_{N_{0}}[f_{N}](z_{1},z_{2})|

\displaystyle\quad\lesssim\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}}|\partial_{1}\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{1}-y_{1})|\mathbf{1}_{|x_{1}-y_{1}|>N_{0}^{-1+\kappa_{\circ}}}\,|\partial_{2}\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{2}-y_{2})||f_{N}(\mathbf{z}_{1},\mathbf{z}_{2})|dy_{1}dy_{2}.

By (
2.41
), the current assumption and Proposition
5.5
, we thus have

\displaystyle|\partial_{1}\partial_{2}\mathfrak{F}_{N_{0}}[f_{N}](z_{1},z_{2})|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim_{A}N_{0}^{6-A\kappa_{\circ}}\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}}|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|_{+}^{-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}dy_{1}dy_{2},

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim_{\kappa_{0}}1,

upon choosing
A
large enough depending on
\kappa_{\circ}
. The last bound implies (
5.126
) and (
5.127
). Note that by symmetry, we have a similar bound if
|x_{2}-y_{2}|>N_{0}^{-1+\kappa_{\circ}}
. Thus, we may assume that the bound
|x_{j}-y_{j}|\leq N_{0}^{-1+\kappa_{\circ}}
holds for any
j=1,2
. This reduction allows us to compare
|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{+}
and
|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|_{+}
in the following case: if
\max\big(|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{+},|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|_{+}\big)>N_{0}^{-1+2\kappa_{\circ}}
then we have

\displaystyle\big||z_{1}-z_{2}|_{+}-|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|_{+}\big|\leq|x_{1}-y_{2}+y_{1}-x_{2}|\leq 2N_{0}^{-1+\kappa_{\circ}},

and hence

(5.128)

\displaystyle|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{+}\sim|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|_{+},

for
N_{0}
large enough, as claimed. Similarly, if
\max\big(|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{-},|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|_{-}\big)>N_{0}^{-1+2\kappa_{\circ}}
, then we also have

(5.129)

\displaystyle|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{-}\sim|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|_{-}.

Now, we assume
|y_{1}-y_{2}|\leq N_{0}^{-c_{1}}
, with
c_{1}=10^{10}\cdot(2-\kappa_{\circ}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi})^{-1}\gg 1
on the integrand of
\mathfrak{F}_{N_{0}}[f_{N}]
. Then, we similarly get

\displaystyle|\partial_{1}\partial_{2}\mathfrak{F}_{N_{0}}[f_{N}](z_{1},z_{2})|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim N_{0}^{6}\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}}|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|_{+}^{-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}\mathbf{1}_{|y_{1}-y_{2}|\leq N_{0}^{-c_{1}}}dy_{1}dy_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim N_{0}^{6-(2-\kappa_{\circ}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi})c_{1}}\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}}|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|_{+}^{-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}|y_{1}-y_{2}|^{-2+\kappa_{\circ}+\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}dy_{1}dy_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim 1.

The last bound implies (
5.126
) and (
5.127
). Thus, we henceforth assume that the condition
|y_{1}-y_{2}|>N_{0}^{-c_{1}}
holds on the integrand of
\mathfrak{F}_{N_{0}}[f_{N}]
.

Let us now assume that
|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|_{-}\leq N_{0}^{-c_{2}}
, with
c_{2}=10^{10}\cdot c_{1}\gg 1
, holds on the integrand of
\mathfrak{F}_{N_{0}}[f_{N}]
. Then, by the previous reduction and Proposition
5.5
, we have

\displaystyle|\partial_{1}\partial_{2}\mathfrak{F}_{N_{0}}[f_{N}](z_{1},z_{2})|

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim N_{0}^{6}\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}}|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|_{+}^{-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}\mathbf{1}_{|y_{1}-y_{2}|>N_{0}^{-c_{1}}}\mathbf{1}_{|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|_{-}\leq N_{0}^{-c_{2}}}dy_{1}dy_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim N_{0}^{6+\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}c_{1}-\frac{c_{2}}{2}}\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}}|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|_{-}^{-\frac{1}{2}}dy_{1}dy_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim 1.

The last bound implies (
5.126
) and (
5.127
). To sum up, we assume in the remaining part of the proof that the following conditions hold:

(5.130)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|x_{1}-y_{1}|&\leq N_{0}^{-1+\kappa_{\circ}},\\
|x_{2}-y_{2}|&\leq N_{0}^{-1+\kappa_{\circ}},\\
|y_{1}-y_{2}|&>N_{0}^{-c_{1}},\\
|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|_{-}&>N_{0}^{-c_{2}}\end{split}

for some constants
c_{1},c_{2}>0
. We denote by
\mathcal{C}
the set pertaining to conditions (
5.130
). In what follows, we always assume that the indicator function
\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{C}}
is included in the integrand of
\mathfrak{F}[f_{N}]
, but we might omit to write it when it is not necessary.

\bullet
Step
I I
: case-by-case analysis on output variables.
Assume
{|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{+}\leq N_{0}^{-1+2\kappa_{\circ}}}
. By Proposition
5.5
, we have

(5.131)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&|\partial_{1}\partial_{2}\mathfrak{F}_{N_{0}}[f_{N}](z_{1},z_{2})|\lesssim\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}}|\partial_{1}\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{1}-y_{1})||\partial_{2}\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{2}-y_{2})||\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|_{+}^{-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}dy_{1}dy_{2}.\end{split}

From (
2.41
) and Lemma
3.1
, we deduce that

(5.132)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}}|\partial_{1}\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{1}-y_{1})||\partial_{2}\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{2}-y_{2})||\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|_{+}^{-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}dy_{1}dy_{2}\\
&\qquad\lesssim\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}}|\partial_{1}\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{1}-y_{1})||\partial_{2}\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{2}-y_{2})||y_{1}-y_{2}|^{-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}dy_{1}dy_{2}\\
&\qquad\lesssim N_{0}^{1+\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}|\partial_{1}\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{1}-y_{1})|dy_{1}\\
&\qquad\lesssim N_{0}^{2+\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}\end{split}

Therefore, combining (
5.131
), (
5.132
) and the condition
|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{+}\leq N_{0}^{-1+2\kappa_{\circ}}
, gives

(5.133)

\displaystyle|\partial_{1}\partial_{2}\mathfrak{F}_{N_{0}}[f_{N}](z_{1},z_{2})|\lesssim N_{0}^{\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}+C\kappa_{\circ}}\cdot|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{+}^{-2+\kappa_{\circ}},

which is acceptable since
|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{+}\geq|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{-}
. Hence, we now assume
|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{+}>N_{0}^{-1+2\kappa_{\circ}}
.

Assume
|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{-}\leq N_{0}^{-1+2\kappa_{\circ}}
and
|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{+}>N_{0}^{-1+2\kappa_{\circ}}
. In view of the discussion leading to (
5.128
), we have
|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{+}\sim|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|_{+}
. Then, by Propositions
5.5
and
5.7
and the conditions (
5.130
), we have

(5.134)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&|\partial_{1}\partial_{2}\mathfrak{F}_{N_{0}}[f_{N}](z_{1},z_{2})|\\
&\quad\lesssim\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbf{z}_{1},\mathbf{z}_{2})\cdot|\partial_{1}\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{1}-y_{1})||\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{2}-y_{2})||\partial_{2}f_{N}(\mathbf{z}_{1},\mathbf{z}_{2})|dy_{1}dy_{2}\\
&\quad\lesssim_{\kappa_{\circ}}\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbf{z}_{1},\mathbf{z}_{2})\cdot|\partial_{1}\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{1}-y_{1})||\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{2}-y_{2})|\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\Big(|y_{1}-y_{2}|^{-\kappa_{\circ}}+|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}_{+}|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|_{-}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\kappa_{0}}\Big)dy_{1}dy_{2}\\
&\quad\lesssim N_{0}^{C\kappa_{0}}\cdot|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{+}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}}|\partial_{1}\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{1}-y_{1})||\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{2}-y_{2})||\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|_{-}^{-\frac{1}{2}}dy_{1}dy_{2}.\end{split}

By Lemma
3.3
, we have

(5.135)

\displaystyle\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}}|\partial_{1}\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{1}-y_{1})||\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{2}-y_{2})||\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|_{-}^{-\frac{1}{2}}dy_{1}dy_{2}\lesssim N_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}+\kappa_{0}}.

If
\beta^{2}\in[2\pi,3\pi)
, then by (
5.134
), (
5.135
) and the conditions
|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{-}\leq N_{0}^{-1+2\kappa_{\circ}}
and
|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{+}>N_{0}^{-1+2\kappa_{\circ}}
, we get

(5.136)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\partial_{1}\partial_{2}\mathfrak{F}_{N_{0}}[f_{N}](z_{1},z_{2})|&\lesssim N_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}+C\kappa_{\circ}}\cdot|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{+}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}\\
&\lesssim N_{0}^{\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}\cdot|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{+}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}+\kappa_{\circ}}\,|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{-}^{-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}+\kappa_{\circ}}.\end{split}

Otherwise, we have
\beta^{2}\in[3\pi,4\pi)
. Therefore, from (
5.134
), (
5.135
) and the condition
{|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{+}>N_{0}^{-1+2\kappa_{\circ}}}
, we have

(5.137)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|\partial_{1}\partial_{2}\mathfrak{F}_{N_{0}}[f_{N}](z_{1},z_{2})|&\lesssim N_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}+C\kappa_{\circ}}\cdot|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{+}^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}\\
&\lesssim N_{0}^{\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}\cdot|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{+}^{-2+\kappa_{\circ}}.\end{split}

Lastly, assume
|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{-}>N_{0}^{-1+2\kappa_{\circ}}
and
|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{+}>N_{0}^{-1+2\kappa_{\circ}}
. Then, as in (
5.128
) and (
5.129
), we have that
|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{+}\sim|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|_{+}
and
|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{-}\sim|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|_{-}
. Thus, from Propositions
5.5
and
5.7
and the conditions (
5.130
), we have

(5.138)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&|\partial_{1}\partial_{2}\mathfrak{F}_{N_{0}}[f_{N}](z_{1},z_{2})|\\
&\quad\lesssim\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbf{z}_{1},\mathbf{z}_{2})\cdot|\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{1}-y_{1})||\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{2}-y_{2})||\partial_{1}\partial_{2}f_{N}(\mathbf{z}_{1},\mathbf{z}_{2})|dy_{1}dy_{2}\\
&\quad\lesssim_{\kappa_{\circ}}\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{C}}(\mathbf{z}_{1},\mathbf{z}_{2})\cdot|\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{1}-y_{1})||\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{2}-y_{2})|\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\Big(|y_{1}-y_{2}|^{-\kappa_{\circ}}+|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}_{+}|\mathbf{z}_{1}-\mathbf{z}_{2}|_{-}^{-\frac{3}{2}-\kappa_{0}}\Big)dy_{1}dy_{2}\\
&\quad\lesssim N_{0}^{C\kappa_{0}}\cdot|z_{1}-z_{2}|^{-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}}|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{-}^{-\frac{3}{2}-\kappa_{0}}.\end{split}

The estimates (
5.126
) and (
5.127
) immediately follow from (
5.138
) and the conditions
{|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{-}>N_{0}^{-1+2\kappa_{\circ}}}
and
|z_{1}-z_{2}|_{+}>N_{0}^{-1+2\kappa_{\circ}}
.
∎

5.6.
Bounds on singular integrals

In this subsection, we integrate the singularities output by Lemma
5.21
against the kernel of
\Box^{b}
for
b<-\frac{1}{2}
introduced in (
2.27
).

Let
b<-\frac{1}{2}
,
s,s_{1},s_{2}>0
and define

(5.139)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}^{+,b,s}(t)=\int_{[0,1]^{2}\times(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{2}}\prod_{j=1}^{2}dt_{j}dy_{j}\,\mathfrak{K}_{b}(t-t_{j},x_{j})\cdot\big(|t_{1}-t_{2}|+|x_{1}-x_{2}|_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\big)^{-s}

and

(5.140)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathfrak{I}^{-,b,s_{1},s_{2}}(t)&=\int_{[0,1]^{2}\times(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{2}}\prod_{j=1}^{2}dt_{j}dy_{j}\,\mathfrak{K}_{b}(t-t_{j},x_{j})\\
&\qquad\quad\times\big(|t_{1}-t_{2}|+|x_{1}-x_{2}|_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\big)^{-s_{1}}\big||t_{1}-t_{2}|-|x_{1}-x_{2}|_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\big|^{-s_{2}}\end{split}

for
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Here,
\mathfrak{K}_{b}
is as in (
2.33
).

Our main result in this subsection is the following quantitative estimate on the the integrals
\mathfrak{I}^{+,b,s}(t)
and
\mathfrak{I}^{-,b,s_{1},s_{2}}(t)
.

Lemma 5.22
.

Fix
b<-\frac{1}{2}
,
0<s,s_{1}<2
and
0<s_{2}<\frac{1}{2}
such that
s_{1}+s_{2}<2
. Then, the following bounds hold:

(5.141)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}^{+,b,s}(t)

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-2-4b},

(5.142)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}^{-,b,s_{1},s_{2}}(t)

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-2-4b}.

In order to prove the bounds (
5.141
) and (
5.142
), we proceed with several spatial localizations of the integrands of
\mathfrak{I}^{+,b,s}(t)
and
\mathfrak{I}^{-,b,s_{1},s_{2}}(t)
in what follows. For each
k\in 2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{2}
,
\lambda>0
and
r\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, we write

(5.143)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k}&=[-\pi,\pi)^{2}+k,\\
A_{r}(\lambda)&=\big\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{2}:\lambda-\frac{r+1}{100}\leq|x|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}<\lambda-\frac{r}{100}\big\}.\end{split}

The most challenging part of the proof of Lemma
5.22
is to estimate the contribution of the portion of the integrals
\mathfrak{I}^{-,b,s}(t)
and
\mathfrak{I}^{-,b,s_{1},s_{2}}(t)
close to their respective elliptic and hyperbolic singularities. This is the purpose of the next lemma.

Lemma 5.23
.

Fix
0\leq\eta_{1},\eta_{2},\eta_{1,2}<2
such that
\eta_{1}+\eta_{2}+\eta_{1,2}<4
,
0\leq\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}<1
and
0<\alpha_{1,2}<\frac{1}{2}
. We use the shorthand notation
\bar{\alpha}=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\alpha_{1,2})
and
\bar{\eta}=(\eta_{1},\eta_{2},\eta_{1,2})
. For
(t,t_{1},t_{2})\in\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]^{2}
,
k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\in 2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{2}
with
|k_{3}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\lesssim 1
, let
f^{\bar{\alpha}}_{k_{3},t,t_{1},t_{2}}
be the function

f^{\bar{\alpha}}_{k_{3},t,t_{1},t_{2}}(x_{1},x_{2})=\frac{\mathbf{1}_{||t_{1}-t_{2}|-|x_{1}-x_{2}+k_{3}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|\ll 1}\cdot\big||t_{1}-t_{2}|-|x_{1}-x_{2}+k_{3}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big|^{-\alpha_{1,2}}}{\big||t-t_{1}|-|x_{1}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big|^{\alpha_{1}}\big||t-t_{2}|-|x_{2}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big|^{\alpha_{2}}}

and define
\mathfrak{O}^{+,\bar{\eta}}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}
and
\mathfrak{C}^{-,\bar{\alpha}}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}(t,t_{1},t_{2})
by

(5.144)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{O}^{+,\bar{\eta}}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k_{1}}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k_{2}}}|x_{1}|^{-\eta_{1}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|x_{2}|^{-\eta_{2}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}|x_{1}-x_{2}+k_{3}|^{-\eta_{1,2}}_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}dx_{1}dx_{2},

(5.145)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}^{-,\bar{\alpha}}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}(t,t_{1},t_{2})

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k_{1}}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k_{2}}}\mathbf{1}_{A_{0}(|t-t_{1}|)}(x_{1})\mathbf{1}_{A_{0}(|t-t_{2}|)}(x_{2})\cdot f^{\bar{\alpha}}_{k_{3},t,t_{1},t_{2}}(x_{1},x_{2})dx_{1}dx_{2},

where
A_{0}(|t-t_{j}|)
for
j=1,2
is as in (
5.143
). Then, we have

(5.146)

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\,\in 2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{2}\\
|k_{3}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\lesssim 1\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{O}^{+,\bar{\eta}}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}<\infty,

(5.147)

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\,\in 2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{2}\\
|k_{3}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\lesssim 1\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{C}^{-,\bar{\alpha}}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}(t,t_{1},t_{2})\lesssim\omega(t,t_{1},t_{2})|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{1-\alpha_{1,2}},

where
\omega(t,t_{1},t_{2})
is given by

(5.148)

\displaystyle\omega(t,t_{1},t_{2})=\begin{cases}|t-t_{1}|+|t-t_{2}|&\quad\text{for $|t|\leq 10$},\\
1&\quad\text{for $|t|>10$}.\end{cases}

Obtaining (
5.147
) constitutes the most challenging part of Lemma
5.23
. It essentially follows from bounding appropriately the volume of the intersection of transverse tubes in
\mathbb{R}^{4}
; see (
5.151
) below.

Proof.

In this proof, we write
|\cdot|
for
|\cdot|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}
. We only prove (
5.147
) as the proof of (
5.146
) is much simpler and follows from arguments similar to those in the estimates (
5.104
)-(
5.108
) (which essentially correspond to the case
k_{1}=k_{2}=k_{3}=0
) in the proof of Proposition
5.15
.

We proceed with a multiscale decomposition procedure: we write

(5.149)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}^{-,\bar{\alpha}}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}(t,t_{1},t_{2})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\mu_{1,2}\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}}\\
\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\mu_{1,2}\ll 1\end{subarray}}(\mu_{1})^{-\alpha_{1}}(\mu_{2})^{-\alpha_{2}}(\mu_{1,2})^{-\alpha_{1,2}}\cdot\mathfrak{C}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}^{-}[\mu],

with
\mu=(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\mu_{1,2})
and
\mathfrak{C}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}^{-}[\mu](t,t_{1},t_{2})=|S^{1}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}[\mu](t,t_{1},t_{2})|
, where

(5.150)

\displaystyle\begin{split}S^{1}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}[\mu](t,t_{1},t_{2})&=\Big\{(y_{1},y_{2})\in\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k_{1}}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k_{2}}:\frac{\mu_{j}}{2}\leq\big||t-t_{j}|-|y_{j}|\big|<\mu_{j},\,j=1,2,\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\,\frac{\mu_{1,2}}{2}\leq\big||t_{1}-t_{2}|-|y_{1}-y_{2}+k_{3}|\big|<\mu_{1,2}\Big\}.\end{split}

The bound (
5.147
) reduces to proving

(5.151)

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}\,\in 2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{2}\\
|k_{3}|\lesssim 1\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{C}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}^{-,\bar{\alpha}}[\mu](t,t_{1},t_{2})\lesssim\omega(t,t_{1},t_{2})|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\mu_{1}\mu_{2}(\mu_{1,2})^{\frac{1}{2}},

where
\omega(t,t_{1},t_{2})
is as in (
5.148
). Indeed summing (
5.151
) over
\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\mu_{1,2}
and noting
\mu_{1,2}\lesssim|t_{1}-t_{2}|
gives (
5.147
). In what follows, we omit the dependence of all quantities in
(t,t_{1},t_{2})
for notational convenience; i.e. we write
\mathfrak{C}^{-,\bar{\alpha}}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}[\mu]
for
\mathfrak{C}^{-,\bar{\alpha}}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}[\mu](t,t_{1},t_{2})
for instance.

\bullet
Case 1:
\mu_{1,2}\gtrsim\max(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})
.
Let us assume
t\geq t_{1}\geq t_{2}
in the following. The other cases follow from similar arguments upon changing signs in the expressions below. Then, for
(y_{1},y_{2})\in S^{1}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}[\mu]
, we have

(5.152)

\displaystyle|y_{1}|-|y_{2}|=-|y_{1}-y_{2}+k_{3}|+O(\mu_{1,2}).

Squarring (
5.152
) and doing some algebra then shows

(5.153)

\displaystyle\langle y_{1},y_{2}\rangle-\langle y_{1}-y_{2},k_{3}\rangle=\frac{1}{2}|k_{3}|^{2}+|y_{1}||y_{2}|+O\big(|t_{1}-t_{2}|\mu_{1,2}+\mu_{1,2}^{2}\big).

From (
5.153
) and
\mu_{1,2}\lesssim|t_{1}-t_{2}|
, there exists a function
C=C(k_{3},y_{2},|y_{1}|)
depending only on
y_{2}
and
|y_{1}|
such that

\displaystyle\langle y_{1},y_{2}-k_{3}\rangle=C+O(|t_{1}-t_{2}|\mu_{1,2})

so that

(5.154)

\displaystyle\cos(\angle(y_{1},y_{2}-k_{3}))=C_{1}+O\Big(\frac{|t_{1}-t_{2}|\mu_{1,2}}{|y_{1}||y_{2}-k_{3}|}\Big),

where
C_{1}=C_{1}(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3},y_{2},|y_{1}|)
is another function depending only on
y_{2}
and
|y_{1}|
.

\bullet
Subcase 1.1:
k_{3}=0
.
By (
5.154
), there exists an interval
J_{1}=J_{1}(k_{3},y_{2},|y_{1}|)
with

(5.155)

\displaystyle|J_{1}|\lesssim\frac{|t_{1}-t_{2}|\mu_{1,2}}{|y_{1}||y_{2}|}

such that
\cos(\angle(y_{1},y_{2}-k_{3}))\in J_{1}
. It is easy to observe via a Taylor expansion that

(5.156)

\displaystyle\sup_{I}\big\{\theta\in[0,2\pi]:\cos(\theta)\in I\big\}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}},

where the supremum is taken over intervals
I\subset[-1,1]
such that
|I|\lesssim\varepsilon
. Therefore, by (
5.154
), (
5.155
), (
5.156
), noting that
\angle(y_{2},e_{1})
, with
e_{1}=(1,0)
, belongs to an interval
J_{2}=J_{2}(k_{2})
of length
\lesssim\langle k_{2}\rangle^{-1}
and switching to polar coordinates, we have

(5.157)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathfrak{C}^{-,\bar{\alpha}}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}[\mu]&\lesssim\int_{[0,2\pi]}\mathbf{1}_{\theta_{2}\in J_{2}(k_{2})}d\theta_{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{||t-t_{1}|-r_{1}|<\mu_{1}}\mathbf{1}_{||t-t_{2}|-r_{2}|<\mu_{2}}r_{1}r_{2}dr_{1}dr_{2}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\times\int_{[0,2\pi]}\mathbf{1}_{\cos\theta_{1}\in J_{1}(k_{3},r_{1},r_{2},\theta_{2})}d\theta_{1}\\
&\lesssim\big(|t_{1}-t_{2}|\mu_{1,2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{[0,2\pi]}\mathbf{1}_{\theta_{2}\in J_{2}(k_{2})}d\theta_{2}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\times\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{||t-t_{1}|-r_{1}|<\mu_{1}}\mathbf{1}_{||t-t_{2}|-r_{2}|<\mu_{2}}(r_{1}r_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}dr_{1}dr_{2}\\
&\lesssim\big(|t-t_{1}||t-t_{2}||t_{1}-t_{2}|\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\langle k_{2}\rangle^{-1}\cdot\mu_{1}\mu_{2}(\mu_{1,2})^{\frac{1}{2}},\end{split}

which shows (
5.151
) since
\langle k_{2}\rangle\sim\langle t-t_{1}\rangle\sim\langle t-t_{2}\rangle
.

\bullet
Subcase 1.2:
k_{3}\neq 0
and
|k_{2}|\gg 1
.
Then, since
y_{2}\in\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k_{2}}
and
|k_{3}|\lesssim 1
, we have

(5.158)

\displaystyle|y_{2}-k_{3}|\sim|k_{2}|.

Therefore, from (
5.158
), (
5.154
) and arguing as in (
5.155
)-(
5.157
), we get

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}^{-,\bar{\alpha}}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}[\mu]

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(|t-t_{1}||t_{1}-t_{2}|\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}|t-t_{2}|\,\langle k_{1}\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}}\cdot\mu_{1}\mu_{2}(\mu_{1,2})^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\mu_{1}\mu_{2}(\mu_{1,2})^{\frac{1}{2}},

as desired in (
5.151
) since
\langle k_{2}\rangle\sim\langle t-t_{1}\rangle\sim\langle t-t_{2}\rangle\gg 1
.

\bullet
Subcase 1.3:
k_{3}\neq 0
and
|k_{2}|\lesssim 1
.
Fix
0<\delta\ll 1
. If we have

|y_{2}-y_{1}-k_{3}|\leq(1-\delta)\cdot|y_{1}|,

then we get

(5.159)

\displaystyle|y_{2}-k_{3}|\geq|y_{1}|-|y_{2}-y_{1}-k_{3}|\geq\delta\cdot|y_{1}|.

Hence, from (
5.159
), (
5.154
) and arguing as in (
5.155
)-(
5.157
), we get

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}^{-,\bar{\alpha}}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}[\mu]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\delta}|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}|t-t_{2}|\,\langle k_{2}\rangle^{-1}\cdot\mu_{1}\mu_{2}(\mu_{1,2})^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|t-t_{2}||t_{1}-t_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\mu_{1}\mu_{2}(\mu_{1,2})^{\frac{1}{2}},

as desired in (
5.151
) since
\langle k_{2}\rangle\sim 1
. Otherwise, we have

|y_{1}|<(1-\delta)^{-1}\cdot|y_{2}-y_{1}-k_{3}|,

which yields

\displaystyle|y_{2}|

\displaystyle\leq|t-t_{2}|+\mu_{2}\leq|t-t_{1}|+|t_{1}-t_{2}|+\mu_{2}

\displaystyle\leq|y_{1}|+|t_{1}-t_{2}|+\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\delta)^{-1}\cdot|y_{2}-y_{1}-k_{3}|+|t_{1}-t_{2}|+\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big((1-\delta)^{-1}+1\big)|t_{1}-t_{2}|+10\max(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\mu_{1,2})

\displaystyle<3,

since
(t_{1},t_{2})\in[0,1]^{2}
and
\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\mu_{1,2},\delta\ll 1
. Noting,
|k_{3}|\geq 2\pi
since
k_{3}\neq 0
and we have

(5.160)

\displaystyle|y_{2}-k_{3}|>1.

Thus, from (
5.160
), (
5.154
) and arguing as in (
5.155
)-(
5.157
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}^{-,\bar{\alpha}}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}[\mu]

\displaystyle\lesssim|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}|t-t_{1}|^{\frac{1}{2}}|t-t_{2}|\,\langle k_{2}\rangle^{-1}\cdot\mu_{1}\mu_{2}(\mu_{1,2})^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|t-t_{1}|^{\frac{1}{2}}|t-t_{2}||t_{1}-t_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\mu_{1}\mu_{2}(\mu_{1,2})^{\frac{1}{2}},

as desired in (
5.151
) since
\langle k_{2}\rangle\sim 1
.

\bullet
Case 2:
\mu_{1,2}\ll\max(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})
.
We for instance assume
\mu_{2}=\max(\mu_{1},\mu_{1,2})
. The case
\mu_{1}=\max(\mu_{2},\mu_{1,2})
may be treated in a similar way. Then, by a change of variable, we have
|S^{1}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}[\mu]|\leq|S^{2}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}[\mu]|
, where

\displaystyle S^{2}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}[\mu]

\displaystyle=\Big\{(y_{1},y_{2})\in\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k_{1}}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}_{0}:\frac{\mu_{1}}{2}\leq\big||t-t_{1}|-|y_{1}|\big|<\mu_{1},

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\,\frac{\mu_{1,2}}{2}\leq\big||t_{1}-t_{2}|-|y_{2}|\big|<\mu_{1,2},

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\,\frac{\mu_{2}}{2}\leq\big||t-t_{2}|-|y_{1}-y_{2}+k_{3}|\big|<\mu_{2}\Big\}.

As in (
5.152
)-(
5.153
), we have that

(5.161)

\displaystyle\langle y_{1},y_{2}\rangle-\langle y_{1}-y_{2},k_{3}\rangle=\frac{1}{2}|k_{3}|^{2}+|y_{1}||y_{2}|+O\big(|t-t_{2}|\mu_{2}+\mu_{2}^{2}\big).

Therefore, from (
5.161
) and as in (
5.154
), we have

(5.162)

\displaystyle\cos(\angle(y_{2},y_{1}+k_{3}))=C_{2}+O\Big(\frac{|t-t_{2}|\mu_{2}}{|y_{2}||y_{1}+k_{3}|}\Big),

where
C_{2}=C_{2}(k_{3},y_{1},|y_{2}|)
is a function depending only on
y_{1}
and
|y_{2}|
.

\bullet
Subcase 2.1:
k_{3}=0
.
From (
5.162
), there exists an interval
J_{3}=J_{3}(k_{3},y_{1},|y_{2}|)
with

(5.163)

\displaystyle|J_{3}|\lesssim\frac{|t-t_{2}|\mu_{2}}{|y_{2}||y_{1}|}

such that
\cos(\angle(y_{2},y_{1}+k_{3}))\in J_{3}
. From (
5.156
), (
5.163
) and since
\angle(y_{1},e_{1})
, with
e_{1}=(1,0
), belongs to an interval
J_{4}(k_{1})
of length
\lesssim\langle k_{1}\rangle^{-1}
, we have that

(5.164)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathfrak{C}^{-,\bar{\alpha}}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}[\mu]&\lesssim\int_{[0,2\pi]}\mathbf{1}_{\theta_{1}\in J_{4}(k_{1})}d\theta_{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{||t-t_{1}|-r_{1}|<\mu_{1}}\mathbf{1}_{||t_{1}-t_{2}|-r_{2}|<\mu_{1,2}}r_{1}r_{2}dr_{1}dr_{2}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\times\int_{[0,2\pi]}\mathbf{1}_{\cos\theta_{1}\in J_{3}(k_{3},r_{1},r_{2},\theta_{1})}d\theta_{2}\\
&\lesssim\big(|t-t_{2}|\mu_{2}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{[0,2\pi]}\mathbf{1}_{\theta_{2}\in J_{4}(k_{1})}d\theta_{2}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{||t-t_{1}|-r_{1}|<\mu_{1}}\mathbf{1}_{||t_{1}-t_{2}|-r_{2}|<\mu_{1,2}}(r_{1}r_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}dr_{1}dr_{2}\\
&\lesssim\big(|t-t_{1}||t-t_{2}||t_{1}-t_{2}|\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,\langle k_{1}\rangle^{-1}\cdot\mu_{1}\mu_{1,2}(\mu_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}},\end{split}

which shows (
5.151
) since
\langle k_{1}\rangle\sim\langle t-t_{1}\rangle\sim\langle t-t_{2}\rangle
.

\bullet
Subcase 2.2:
k_{3}\neq 0
and
|k_{1}|\gg 1
.
As in Subcase 2.1 above, we have

(5.165)

\displaystyle|y_{1}+k_{3}|\sim|k_{1}|.

Hence, from (
5.165
), (
5.162
) and arguing as in (
5.163
)-(
5.164
), we get

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}^{-,\bar{\alpha}}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}[\mu]

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(|t_{1}-t_{2}||t-t_{2}|\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}|t-t_{1}|\langle k_{1}\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}}\cdot\mu_{1}\mu_{1,2}(\mu_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\mu_{1}\mu_{1,2}(\mu_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}},

as desired in (
5.151
) since
\langle k_{1}\rangle\sim\langle t-t_{1}\rangle\sim\langle t-t_{2}\rangle\gg 1
.

\bullet
Subcase 2.3:
k_{3}\neq 0
and
|k_{1}|\lesssim 1
.
Fix
0<\delta\ll 1
. If we have

|y_{2}|\leq(1-\delta)\cdot|y_{1}-y_{2}+k_{3}|,

then we deduce

(5.166)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|y_{1}+k_{3}|&\geq|y_{1}-y_{2}+k_{3}|-|y_{2}|\\
&\geq\delta\cdot|y_{1}-y_{2}+k_{3}|\\
&\gtrsim_{\delta}|t-t_{2}|\end{split}

Therefore, from (
5.166
), (
5.162
) and arguing as in (
5.163
)-(
5.164
), we have that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}^{-,\bar{\alpha}}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}[\mu]

\displaystyle\lesssim|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}|t-t_{1}|\,\langle k_{1}\rangle^{-1}\cdot\mu_{1}\mu_{1,2}(\mu_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{\frac{1}{2}}|t-t_{1}|\cdot\mu_{1}\mu_{1,2}(\mu_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}},

as desired in (
5.151
) since
\langle k_{1}\rangle\sim 1
. Otherwise, we have

|y_{1}-y_{2}+k_{3}|<(1-\delta)^{-1}\cdot|y_{2}|,

\displaystyle|y_{1}|

\displaystyle\leq|t-t_{1}|+\mu_{1}\leq|t-t_{2}|+|t_{1}-t_{2}|+\mu_{1}

\displaystyle\leq|y_{1}-y_{2}+k_{3}|+|t_{1}-t_{2}|+\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\delta)^{-1}\cdot|y_{2}|+|t_{1}-t_{2}|+\mu_{1}+\mu_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\big((1-\delta)^{-1}+1\big)|t_{1}-t_{2}|+10\max(\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\mu_{1,2})

\displaystyle<3,

since
(t_{1},t_{2})\in[0,1]^{2}
and
\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\mu_{1,2},\delta\ll 1
. Hence,
|k_{3}|\geq 2\pi
since
k_{3}\neq 0
and we have

(5.167)

\displaystyle|y_{1}+k_{3}|>1.

Therefore, from (
5.167
), (
5.162
) and arguing as in (
5.163
)-(
5.164
), we have that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{C}^{-,\bar{\alpha}}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}[\mu]

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(|t-t_{2}||t_{1}-t_{2}|\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}|t-t_{1}|\,\langle k_{1}\rangle^{-1}\cdot\mu_{1}\mu_{1,2}(\mu_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(|t-t_{2}||t_{1}-t_{2}|\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}|t-t_{1}|\cdot\mu_{1}\mu_{1,2}(\mu_{2})^{\frac{1}{2}},

as desired in (
5.151
) since
\langle k_{1}\rangle\sim 1
.

∎

We now prove Lemma
5.22
.

Proof of Lemma
5.22
.

We have

(5.168)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}^{+,b,s}(t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\in 2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\sum_{r_{1},r_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\mathfrak{I}^{+,b,s}_{r_{1},r_{2},k_{1},k_{2}}(t),

(5.169)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}^{-,b,s_{1},s_{2}}(t)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k_{1},k_{2}\in 2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\sum_{r_{1},r_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}\mathfrak{I}^{-,b,s_{1},s_{2}}_{r_{1},r_{2},k_{1},k_{2}}(t)

for any
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}^{+,b,s}_{r_{1},r_{2},k_{1},k_{2}}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,1]^{2}\times(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{2}}\prod_{j=1}^{2}dt_{j}dy_{j}\,\mathfrak{K}_{b}(t-t_{j},x_{j})\mathbf{1}_{\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k_{j}}}(x_{j})\mathbf{1}_{A_{r_{j}}(|t-t_{j}|)}(x_{j})dx_{1}dx_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\big(|t_{1}-t_{2}|+|x_{1}-x_{2}|_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\big)^{-s}

and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}^{-,b,s_{1},s_{2}}_{r_{1},r_{2},k_{1},k_{2}}(t)

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,1]^{2}\times(\mathbb{R}^{2})^{2}}\prod_{j=1}^{2}dt_{j}dy_{j}\,\mathfrak{K}_{b}(t-t_{j},x_{j})\mathbf{1}_{\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k_{j}}}(x_{j})\mathbf{1}_{A_{r_{j}}(|t-t_{j}|)}(x_{j})dx_{1}dx_{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad\times\big(|t_{1}-t_{2}|+|x_{1}-x_{2}|_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\big)^{-s_{1}}\big||t_{1}-t_{2}|-|x_{1}-x_{2}|_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\big|^{-s_{2}}.

Note that the sums in (
5.168
) and (
5.169
) are finite for
t
fixed: in view of the spatial localization in the kernel (
2.33
), we have
|k_{j}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\lesssim\langle t\rangle
and
r_{j}\leq 100(1+|t|)
for
j=1,2
. We claim the following estimates on the localized integrals defined above:

(5.170)

\displaystyle\sup_{k_{1},k_{2}\in 2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\mathfrak{I}^{+,b,s}_{r_{1},r_{2},k_{1},k_{2}}(t)

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle r_{1}\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}\langle r_{2}\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}\langle t\rangle^{-3-2b},

(5.171)

\displaystyle\sup_{k_{1},k_{2}\in 2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\mathfrak{I}^{-,b,s_{1},s_{2}}_{r_{1},r_{2},k_{1},k_{2}}(t)

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle r_{1}\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}\langle r_{2}\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}\langle t\rangle^{-3-2b}.

for any
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
(r_{1},r_{2})\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{2}
. Let us show how the bounds (
5.170
) and (
5.171
) imply (
5.141
) and (
5.142
). Assume the estimate (
5.170
). Then, for any fixed
r\leq 100(1+|t|)
, a volume packing argument shows that

\#\big\{k\in 2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{2}:A_{r}(|t-t^{\prime}|)\cap\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k}\neq\emptyset\big\}\lesssim 1+|t-t^{\prime}|-\frac{r}{100}\lesssim\langle t\rangle,

uniformly in
|t^{\prime}|\leq 1
. Hence, we have

(5.172)

\displaystyle\#\{(k_{1},k_{2})\in(2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{2})^{2}:\mathfrak{I}^{+,b,s}_{r_{1},r_{2},k_{1},k_{2}}(t)\neq 0\}\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{2}.

for each fixed
(r_{1},r_{2})\in(\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0})^{2}
and
t\in\mathbb{R}
, with an implicit constant which is uniform in the parameters
r_{1},r_{2}
and
t
. Therefore, from (
5.168
), (
5.170
) and (
5.172
), we have that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}^{+,b,s}(t)

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-1-2b}\cdot\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r_{1}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\\
0\leq r_{1}\leq 100(1+|t|)\end{subarray}}\,\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}r_{2}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\\
0\leq r_{2}\leq 100(1+|t|)\end{subarray}}\langle r_{1}\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}\langle r_{2}\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}

\displaystyle\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-2-4b},

which is exactly (
5.141
). Similarly, one shows that (
5.142
) follows from (
5.171
) together with (
5.169
).

We first prove the simpler bound (
5.170
) and start with the case
(r_{1},r_{2})=(0,0)
. First, we rewrite
|x_{1}-x_{2}|_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}
in the integrand of
\mathfrak{I}^{+,b,s}_{r_{1},r_{2},k_{1},k_{2}}(t)
by using our spatial localizations. Let
(x_{1},x_{2})\in\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k_{1}}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k_{2}}
and write
x_{1}=y_{1}+k_{1}
and
x_{2}=y_{2}+k_{2}
with
(y_{1},y_{2})\in[-\pi,\pi)^{2}
. Thus, by definition of the norm
|\cdot|_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}
, we have

(5.173)

\displaystyle\begin{split}|x_{1}-x_{2}|_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}&=\min_{k_{3}\,\in 2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{2}}|y_{1}-y_{2}+k_{3}+k_{2}-k_{1}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\\
&=\min_{k_{3}\,\in B(k_{1}-k_{2},4\pi)\cap 2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{2}}|y_{1}-y_{2}+k_{3}+k_{2}-k_{1}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\\
&=\min_{k_{3}\,\in B(k_{1}-k_{2},4\pi)\cap 2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{2}}|x_{1}-x_{2}+k_{3}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}},\end{split}

since
|y_{1}-y_{2}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\in[-2\pi,2\pi)^{2}\subset B(0,3\pi)
. Fix
k_{1},k_{2}\in 2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{2}
. Note that by definition of the annulus
A_{r}(\lambda)
in (
5.143
), we have

(5.174)

\displaystyle\sup_{(t,y)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}\int_{[0,1]}\big||t-t^{\prime}|-|y|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big|^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}\mathbf{1}_{A_{0}(|t-t^{\prime}|)}(y)\,dt^{\prime}<\infty

for
b<-\frac{1}{2}
. If
|t|\lesssim 1
(which implies
|k_{1}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}},|k_{2}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\lesssim 1
), then by using the estimates (
5.174
) and (
5.173
) together with (
5.146
) in Lemma
5.23
, we find

(5.175)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathfrak{I}^{+,b,s}_{0,0,k_{1},k_{2}}(t)&\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k_{1}}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k_{2}}}|x_{1}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}|x_{2}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}|x_{1}-x_{2}|_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}^{-s}dx_{1}dx_{2}\\
&\qquad\quad\times\int_{[0,1]}\big||t-t_{1}|-|x_{1}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big|^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}\mathbf{1}_{A_{0}(|t-t_{1}|)}(x_{1})\,dt_{1}\\
&\qquad\qquad\quad\times\int_{[0,1]}\big||t-t_{2}|-|x_{2}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big|^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}\mathbf{1}_{A_{0}(|t-t_{2}|)}(x_{2})\,dt_{2}\\
&\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k_{1}}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k_{2}}}|x_{1}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}|x_{2}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}|x_{1}-x_{2}|_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}^{-s}dx_{1}dx_{2} \\
&\lesssim\sum_{k_{3}\,\in B(k_{1}-k_{2},4\pi)\cap 2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\mathfrak{O}^{+,\frac{3}{2}+b,\frac{3}{2}+b,s}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}\\
&\lesssim 1,\end{split}

since the set
B(k_{1}-k_{2},4\pi)\cap 2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{2}
has at most
10
elements and
2b+s<1
(which is always true as
b<-\frac{1}{2}
and
s<2
). Here,
\mathfrak{O}^{+,\eta_{1},\eta_{2},\eta_{1,2}}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}
is as in (
5.144
). Similarly, if
|t|\gg 1
, we have

(5.176)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathfrak{I}^{+,b,s}_{0,0,k_{1},k_{2}}(t)&\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-3-2b}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k_{1}}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k_{2}}}|x_{1}-x_{2}|_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}^{-s}dx_{1}dx_{2}\\
&\qquad\quad\times\int_{[0,1]}\big||t-t_{1}|-|x_{1}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big|^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}\mathbf{1}_{A_{0}(|t-t_{1}|)}(x_{1})dt_{1}\\
&\qquad\qquad\quad\times\int_{[0,1]}\big||t-t_{2}|-|x_{2}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big|^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}\mathbf{1}_{A_{0}(|t-t_{2}|)}(x_{2})dt_{2}\\
&\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-3-2b}\sum_{k_{3}\,\in B(k_{1}-k_{2},4\pi)\cap 2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\mathfrak{O}^{+,0,0,s}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}\\
&\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-3-2b}.\end{split}

Therefore, combining (
5.175
) and (
5.176
) yields

(5.177)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}^{+,b,s}_{0,0,k_{1},k_{2}}(t)\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-3-2b}

for all
t\in\mathbb{R}
, as desired in (
5.170
). Now, assume
r_{1},r_{2}\geq 1
. Then, by definition of the annulus
A_{r}(\lambda)
in (
5.143
) and (
5.173
), we have

(5.178)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathfrak{I}^{+,b,s}_{r_{1},r_{2},k_{1},k_{2}}(t)&\lesssim\langle r_{1}\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}\langle r_{2}\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k_{1}}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k_{2}}}|x_{1}-x_{2}|_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}^{-s}dx_{1}dx_{2}\\
&\qquad\quad\times\int_{[0,1]^{2}}|t-t_{1}|^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}|t-t_{2}|^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}dt_{1}dt_{2}\\
&\lesssim\langle r_{1}\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}\langle r_{2}\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}\langle t\rangle^{-3-2b}\sum_{k_{3}\,\in B(k_{1}-k_{2},4\pi)\cap 2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\mathfrak{O}^{+,0,0,s}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}\\
&\lesssim\langle r_{1}\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}\langle r_{2}\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}\langle t\rangle^{-3-2b},\end{split}

as desired in (
5.170
). Thus (
5.177
) and (
5.178
) proves (
5.170
) in the cases
(r_{1},r_{2})=0
and
r_{1},r_{2}\geq 1
. The mixed case
r_{1}=0
or
r_{2}=0
and
(r_{1},r_{2})\neq 0
is treated via similar arguments; we omit details.

We now turn our attention to (
5.171
). Consider the contribution of
\big||t_{1}-t_{2}|-|x_{1}-x_{2}|_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\big|\gtrsim 1
to the integrand of
\mathfrak{I}^{-,b,s_{1},s_{2}}_{r_{1},r_{2},k_{1},k_{2}}(t)
. Then,
\mathfrak{I}^{-,b,s_{1},s_{2}}_{r_{1},r_{2},k_{1},k_{2}}(t)
basically reduces to
\mathfrak{I}^{+,b,s_{1}}_{r_{1},r_{2},k_{1},k_{2}}(t)
and the bound (
5.171
) follows from (
5.170
). Thus, in what follows, we only need to bound
\mathfrak{I}^{-,b,s_{1},s_{2}}_{r_{1},r_{2},k_{1},k_{2}}(t)
under the assumption
\big||t_{1}-t_{2}|-|x_{1}-x_{2}|_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\big|\ll 1
.

We consider the case
(r_{1},r_{2})=(0,0)
. From (
5.173
) together with (
5.147
) in Lemma
5.23
, we have

(5.179)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathfrak{I}^{-,b,s_{1},s_{2}}_{0,0,k_{1},k_{2}}(t)&\lesssim\int_{[0,1]^{2}}|t-t_{1}|^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}|t-t_{2}|^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{-s_{1}}dt_{1}dt_{2}\\
&\qquad\times\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k_{1}}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k_{2}}}\mathbf{1}_{A_{0}(|t-t_{1}|)}(x_{1})\mathbf{1}_{A_{0}(|t-t_{2}|)}(x_{2})\mathbf{1}_{||t_{1}-t_{2}|-|x_{1}-x_{2}|_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}|\ll 1}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\times\frac{\big||t_{1}-t_{2}|-|x_{1}-x_{2}|_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\big|^{-s_{2}}}{\big||t-t_{1}|-|x_{1}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big|^{\frac{3}{2}+b}\big||t-t_{2}|-|x_{2}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\big|^{\frac{3}{2}+b}}dx_{1}dx_{2}\\
&\lesssim\sum_{k_{3}\,\in B(k_{1}-k_{2},4\pi)\cap 2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{2}}\int_{[0,1]^{2}}|t-t_{1}|^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}|t-t_{2}|^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{-s_{1}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\mathfrak{C}^{-,\frac{3}{2}+b,\frac{3}{2}+b,s_{2}}_{k_{1},k_{2},k_{3}}(t,t_{1},t_{2})dt_{1}dt_{2}\\
&\lesssim\int_{[0,1]^{2}}|t-t_{1}|^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}|t-t_{2}|^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{1-s_{1}-s_{2}}\,\omega(t,t_{1},t_{2})dt_{1}dt_{2},\end{split}

where the function
\omega
is as in (
5.148
). Here, we used the fact that
B(k_{1}-k_{2},4\pi)\cap 2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{2}
has at most
10
elements along with the conditions
\frac{3}{2}+b<1
and
s_{2}<\frac{1}{2}
. A simple computation then shows

(5.180)

\displaystyle\int_{[0,1]^{2}}|t-t_{1}|^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}|t-t_{2}|^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{1-s_{1}-s_{2}}\,\omega(t,t_{1},t_{2})dt_{1}dt_{2}\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-3-2b}

for
\frac{3}{2}+b<1
and
s_{1}+s_{2}<2
. Thus, by combining (
5.179
) and (
5.180
), we deduce

(5.181)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}^{-,b,s_{1},s_{2}}_{0,0,k_{1},k_{2}}(t)\lesssim\langle t\rangle^{-3-2b}.

We now treat the case when
r_{1},r_{2}\geq 1
. Assume
|t|\lesssim 1
. By proceeding as in (
5.175
), we have

(5.182)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathfrak{I}^{-,b,s_{1},s_{2}}_{r_{1},r_{2},k_{1},k_{2}}(t)&\lesssim\langle r_{1}\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}\langle r_{2}\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k_{1}}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k_{2}}}|x_{1}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}|x_{2}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{-s_{1}}_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}dx_{1}dx_{2}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\times\int_{[0,1]^{2}}\big||t_{1}-t_{2}|-|x_{1}-x_{2}|_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}\big|^{-s_{2}}dt_{1}dt_{2} \\
&\lesssim\langle r_{1}\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}\langle r_{2}\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k_{1}}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}_{k_{2}}}|x_{1}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}|x_{2}|_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}|x_{1}-x_{2}|^{-s_{1}}_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}dx_{1}dx_{2}\\
&\lesssim\langle r_{1}\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}\langle r_{2}\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}-b},\end{split}

where we used the conditions
b<-\frac{1}{2}
,
s_{1}<2
and
s_{2}<\frac{1}{2}
. Similarly, by arguing as in (
5.176
), we have the bound

(5.183)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}^{-,b,s_{1},s_{2}}_{r_{1},r_{2},k_{1},k_{2}}(t)\lesssim\langle r_{1}\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}\langle r_{2}\rangle^{-\frac{3}{2}-b}\langle t\rangle^{-3-2b}

for
|t|\gg 1
. Thus (
5.181
), (
5.182
) and (
5.183
) show (
5.171
) in the cases
(r_{1},r_{2})=0
and
r_{1},r_{2}\geq 1
. The mixed case
r_{1}=0
or
r_{2}=0
and
(r_{1},r_{2})\neq 0
may be treated via similar arguments and we omit details.
∎

5.7.
Proofs of Propositions
5.16
and
5.17

We first start with the proof of Proposition
5.17
which is a consequence of the results in Subsections
5.5
and
5.6
.

Proof of Proposition
5.17
.

Let
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
with
\beta^{2}\in[2\pi,4\pi)
,
N_{0}\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}}
,
(N,N_{1},N_{2})\in\mathbb{N}^{3}
with
N_{1}\geq N_{2}
and
\ell\in\{1,2\}
. Fix
\varepsilon>0
and
0<\kappa_{\circ}\ll\varepsilon
as in Lemma
5.21
. Let
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}\cong\mathbb{R}\times[-\pi,\pi)^{2}
.

We first prove (
5.91
). From the properties of the operator
\Box_{\mathbb{T}^{2}}^{b}
in Subsection
2.2
(see in particular (
2.35
)), we have

(5.184)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big|\big(\Box^{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\,\partial_{x^{\ell}}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N})\big)(t,x)\big|^{2}\\
&\qquad=\int_{([0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})^{2}}\mathfrak{K}_{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}(t-t_{1},x-x_{1})\mathfrak{K}_{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}(t-t_{2},x-x_{2})\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\times\partial_{x_{1}^{\ell}}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N})(t_{1},x_{1})\cdot\overline{\partial_{x_{2}^{\ell}}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N})}(t_{2},x_{2})\,dt_{1}dt_{2}dx_{1}dx_{2},\end{split}

where
\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N}(t,\cdot)
is interpreted as a
2\pi
-periodic function on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Recalling
\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}
has convolution kernel
\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}
and by (
5.99
)-(
5.100
) and the smoothness of
\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N}(t,\cdot)
, we then have

(5.185)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\partial_{x_{1}^{\ell}}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N})(t_{1},x_{1})\cdot\overline{\partial_{x_{2}^{\ell}}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N})}(t_{2},x_{2})\Big]\\
&\qquad\qquad=\mathbb{E}_{\mu\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\partial_{x_{1}^{\ell}}\partial_{x_{2}^{\ell}}\big\{\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N}(t_{1},x_{1})\cdot\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N}}(t_{2},x_{2})\big\}\Big]\\
&\qquad\qquad=\partial_{x_{1}^{\ell}}\partial_{x_{2}^{\ell}}\,\mathbb{E}_{\mu\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N}(t_{1},x_{1})\cdot\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N}}(t_{2},x_{2})\Big]\\
&\qquad\qquad=\partial_{x_{1}^{\ell}}\partial_{x_{2}^{\ell}}\mathfrak{F}_{N_{0}}[f_{N}](t_{1},x_{1},t_{2},x_{2}),\end{split}

where
\mathfrak{F}_{N_{0}}
and
f_{N}
are as in (
5.122
) and (
5.121
), respectively.

If
\beta^{2}\in[2\pi,3\pi)
, then by (
5.184
), (
5.185
), Lemma
5.21
(i), we have

(5.186)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\big|\big(\Box^{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\,\partial_{x^{\ell}}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N})\big)(t,x)\big|^{2}\Big]\lesssim N_{0}^{\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}+C\kappa_{\circ}}\cdot\mathfrak{I}^{-,b,s_{1},s_{2}}(t),

where
\mathfrak{I}^{-,b,s_{1},s_{2}}(t)
is as in (
5.140
) and with

\displaystyle b

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon,

\displaystyle s_{1}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}-\kappa_{\circ},

\displaystyle s_{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{3}{2}+\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}-\kappa_{\circ}.

Note that
(b,s_{1},s_{2})
satisfies the conditions in the statement of Lemma
5.22
. Therefore, by (
5.141
) in Lemma
5.22
, we have

(5.187)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\big|\big(\Box^{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\,\partial_{x^{\ell}}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N})\big)(t,x)\big|^{2}\Big]\lesssim N_{0}^{\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}+C\kappa_{\circ}}\langle t\rangle^{4\varepsilon},

as claimed in (
5.91
).

The case
\beta^{2}\in[3\pi,4\pi)
follows in a similar fashion by using Lemma
5.21
(ii) and (
5.142
) in Lemma
5.22
. This proves (
5.91
).

We now prove (
5.92
). By proceeding as in (
5.184
)-(
5.185
), we have

(5.188)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}_{\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\big|\big(\Box^{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\,\partial_{x^{\ell}}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}(\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N_{1}}-\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N_{2}})\big)(t,x)\big|^{2}\Big]\\
&\qquad=\int_{([0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})^{2}}dt_{1}dt_{2}dx_{1}dx_{2},\mathfrak{K}_{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}(t-t_{1},x-x_{1})\mathfrak{K}_{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}(t-t_{2},x-x_{2})\\
&\qquad\qquad\quad\times\partial_{x_{1}^{\ell}}\partial_{x_{2}^{\ell}}\,\mathbb{E}_{\mu\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}(\Theta_{N_{1}}^{\varepsilon_{0}}-\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N_{2}})(t_{1},x_{1})\cdot\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}(\Theta_{N_{1}}^{\varepsilon_{0}}-\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N_{2}})}(t_{2},x_{2})\Big].\end{split}

Next, by arguing as in (
5.115
)-(
5.117
), we have that

(5.189)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\Big|\partial_{x_{1}^{\ell}}\partial_{x_{2}^{\ell}}\,\mathbb{E}_{\mu\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}(\Theta_{N_{1}}^{\varepsilon_{0}}-\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N_{2}})(t_{1},x_{1})\cdot\overline{\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}(\Theta_{N_{1}}^{\varepsilon_{0}}-\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N_{2}})}(t_{2},x_{2})\Big]\Big|\\
&\leq\sum_{j=1}^{2}\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}}\big|\partial_{x_{1}^{\ell}}\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{1}-y_{1})\big|\big|\partial_{x_{2}^{\ell}}\mathcal{K}_{N_{0}}(x_{2}-y_{2})\big|dy_{1}dy_{2}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\Big|e^{\beta^{2}\Gamma_{N_{j}}(t_{1}-t_{2},y_{1}-y_{2})}-e^{\beta^{2}\Gamma_{N_{1},N_{2}}(t_{1}-t_{2},y_{1}-y_{2})}\Big|\\
&\lesssim N_{1}^{-\delta}N_{0}^{6}\int_{(\mathbb{T}^{2})^{2}}|y_{1}-y_{2}|^{-\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}-\delta}dy_{1}dy_{2}\\
&\lesssim N_{1}^{-\delta}N_{0}^{6},\end{split}

where
\delta=\delta(\beta)
is small enough so that
\frac{\beta^{2}}{2\pi}+\delta<2
. Therefore, from (
5.188
) and (
5.189
), we deduce

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P}}\Big[\big|\big(\Box^{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\,\partial_{x^{\ell}}(\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}(\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N_{1}}-\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N_{2}})\big)(t,x)\big|^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim N_{1}^{-\delta}N_{0}^{6}\big\|\mathfrak{K}_{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}(t-t^{\prime},y)\big\|_{L_{t^{\prime},y}^{1}([0,1]\times\mathbb{R}^{2})}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim N_{1}^{-\delta}N_{0}^{6}\,\langle t\rangle^{4\varepsilon},

proving (
5.92
) as claimed.
∎

Next, we present a proof of Proposition
5.16
.

Proof of Proposition
5.16
.

Fix
0<T\leq 1
. From the definition of restriction norms (
2.49
), our goal is to prove the following bounds:

(5.190)

\displaystyle\big\|\mathfrak{q}_{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{hi}}\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}(\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N})\big\|_{L^{2}(\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P})Y_{-\frac{1}{2}-3\varepsilon}^{-\alpha,-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 1,

(5.191)

\displaystyle\big\|\mathfrak{q}_{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{hi}}\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}(\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}(\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N_{1}}-\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N_{2}}))\big\|_{L^{2}(\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P})Y_{-\frac{1}{2}-3\varepsilon}^{-\alpha,-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\lesssim N^{-\delta}

for any integers
N_{2}\geq N_{1}\geq 1
and with some implicit constants are independent of
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
.

We first prove (
5.190
). From (
2.42
) and (
2.43
) and Plancherel’s identity in space (in order to move around spatial derivatives), we have

(5.192)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big\|\mathfrak{q}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{hi}}\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}(\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N})\big\|_{Y_{-\frac{1}{2}-3\varepsilon}^{-\alpha,-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}\\
&\qquad\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N_{0},R\,\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}\\
N_{0}\sim R\end{subarray}}N_{0}^{-\alpha-1}\cdot\big\|\mathbf{T}_{R}\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}\mathfrak{q}_{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{hi}}\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}(\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N})\big\|_{Y_{-\frac{1}{2}-3\varepsilon}^{1,-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}\\
&\qquad\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N_{0},R\,\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}\\
N_{0}\sim R\end{subarray}}N_{0}^{-\alpha-1}\cdot\big\|\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}-3\varepsilon}\big(||\partial_{t}|-|\nabla||^{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\mathfrak{q}_{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\mathbf{T}_{R}\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}|\nabla|(\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N})\big)\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}.\end{split}

Let
\lambda\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R})
be a bump function such that

\displaystyle\lambda(\tau)=\begin{cases}1\quad&\text{for $10^{-10}\leq|\tau|\leq 10^{10}$}\\
0\quad&\text{for $|\tau|<10^{-10}$ or $|\tau|>10^{10}$},\end{cases}

such that

\displaystyle\eta\Big(\frac{\tau}{R}\Big)\phi\Big(\frac{n}{N_{0}}\Big)\cdot\lambda\Big(\frac{|\tau|+|n|}{N_{0}}\Big)=\eta\Big(\frac{\tau}{R}\Big)\phi\Big(\frac{n}{N_{0}}\Big)

for any
(\tau,n)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}^{2}
and
N_{0}\sim R
. Here, the bump functions
\phi
and
\eta
are as in (
2.37
) and
\eta\big(\tau/R\big)
and
\phi\big(n/N_{0}\big)
are the symbols of the multipliers
\mathbf{T}_{R}
and
\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}
, respectively. Set
\widetilde{\lambda}(\tau)=|\tau|^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}\lambda(\tau)
and let
