T_{N_{0}}
be the Fourier multiplier on
\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}
given by

\widehat{T_{N_{0}}u}(\tau,n)=\widetilde{\lambda}\Big(\frac{|\tau|+|n|}{N_{0}}\Big)\cdot\widehat{u}(\tau,n),\quad(\tau,n)\in\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{Z}^{2}.

With these notations, we continue

(5.193)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big\|\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}-3\varepsilon}\big(||\partial_{t}|-|\nabla||^{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\mathfrak{q}_{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\mathbf{T}_{R}\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}|\nabla|(\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N})\big)\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\\
&\qquad\quad=N_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}\big\|\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}-3\varepsilon}\big(T_{N_{0}}\,\big||\partial_{t}|^{2}-|\nabla|^{2}\big|^{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\mathfrak{q}_{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\mathbf{T}_{R}\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}|\nabla|(\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N})\big)\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\\
&\qquad\quad=N_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}\big\|\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}-3\varepsilon}\big(T_{N_{0}}\mathbf{T}_{R}\,\Box^{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}|\nabla|(\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N})\big)\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\\
&\qquad\quad\lesssim N_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}\big\|\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}-3\varepsilon}\big(\Box^{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}|\nabla|\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}(\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N})\big)\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}.\end{split}

Here, we used Corollary
4.5
twice to the operators
T_{N_{0}}
and
\mathbf{T}_{R}
and the fact that Fourier multipliers
|\nabla|
and
\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}
commute in the last inequality.

Now, by taking the
L^{2}(\mu\otimes\mathbb{P})
-norm with Hölder’s inequality and Proposition
5.17
, we deduce that

(5.194)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big\|\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}-3\varepsilon}\big(\Box^{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}|\nabla|\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}(\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N})\big)\big\|_{L^{2}(\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P})L^{2}_{t,x}}\\
&\qquad=\big\|\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}-3\varepsilon}\big\|\Box^{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}|\nabla|\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}(\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N})\big\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}(\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P})}\big\|_{L^{2}_{t}}\\
&\qquad\lesssim N_{0}^{\frac{\beta^{2}}{4\pi}+\varepsilon}\cdot\|\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{t}}\\
&\qquad\lesssim N_{0}^{\frac{\beta^{2}}{4\pi}+\varepsilon}.\end{split}

Hence, from (
5.192
), (
5.193
), (
5.194
) and taking the
L^{2}(\mu\otimes\mathbb{P})
-norm, we have

\displaystyle\big\|\mathfrak{q}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{hi}}\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}(\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N})\big\|_{L^{2}(\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P})Y_{-\frac{1}{2}-3\varepsilon}^{-\alpha,-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}\lesssim\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}N_{0},R\,\in 2^{\mathbb{N}}\\
N_{0}\sim R\end{subarray}}N_{0}^{-\alpha-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\beta^{2}}{4\pi}+2\varepsilon}\lesssim 1

when
\alpha>\frac{\beta^{2}}{4\pi}-\frac{1}{2}+2\varepsilon
. This proves (
5.190
).

We now focus on (
5.191
). By proceeding as in (
5.194
) and interpolating (
5.91
) and (
5.92
), we get

(5.195)

\displaystyle\big\|\langle t\rangle^{-\frac{1}{2}-3\varepsilon}\big(\Box^{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}|\nabla|\mathbf{P}_{N_{0}}(\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}(\Theta_{N_{1}}-\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N_{2}}))\big)\big\|_{L^{2}(\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P})L^{2}_{t,x}}\lesssim N_{0}^{\frac{\beta^{2}}{4\pi}+2\varepsilon}N_{1}^{-\theta_{1}}

for some small constant
\theta>0
. Therefore the bound (
5.191
) follows from (
5.195
) and arguments similar to the proof of the estimate (
5.190
) above.
∎

6.
Well-posedness

In this section, we prove Theorem
1.1
.

6.1.
A deterministic global well-posedness result

Here, we prove well-posedness on the time interval
[0,1]
for the model equation:

(6.1)

\displaystyle v(t)=\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}(t)(u_{0},v_{0})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}\big(e^{iv}e^{i\Psi}\cdot\Theta\big)(t),\quad t\in\mathbb{R}

for
\Theta
and
\Psi
two distributions and where
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}
is as in (
2.55
) and (
2.56
).

Next, we prove that (
6.1
) is well-posed on
[0,1]
. Recall the definition of the space
\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha,\varepsilon}([0,1])
in (
5.90
).

Proposition 6.1
.

Let
0<\alpha<\frac{3\sqrt{241}-41}{244}
,
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(\alpha)>0
a small real number and
\delta=\alpha+10\varepsilon
. Then, the equation (
6.1
) is well-posed on
[0,1]
. More precisely, given an enhanced data set
(u_{0},v_{0},\Psi,\Theta)
belonging to

\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\delta,\varepsilon}([0,1]):=\mathcal{H}^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}(\mathbb{T}^{2})\times\big(\Lambda_{\infty}^{1-\varepsilon,0}\cap\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\infty}\big)([0,1])\times\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha,\varepsilon}([0,1]),

there exists a unique solution
v
to (
6.1
) in the class
X^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta,\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}([0,1])
. Furthermore, the solution map

(u_{0},v_{0},\Psi,\Theta)\in\mathcal{X}^{\alpha,\delta,\varepsilon}([0,1])\mapsto v\in X^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta,\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}([0,1])

is Lipschitz continuous.

Proof.

Define the map
\Gamma_{\Psi,\Theta}
via

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\Psi,\Theta}(v)(t)=\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}(t)(u_{0},v_{0})-\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}\big(e^{iv}e^{i\Psi}\Theta\big)(t).

We start by proving a difference estimate for
\Gamma_{\psi,\Theta}
. Let
0<T\leq 1
. From Lemmas
2.7
,
2.8
,
2.9
and
2.12
(ii), we have that

(6.2)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|\Gamma_{\Psi,\Theta}(v_{1})-\Gamma_{\Psi,\Theta}(v_{2})\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta,\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}_{T}}&\lesssim\|(e^{iv_{1}}-e^{iv_{2}})e^{i\Psi}\Theta\|_{X^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta,-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}_{T}}\\
&\lesssim T^{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}(t)(e^{iv_{1}}-e^{iv_{2}})e^{i\Psi}\Theta\|_{X^{-\frac{1}{2}+\delta,-\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}}\\
&\lesssim T^{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}w\in X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\\
\|w\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}-\delta,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}\leq 1\end{subarray}}A(w;v_{1},v_{2},\Psi,\Theta),\end{split}

where
A
is given by

\displaystyle A(w;v_{1},v_{2},\Psi,\Theta)=\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}}\tilde{u}w\cdot\widetilde{\Theta}\,dtdx,

with

\tilde{u}=\lambda(t)F(v_{1},v_{2})e^{i\widetilde{\Psi}},

where
F(v_{1},v_{2})=\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}(t)(e^{iv_{1}}-e^{iv_{2}})
,
\widetilde{\Psi}=\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}(t)\Psi
,
\lambda\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R};\mathbb{R})
such that
\lambda\equiv 1
on
[0,1]
and
\widetilde{\Theta}=\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}(t)\Theta
. Recall the definitions of the multiplier
\mathfrak{q}_{b}
in (
2.28
) and the space-time localizations in (
2.42
), (
2.42
), (
2.43
), (
2.43
) and (
2.43
). By Plancherel’s identity and duality, we have

\displaystyle A(w;v_{1},v_{2},\Psi,\Theta)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{lo}}\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}(\tilde{u}w)\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textup{lo}}\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}\big(\widetilde{\Theta}\big)+\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{hi}}\mathfrak{q}_{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}^{-1}\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}(\tilde{u}w)\cdot\mathbf{P}_{\textup{hi}}\mathfrak{q}_{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}\big(\widetilde{\Theta}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}}\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{lo,hi}}(\tilde{u}w)\cdot\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{lo,hi}}\big(\widetilde{\Theta}\big)+\int_{\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{T}^{2}}\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,lo}}(\tilde{u}w)\cdot\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,lo}}\big(\widetilde{\Theta}\big)

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\tilde{u}w\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}\|\mathbf{P}_{\textup{lo}}\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}(\tilde{\Theta})\|_{L^{\infty}_{t,x}}+\big\|\mathfrak{q}_{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}^{-1}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{hi}}\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}(\tilde{u}w)\big\|_{Y_{\frac{1}{2}+3\varepsilon}^{\alpha,\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}}\|\mathfrak{q}_{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}\big(\widetilde{\Theta}\big)\|_{Y_{-\frac{1}{2}-3\varepsilon}^{-\alpha,-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\|\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{lo,hi}}(\tilde{u}w)\|_{\Lambda^{\alpha,\frac{1}{2}-2\varepsilon}_{1}}\|\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{lo,hi}}\big(\widetilde{\Theta}\big)\|_{\Lambda^{-\alpha,-\frac{1}{2}+2\varepsilon}_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\|\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,lo}}(\tilde{u}w)\|_{\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\alpha,0}_{1+\varepsilon}}\|\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{lo,hi}}\big(\widetilde{\Theta}\big)\|_{\Lambda^{-\frac{1}{2}-\alpha,0}_{\frac{1+\varepsilon}{\varepsilon}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\Big(\|\tilde{u}w\|_{L^{1}_{t,x}}+\big\|\mathfrak{q}_{-\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}^{-1}\mathbf{P}_{\textup{hi}}\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,hi}}(\tilde{u}w)\big\|_{Y^{\alpha,\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}_{\frac{1}{2}+3\varepsilon}}+\|\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{lo,hi}}(\tilde{u}w)\|_{\Lambda^{\alpha,\frac{1}{2}-2\varepsilon}_{1}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad+\|\mathbf{Q}^{\textup{hi,lo}}(\tilde{u}w)\|_{\Lambda^{\alpha+\frac{1}{2},0}_{1+\varepsilon}}\Big)\cdot\|\Theta\|_{\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha,\varepsilon}([0,1])}.

Hence, by Propositions
4.8
,
4.13
and
4.14
, we get

(6.3)

\displaystyle\begin{split}A(w;v_{1},v_{2},\Psi,\Theta)&\lesssim\Big(\big\|F(v_{1},v_{2})e^{i\beta\widetilde{\Psi}}\big\|_{\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{1},0}_{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})}}}+\big\|F(v_{1},v_{2})e^{i\beta\widetilde{\Psi}}\big\|_{\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\frac{3}{2+\delta_{2}}}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\quad+\big\|F(v_{1},v_{2})e^{i\beta\widetilde{\Psi}}\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\Big)\|\Theta\|_{\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha,\varepsilon}([0,1])},\end{split}

where
\delta_{1}:=\alpha+5\varepsilon
and
\delta_{2}=\alpha+15\varepsilon
. From the product estimate (Lemma
4.1
), the fractional chain rule (Lemma
4.2
) and Hölder’s and Sobolev’s inequalities, we have

(6.4)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\big\|F(v_{1},v_{2})e^{i\beta\widetilde{\Psi}}\big\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})}}W_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{1},\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})}}}\\
&\qquad\lesssim\|F(v_{1},v_{2})\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})}}W_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{1},\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})-3\varepsilon}}}\big\|e^{i\beta\widetilde{\Psi}}\big\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})}}W_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{1},\frac{1}{\varepsilon}}}\\
&\qquad\lesssim\|F(v_{1},v_{2})\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})}}W_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta_{1}+\varepsilon,\frac{3}{2(1-\delta_{1})}}}\|\Psi\|_{\Lambda_{\infty}^{1-\varepsilon,0}([0,1])}\\
&\qquad\lesssim\|F(v_{1},v_{2})\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta)}}W_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta,\frac{3}{2(1-\delta)}}}\|\Psi\|_{\Lambda_{\infty}^{1-\varepsilon,0}([0,1])}.\end{split}

Similarly, we also have that

(6.5)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\big\|F(v_{1},v_{2})e^{i\beta\widetilde{\Psi}}\big\|_{\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\frac{3}{2+\delta_{2}}}}&\lesssim\big\|F(v_{1},v_{2})\big\|_{\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\frac{3}{2+\delta}}}\|\Psi\|_{\Lambda_{\infty}^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}([0,1])},\\
\big\|F(v_{1},v_{2})e^{i\beta\widetilde{\Psi}}\big\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}&\lesssim\|F(v_{1},v_{2})\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\|\Psi\|_{L^{\infty}([0,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{2})}.\end{split}

By the mean value theorem, we may write
F(v_{1},v_{2})
as

\displaystyle F(v_{1},v_{2})=\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}(t)(v_{1}-v_{2})\int_{0}^{1}e^{i(v_{1}+s(v_{2}-v_{1})}ds=:\mathbf{1}_{[0,1]}(t)(v_{1}-v_{2})G(v_{1},v_{2}).

Hence, by Lemma
4.1
, Lemma
4.2
, Hölder’s and Sobolev’s inequality in time and (
2.61
), we have that

(6.6)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|F(v_{1},v_{2})\|_{\Lambda^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta,0}_{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta)}}}&=\|(v_{1}-v_{2})G(v_{1},v_{2})\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta)}}([0,T])W_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta,\frac{3}{2(1-\delta)}}}\\
&\lesssim\|v_{1}-v_{2}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}([0,T])H_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}}\|G(v_{1},v_{2})\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta)}}([0,T])L_{x}^{\frac{6}{1-4\delta}}}\\
&\qquad+\|G(v_{1},v_{2})\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}([0,T])H_{x}^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}}\|v_{1}-v_{2}\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{3}{2(1-\delta)}}([0,T])L_{x}^{\frac{6}{1-4\delta}}}\\
&\lesssim\|v_{1}-v_{2}\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta,\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}_{T}}\big(1+\|v_{1}\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta,\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}_{T}}+\|v_{2}\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta,\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}_{T}}\big).\end{split}

Similarly, by Lemma
4.1
, Lemma
4.2
, Hölder’s inequality, (
2.61
) and Lemma
2.12
(ii), we have

(6.7)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\|F(v_{1},v_{2})\|_{\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\frac{3}{2+\delta}}}&=\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}(t)(v_{1}-v_{2})G(v_{1},v_{2})\|_{L_{x}^{\frac{3}{2+\delta}}W_{t}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon,\frac{3}{2+\delta}}}\\
&\lesssim\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}(t)(v_{1}-v_{2})\|_{L_{x}^{2}H_{t}^{\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}}\|G(v_{1},v_{2})\|_{L_{x}^{\frac{6}{1+2\delta}}L_{t}^{\frac{6}{1+2\delta}}([0,T])}\\
&\qquad+\|\mathbf{1}_{[0,T]}(t)G(v_{1},v_{2})\|_{L_{x}^{2}H_{t}^{\frac{1}{2}+\varepsilon}}\|v_{1}-v_{2}\|_{L_{x}^{\frac{6}{1+2\delta}}L_{t}^{\frac{6}{1+2\delta}}([0,T])}\\
&\lesssim\|v_{1}-v_{2}\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta,\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}_{T}}\big(1+\|v_{1}\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta,\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}_{T}}+\|v_{2}\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta,\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}_{T}}\big).\end{split}

We immediately have

(6.8)

\displaystyle\|F(v_{1},v_{2})\|_{L^{2}_{t,x}}\lesssim\|v_{1}-v_{2}\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta,\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}_{T}}.

Thus, by combining (
6.2
), (
6.3
), (
6.4
), (
6.5
), (
6.6
), (
6.7
), (
6.8
), we deduce that

(6.9)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|\Gamma_{\Psi,\Theta}(v_{1})-\Gamma_{\Psi,\Theta}(v_{2})\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta,\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}_{T}}\\
&\qquad\quad\lesssim T^{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\|v_{1}-v_{2}\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta,\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}_{T}}\big(1+\|v_{1}\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta,\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}_{T}}+\|v_{2}\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta,\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}_{T}}\big)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\|\Psi\|_{(\Lambda_{\infty}^{1-\varepsilon,0}\cap\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\infty})([0,1])}\|\Theta\|_{\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha,\varepsilon}([0,1])}.\end{split}

By arguing as in the proof of (
6.9
),

25

25

the proof is in fact easier as we do not have to use product estimates as in (

) and (

).
we get the following a priori estimate on
\Gamma_{\psi,\Theta}(v)
:

(6.10)

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\|\Gamma_{\Psi,\Theta}(v)\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta,\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}_{T}}\\
&\qquad\quad\lesssim\|(u_{0},v_{0})\|_{\mathcal{H}^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}}+T^{\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}\|v\|_{X^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta,\frac{1}{2}+\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}_{T}}\|\Psi\|_{(\Lambda_{\infty}^{1-\varepsilon,0}\cap\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\infty})([0,1])}\|\Theta\|_{\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha,\varepsilon}([0,1])}.\end{split}

Hence, from (

The claimed regularity of the map

is established via similar estimates.

By reiterating the local-in-time argument in above, noting that the local existence time (

6.2.
Proof of Theorem
1.1

In this subsection, we combine the results in the previous sections and prove our main theorem.

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

Let
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
with

0<\beta^{2}<2\pi\Big(1+\frac{3\sqrt{241}-41}{122}\Big).

Then, there exists
\alpha=\alpha(\beta)>0
and
\varepsilon=\varepsilon(\alpha)>0
such that

(6.12)

\displaystyle\frac{\beta^{2}}{4\pi}-\frac{1}{2}+100\varepsilon<\alpha<\frac{3\sqrt{241}-41}{244}.

Furthermore, we may choose
\varepsilon
small enough so that the estimates in Subsection
4.3
hold.

For each
N\in\mathbb{N}
and
\varepsilon_{0}\in\{+1,-1\}
, let
\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N}
be as in (
1.34
). Recall the definitions of the truncated stochastic convolutions in (
1.26
) and (
1.29
). Set
\Psi_{N}:=\Psi^{\textup{KG}}_{N}-\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{N}
. Then, by Lemma
5.4
and Proposition
5.13
with (
6.12
), there exists
(\Psi,\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}})\in\big(\Lambda_{\infty}^{1-\varepsilon,0}\cap\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\infty}\big)([0,1])\times\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha,\varepsilon}([0,1])
such that

\displaystyle(\Psi_{N},\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}_{N})\longrightarrow(\Psi,\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}})\quad\text{ in }\big(\Lambda_{\infty}^{1-\varepsilon,0}\cap\Lambda^{0,\frac{1}{2}-\varepsilon}_{\infty}\big)([0,1])\times\mathcal{Z}^{\alpha,\varepsilon}([0,1]),

\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P}
-almost surely as
N\to\infty
.

Therefore, by Proposition
6.1
, there exists
(v,\partial_{t}v)\in C([0,1];\mathcal{H}^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}(\mathbb{T}^{2})
,
\delta=\alpha+10\varepsilon
, solving the equation

\displaystyle v=-\sum_{\varepsilon_{0},\varepsilon_{1}\in\{+,-\}}c_{\varepsilon_{0},\varepsilon_{1}}\mathbf{\Pi}_{\leq N}\mathcal{I}\Big(e^{i\varepsilon_{1}\beta v}e^{i\beta(\Psi^{\textup{KG}}-\Psi^{\textup{wave}})}\cdot\Theta^{\varepsilon_{0}}\Big),

where the constants
c_{\varepsilon_{0},\varepsilon_{1}}
are as in (
1.33
), such that the solution
v_{N}
to (
1.32
) satisfies

\displaystyle(v_{N},\partial_{t}v_{N})\longrightarrow(v,\partial_{t}v)\quad\text{ in }C([0,1];\mathcal{H}^{\frac{1}{2}+\delta}(\mathbb{T}^{2}),

\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P}
-almost surely as
N\to\infty
. Let
u_{N}=\Psi^{\textup{KG}}+v_{N}
26
26

26

Here,
\Psi^{\textup{KG}}=\Psi^{\textup{wave}}+\Psi
, where
\Psi^{\textup{wave}}
is the distributional limit of the sequence
\{\Psi^{\textup{wave}}_{N}\}
provided by Lemma
5.3
.
be the solution to (
1.15
) and
u:=\Psi^{\textup{KG}}+v
. Then, we deduce from the above and Lemma
5.3
that
(u_{N},\partial_{t}u_{N})
converges to
(u,\partial_{t}u)
in
C([0,1];\mathcal{H}^{0-}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))

\vec{\mu}_{1}\otimes\mathbb{P}
-almost surely as
N\to\infty
.

From Lemma
5.1
, we get that
(u_{N},\partial_{t}u_{N})
converges to
(u,\partial_{t}u)
in
C([0,1];\mathcal{H}^{0-}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))

\vec{\rho}\otimes\mathbb{P}
-almost surely as
N\to\infty
. Moreover, in view of Lemma
5.2
, the law of
(u(t),\partial_{t}u(t))
is given by
\vec{\rho}
for each
t\in[0,1]
.

Since
\operatorname{Law}(u(1),\partial_{t}u(1))=\vec{\rho}
, we may extend reiterate the above argument and extend
(u,\partial_{t}u)
to the time interval
[1,2]
. Iterating this process gives a stochastic process
(u,\partial_{t}u)\in C(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathcal{H}^{0-}(\mathbb{T}^{2}))
such that

for each

. This concludes the proof.
∎
